# Mechanics

One entry per mechanic the engine or the card grammar supports: the rules, the AST nodes, the tests. Entries from different slices are separate sections; add yours as a new section.

## How the parser maps to the engine

The Oracle text grammar is in `packages/cards/src/convert/parser/` (the pipeline is in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, "Parser pipeline"; the design record is `docs/design/parser-architecture.md`, D-063 to D-069). A rule reads one phrase of an ability, not a whole ability: the segmenter finds the slots of a line, the phrase rules of each family read them into the clause IR, a binding pass numbers targets and resolves pronouns, and a typed lowering builds the engine AST and checks every feature it needs against `SUPPORTED_FEATURES` (D-007, D-068). A card is `supported` only when every ability lowers and `validateCard` is clean.

- **Where the rules are.** One file per rule under `packages/cards/src/convert/parser/rules/<family>/`. Each carries `examples` (real lines), `rejects` (near misses) and `cites` (the CR rules), and `packages/cards/test/parser-registry.test.ts` runs them and forbids ambiguity; the stable rule ids (`closestRule`, `recognised`) are the ones the files declare. The per-rule tables that used to be here were removed in the docs cleanup because the rule files are the source of truth.
- **How a stage is added** (class T and P of `docs/CARD_SUPPORT_PLAYBOOK.md`): take the heaviest unread shapes of `reports/template-replay.md` (`pnpm corpus:replay`) and `reports/backlog.md`, add the engine primitive if it is missing (entries below, each with "What the parser must produce"), then the phrase rules, the lowering, the grammar tests (`convert-grammar-*.test.ts`) and the golden corpus. Bump `PARSER_VERSION` (`packages/cards/src/version.ts` keeps one comment per version) so cached support reports are recomputed.
- **Golden corpus.** `packages/cards/test/fixtures/corpus/golden.jsonl` holds real Oracle text the owner extracts (`pnpm corpus:extract`, `docs/CORPUS.md`); `golden.expected.json` is the snapshot gate (supported or not, and the AST of the supported ones) and `golden.test.ts` compares it. After a deliberate change, review the diff of `UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 pnpm exec vitest run packages/cards/test/golden.test.ts`. A parser change must not silently drop a supported card or change its AST.
- **What the grammar does not read** is listed per theme in `docs/ROADMAP.md`; a line the grammar cannot read leaves the card unsupported and the card's "Why unsupported?" names the text, never a half-run ability.

### Parser stages and where their engine primitives are

Each stage added the wordings of one engine slice or one sweep of the replay report. The engine entries further down (same decision numbers) say what the parser must produce for each line; the rules themselves are in the rule files.

- **P2**: activated abilities, durations, library search, discard, Cycling, a choice of types
- **P3**: Auras, Equipment and static abilities with subjects
- **P4**: Kicker, Flashback, "if ~ was kicked", the intervening "if", host-typed attached subjects, Food and Clue
- **P5**: keyword gains, Turn to Frog, flicker, Prowess, the pronoun "it", dash-form periods
- **P6**: Ward, Crew, token copies, additive types, a stated control on return
- **P7a**: damage prevention shields
- **P7b**: multi-face layouts and `Transform ~`
- **P8**: Saga chapter abilities
- **P9**: class T gaps of the template replay
- **P10**: scry, surveil, look and reorder, put from the hand on top
- **P11**: the references of D-092 and D-096, and fear, intimidate and shadow
- **P12**: restrictions, "can't be countered" and landwalk (D-094, D-098)
- **P13**: additional costs, cost reductions, the free cast, discard costs and counted amounts (D-095, D-101)
- **P14**: the mana slice: "could produce", filter lands, "Activate only if", life for mana and granted mana abilities (D-099, D-102)
- **P15**: a second class-T sweep: subtype words, "another", tokens with keywords, group effects and the choices of D-100
- **P16**: X (D-103, D-107): the parser reads what the engine announces
- **P17**: keyword batch 1 (D-104, D-110): the parser reads the keywords the engine runs
- **P18**: the trigger slice (D-108, D-111): the parser reads the steps, draws, combat damage, leaving, the attached permanent, comparisons and "unless"
- **P19**: modal spells and abilities (D-105, D-114): the parser reads "Choose one —" and its bullets
- **P20**: counted power and toughness and X in a pump (D-109, D-115): the parser reads "gets +1/+1 for each ...", "power and toughness are each equal to the number of ..." and "-X/-X"
- **P21**: keyword batch 2 (D-116, D-120): the parser reads the keywords, regeneration, "blocks" and "becomes blocked" and "you may pay [cost]. If you do, ..."
- **P22**: the named alternative costs (D-117, D-121): the parser reads "Overload {M}", "Evoke {M}" and "Dash {M}"
- **P23**: the second slice of named alternative costs (D-117, D-124, D-123): the parser reads "Spectacle {M}", "Escape—{M}, Exile N other cards from your graveyard.", "Unearth {M}" and "sacrifice it unless it escaped"
- **P24**: the play rules (D-125): the parser reads "You may play an additional land on each of your turns.", "You may look at the top card of your library any time.", the attack tax and "You may choose not to untap ~ during your untap step."
- **P25**: the upkeep slice (D-127): the parser reads "Echo {M}", "Cumulative upkeep {M}" and "When ~ enters, you become the monarch."
- **P26**: the cast-later slice (D-128): the parser reads "Suspend N—{M}", "Madness {M}", "Foretell {M}" and "You draw a card at the beginning of the next turn's upkeep."
- **P27**: the copy slice (D-129): the parser reads "Storm", "Replicate {M}" and the copies of a spell
- **P28**: the counted-amounts slice (D-131): the parser reads the amounts that come from the game, "where X is ...", tokens that enter tapped, the player effects, the bite and the conditions about players
- **P29**: the subjects slice (D-133, D-134): the parser reads groups of creatures by type, lords, conditional statics, filtered trigger subjects, "that player", the new costs, a death replacement and a cast from the graveyard
- **P30**: the recursion slice (D-135, D-136): the parser reads "if it's a Zombie card", "you may ... If you do", "When you do", Gift, "enters untapped", "dies or is put into exile", "third from the top", the permission to cast from the graveyard, the ability that works from the graveyard and life paid by an amount
- **P31**: the Artifacts slice (D-139, D-140): the parser reads mass effects and colors, a union of card types, costs that put counters or return permanents, cost reductions for each, once each turn, state, mana and batch triggers, statics for a group, flash for spells, a linked exile, the monarch, a look at the top cards, tapping X of a type and reselecting an attack
- **P32**: the Dinosaurs slice (D-145, D-147): the parser reads tests of the game around an instruction, a restriction lifted by a condition, an Aura that remakes a creature, the commanders' mana value, doubling, fights, the excess damage of trample, reveal until, free casting, divided damage and mana "instead"
- **P33**: the Angels slice (D-149, D-150): the parser reads life gained, counters put and attack batches as triggers, protections and the block tax as statics, restricted and chosen-color mana, exile per opponent and "instead" after a "may"
- **P34**: the Dragons slice (D-151, D-152): the parser reads exiled cards played for a while, Discover and exile-until casts, an additional combat, paying any amount, mana for Dragons, casting from the library, abilities from the graveyard, named options and an enters-as-copy replacement
- **P35**: the Korvold deck's text and token slice (D-175, D-176): the parser reads tokens with abilities in quotes (a quote inside a quote too), "It has \"...\"", "a number of tapped Treasure tokens equal to its power", "a Food token or a Treasure token", "enchanted creature gains ...", "That creature also gains ..." on the next line, a card chosen from the graveyard, "shuffle your graveyard into your library", "return it to its owner's hand", the controller of a permanent as the recipient of damage, "Equip—{2}, Pay 2 life." and "Choose a Background"
- **P36**: the Korvold deck's fetch lands, reflexive sacrifice and Treasure grants slice (D-177, D-178): the parser reads "a basic Mountain, Forest, or Plains card", "When you do" after a sacrifice that is not an offer, "that creature's power" for the permanent that was sacrificed, "untap that land" for the card a search found, "If <test>, instead ..." on the line after the one it replaces, "there are seven or more cards in your graveyard", "becomes the target of a spell" and abilities in quotes given to a group (an activated ability, a mana ability that sacrifices its permanent)
- **P37**: the Korvold deck's triggers and small statics slice (D-179, D-180): the parser reads "When you cycle this card", "that spell's mana value", "target player or planeswalker" (as a player), "each creature that player controls", "that was put there from the battlefield this turn", "the exiled card's power", "If you put a Cave onto the battlefield this way", "Lands you control enter untapped", "until your next turn, creatures your opponents control attack each combat if able ...", "for each color among permanents you control" and "Your opponents can't cast spells during your turn"
- **P38**: the Korvold deck's casting and targets slice (D-181, D-182): the parser reads a target that only a kicked spell has (and the target it replaces, "instead"), "As an additional cost to cast ~, sacrifice an artifact or creature or pay {4}" (a choice of two ways), "~ costs {3} less to cast if you control a creature with power 4 or greater" and "Change the target of target spell or ability with a single target"
- **P40**: the Korvold deck's keywords slice (D-185, D-186): the parser reads "Encore {M}" (a rule of its own, `keyword.encore`), "Devour N" and "Riot" (keywords of `keyword.line`, Riot also as the grant of "Nontoken creatures you control have riot"), a token "with flying and devour 2" and "Creature spells you control can't be countered." (`static.spells-cant-be-countered`)
- **P41**: the Korvold deck's expensive singles slice (D-187, D-188): the parser reads "Whenever you attack" (`trigger.you-attack`), "sacrifice one or more Treasures" (`noun.one-or-more-permanents`), "up to that many target creatures" (a branch of the target noun) with "gain ... until end of turn" (`effect.gain-keyword-targets`), "Each creature you control gets +1/+0 for each time it has attacked this turn" (`static.pt-times-attacked`), "if it's your main phase" (`condition.your-main-phase`), "there's an additional combat phase after this phase" (`effect.extra-combat-after`) with the delayed sentence "At the beginning of that combat, untap all creatures you control" folded into it, and "Exile the top two cards of target opponent's library face down" with "You may look at and play those cards for as long as they remain exiled" (`effect.exile-top-face-down`, `effect.may-look-and-play`)
- **P39**: the Korvold deck's mana-spent slice (D-183, D-184): the parser reads "When you cast this spell" (the cast trigger of the spell itself), a token "named Kobolds of Kher Keep", "the amount of mana spent to cast it" and "for each mana from an artifact source spent to cast it"

## Triggered abilities (slice S2: engine)

- CR: 603.2, 603.2b, 603.2c, 603.2e, 603.3, 603.3a, 603.3b, 603.5, 603.6a, 603.6c, 603.10a, 113.7a, 117.5, 101.4 (APNAP)
- Gap class: S (subsystem; design `docs/design/triggered-abilities.md`, D-059 to D-061)
- Code: `packages/engine/src/rules/triggers.ts` (detection), `rules/trigger-stack.ts` (placing on the stack, ordering), `rules/settle.ts` and `rules/priority.ts` (`grantPriority(state, seat, prior)`), `rules/stack.ts` and `rules/effects.ts` (resolution with `ResolutionContext.source`, `confirmOptional`), `state/types.ts` (`PendingTrigger`, `StackData.ability`), decisions `orderTriggers` and `confirmOptional`, events `abilityTriggered` and `optionalAnswered`
- AST nodes: `Ability` `triggered` (`trigger`, `effect`, `optional`; `targets` unsupported). `Trigger` kinds: `entersBattlefield`, `dies`, `attacks`, `becomesTapped` (each with `this` or an `ObjectFilter`), `castSpell` (`filter`, `by`), `landfall`, `upkeep` and `endStep` (`whose: 'you' | 'each'`), `permanentSacrificed` (`by`, optional `filter`). "A or B" is one ability per condition.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-triggers.test.ts` (enters, optional, landfall, source leaves, simultaneous deaths, upkeep and end step, attack, cast, tap, a trigger before a decision), `scenario-xx-trigger-filters.test.ts`, `scenario-xx-trigger-decisions.test.ts` (legal actions, rejected answers, determinism), `scenario-xx-sacrifice-engine.test.ts` (SPEC test 18), `ast-triggers-validate.test.ts`; AI: `random-agent.test.ts`, `casual-permanents.test.ts`, the 100-game and property tests with the `triggers` deck style
- Example cards: Korvold, Fae-Cursed King (when the grammar reads it), Mulldrifter-style "enters, draw", Mentor of the Meek-style watchers, Rhystic Study-style cast triggers (read by the parser since stage P2)
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; the parser slice turns the count into a number
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2, 2026-10-21
- Notes: A triggered ability on the stack is an object with no card carrying its own AST, so its source leaving does not stop it (CR 113.7a). Triggers of one player that are not identical (not the same ability of the same source) are ordered by that player; the first of the chosen order goes on the stack first and resolves last. Events are scanned per batch (an action's, or a resolution's up to a decision); every code path that gives priority must pass its events to `grantPriority` (D-059). Look-back triggers (`dies`, `permanentSacrificed`) see the battlefield before a run of simultaneous departures. Not built: targets and modes on triggers, delayed and reflexive triggers, triggers of objects outside the battlefield (`docs/ROADMAP.md`).

## Sacrifice (slice S2)

- CR: 701.21, 701.21a, 608.2d, 603.10a
- Gap class: P (primitive)
- Code: `rules/sacrifice.ts` (`sacrificePermanent`, `sacrificeCandidates`), `rules/effects.ts` (`sacrifice` and `chooseSacrifice` instructions), decision `choosePermanent`, action `choosePermanents`, events `sacrificed` and `permanentsChosen`
- AST nodes: `Effect` `sacrifice` with `objects: SacrificeSelector` (`this`, or `{ kind: 'choose', filter, count? }`); `Trigger` `permanentSacrificed`; `Cost` `sacrifice` on mana abilities
- Tests: `scenario-xx-sacrifice.test.ts` (choices, illegal answers, automatic and empty cases, N of a kind, event order, look back), `scenario-xx-sacrifice-engine.test.ts`, `scenario-xx-trigger-decisions.test.ts`
- Example cards: Korvold, Fae-Cursed King; any "sacrifice a creature" edict on yourself
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2, 2026-10-21
- Notes: Only the controller's own permanents can be sacrificed; indestructible does not matter; a commander goes to the graveyard and the owner may then move it (CR 903.9a). With no more candidates than the count the engine chooses and emits `permanentsChosen` with `automatic: true`; with none nothing happens and nothing is emitted. "Each player sacrifices" and sacrifice as a spell or activated-ability cost are not built.

## Tokens (slice S2)

- CR: 111.1, 111.2, 111.7, 704.5d, 603.6a, 700.4
- Gap class: P (primitive)
- Code: `rules/tokens.ts` (`createTokens`), `rules/effects.ts` (`createToken`), `cards/tokens.ts` (`TOKEN_PRESETS`), `rules/sba.ts` (a token off the battlefield ceases to exist), event `tokenCreated`
- AST nodes: `Effect` `createToken` (`token: TokenSpec`, `count: Amount`); `TokenSpec` is `{ kind: 'preset', preset }` or `{ kind: 'custom', definition: TokenDefinition }`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.tokenPresets` and `tokenTypes`
- Tests: `scenario-xx-tokens.test.ts`, `scenario-xx-trigger-filters.test.ts` (a token entering triggers "another creature enters"), `ast-triggers-validate.test.ts`
- Example cards: any "create a 1/1 Soldier creature token", "create a Treasure token" card
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The token has its own object id, no card, and `GameObject.token` holds its characteristics and abilities; "enters tapped" and "enters with counters" replacement abilities of the token apply as it enters. A token that dies is moved to the graveyard first (so "dies" triggers see it) and ceases to exist at the next state-based action check. Not built: legendary, copy, double-faced, planeswalker and battle tokens, tokens entering attacking, replacement of the number created.

## Treasure and mana abilities with a sacrifice cost (slice S2)

- CR: 111.10a, 605.1a, 605.3b, 701.21, 106, SPEC 10 "Mana payment"
- Gap class: P (primitive)
- Code: `rules/mana-engine.ts` (`canActivateManaAbility`, `payManaAbilityCosts`, `paymentSources` with the `sacrifice` tier, `activateManaAbility`, `applyPaymentPlan`), `cards/tokens.ts` (`TOKEN_PRESETS.Treasure`), `state/actions.ts` (`payManual` taps take an optional `color`)
- AST nodes: `Ability` `mana` with `cost: [{ kind: 'tap' }, { kind: 'sacrifice', what: { kind: 'this' } }]`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.manaAbilityCostKinds` is `tap` and `sacrifice` (only of this permanent)
- Tests: `scenario-xx-tokens.test.ts` (Treasure section), `mana-payment.test.ts` (the sacrifice tier), `ast-triggers-validate.test.ts`
- Example cards: the Treasure token; Gold-like tokens would need no `tap` cost, which the engine already allows
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The costs are paid first (tapped, sacrificed, `sacrificed` emitted), then the mana is added. Automatic payment prefers lands, then creatures, then artifacts, and sacrifices a Treasure last. A source with a choice of color needs the color on a manual tap (`payManual` taps, or the `activateAbility` option). Food and Clue are defined in `cards/tokens.ts` and run since D-075 (see "Food and Clue" below), so cards creating them are supported when nothing else blocks them.


## Flash (engine)

- CR: 702.8a, 702.8b, 117.1a (sorcery timing), 304.5 (instants)
- Gap class: K (keyword)
- Code: `timingAllows` in `packages/engine/src/rules/legality.ts` (flash is read with `hasKeyword`, so a granted flash counts once layers exist); the windows come from `priorityOptions` in `rules/priority.ts`, so `getLegalActions` and `applyAction` agree
- AST nodes: `Ability` `keyword` with `{ kind: 'flash' }`; in `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.keywordKinds`
- Tests: `scenario-xx-flash.test.ts` (opponent's turn in every step, own turn outside the main phases, in response to a spell, with the own spell on the stack, a flash artifact, a flash commander from the command zone, still needs mana), `ast-supported.test.ts`; AI: `keyword-targeting.test.ts` (100-game soaks with the `keywords` deck style; flash casts outside sorcery timing occur and no other non-instant is cast there)
- Example cards: any creature or artifact with the keyword line "Flash" (the parser already reads it, so such a card is supported when its other text is)
- Cards unlocked: flash-only permanents; the count comes from the owner's coverage run
- Added in: M5, 2026-10-21
- Notes: A flash permanent spell is cast at any time its owner has priority, so the web auto-pass policy (`apps/web/src/policy/should-stop.ts`, `hasRelevantAction`) should also treat a castable card with the `flash` keyword as a reason to stop; the view already lists `keywords` on the hand and command zone objects. Not built: flashback and "as though it had flash" effects.

## Hexproof (engine)

- CR: 702.11a, 702.11b, 115.4, 601.2c, 608.2b, 113.6
- Gap class: K (keyword)
- Code: `canBeTargetedBy` in `packages/engine/src/rules/select.ts`, called from `legalTargets` for object targets and "any target"; `stillLegalTargets` (used by `resolveTop` in `rules/stack.ts`) recomputes `legalTargets`, so the same check runs again on resolution
- AST nodes: `Ability` `keyword` with `{ kind: 'hexproof' }`; in `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.keywordKinds`
- Tests: `scenario-xx-hexproof-shroud.test.ts` (an opponent's Bolt does not offer it, naming it is refused, its controller may target it, removal with only a hexproof target is not castable, a hexproof spell on the stack is targetable, a sweeper still kills it, a target that gains hexproof in response fizzles the spell, the controller's own spell still resolves on it, a two-target spell resolves for the legal target); AI: `keyword-targeting.test.ts`
- Example cards: any creature with the keyword line "Hexproof"; supported when the rest of its text is
- Cards unlocked: hexproof creatures; the count comes from the owner's coverage run
- Added in: M5, 2026-10-21
- Notes: Only permanents on the battlefield are protected (CR 702.11b); a creature spell on the stack can be targeted. "Opponents control" is judged from the controller of the targeting spell or ability. Not built: hexproof on a player (CR 702.11c) and "hexproof from [quality]" (CR 702.11d), see `docs/ROADMAP.md`.

## Shroud (engine)

- CR: 702.18a, 702.18b, 115.4, 601.2c, 608.2b
- Gap class: K (keyword)
- Code: the same `canBeTargetedBy` as hexproof, without the controller exception
- AST nodes: `Ability` `keyword` with `{ kind: 'shroud' }`; in `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.keywordKinds`
- Tests: `scenario-xx-hexproof-shroud.test.ts` (neither the opponent nor the controller is offered it, a spell with only a shroud target is not castable, a target that gains shroud in response is illegal even for its controller on resolution); AI: `keyword-targeting.test.ts`
- Example cards: none named; real cards with printed shroud are few, and the keyword is mostly granted by other cards (which needs the layers)
- Cards unlocked: shroud creatures and artifacts; few
- Added in: M5, 2026-10-21
- Notes: Shroud also stops the controller's own targeting, so a permanent that gains it cannot be the target of its controller's pump spells. Not built: shroud on a player.

## Mana and lands (engine slice, first corpus-driven work)

Six primitives for the most popular unsupported cards of the corpus (the first corpus run, whose note was removed in the docs cleanup): dual lands, pain lands, signets and talismans, "enters tapped unless" lands, shock lands and "no maximum hand size". The engine side only: the grammar turns the card counts into numbers once it reads these shapes. All tests use SYNTHETIC cards (`packages/engine/src/cards/fixtures/synthetic-mana.ts`, or defined in the test); run them with `pnpm exec vitest run packages/engine/test/scenario-xx-mana packages/engine/test/scenario-xx-enters packages/engine/test/scenario-xx-no-max packages/engine/test/mana-payment-costed packages/ai/test/mana-lands`.

### Choice of two or more colors ("{T}: Add {U} or {B}.")

- CR: 106.1b (the types of mana), 111.10a (a Treasure also chooses), 605.1a, 605.3a, 605.3b, 601.2g
- Gap class: P (primitive)
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`ManaProduction`), `rules/mana-engine.ts` (`productionOutcomes`, `activateManaAbility`, `payableSources`), `state/decisions.ts` (`PayManaDecision.manaSources[].colors`)
- AST nodes: `ManaProduction` `{ kind: 'oneOf', colors }`, at least two distinct colors (validation rejects fewer, repeats and unknown colors); `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.manaProductionKinds`
- Tests: `scenario-xx-mana-oneof.test.ts`
- Example cards: dual lands and tri-lands (when the grammar reads them)
- Cards unlocked: about 305 cards carry `{T}: Add {M} or {M}.` (the first corpus run)
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2 (no state shape change), 2026-10-21
- Notes: The payment planner treats it like any color choice restricted to the list; a manual tap must name a color of the list, and the refusal names the colors the source can make. `PayManaDecision.manaSources` entries carry `colors` for `oneOf`, `anyColor` (W U B R G) and `commanderIdentity` (the identity, in WUBRG order), so a client no longer reads the rules text to know when to ask for a color; a one-entry list needs no question. A spell or ability effect (`addMana`) that adds a choice of mana is rejected by `validateCard` (nothing could ask which color while it resolves); only a mana ability chooses (CR 605.3b).

### Mana abilities with an extra effect ("~ deals 1 damage to you.")

- CR: 605.1a, 605.3b, 120.3a, 119.3
- Gap class: P
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Ability` `mana`, `also`), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkManaAbilityAlso`), `rules/effects.ts` (`runImmediateEffects`), `rules/mana-engine.ts`, `mana/payment.ts` (`PaymentSource.extraEffect`)
- AST nodes: `Ability` `mana` gains `also?: Effect[]`: only `dealDamage` to `you`, `loseLife` you and `gainLife` you with a positive whole amount (anything with a target or a decision is rejected); `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.manaAbilityAlsoKinds`
- Tests: `scenario-xx-mana-also.test.ts`
- Example cards: pain lands, Ancient Tomb (when the grammar reads them)
- Cards unlocked: about 26 pain lands in the corpus
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The effects run right after the mana is added, in the same activation, and emit the usual events (`damageDealt` from the permanent, `lifeChanged`), which go through `grantPriority` like any other batch, so triggers see them. Auto-pay prefers sources without an extra effect (a plain land before a pain land, below the sacrifice tier and above the tiers of lands, creatures and artifacts) and never takes its own player to 0 life: `planFor` plans again without the sources whose damage would end the game, so a spell that only a pain land could pay for is then not offered (paying by hand is still allowed).

### Mana abilities with a mana cost ("{1}, {T}: Add {U}{B}.")

- CR: 605.1a, 605.3a, 118.3, 106.6
- Gap class: P
- Code: `mana/payment.ts` (the one-level rule, in the file header), `mana/costed.ts` (which costed sources a search activates), `mana/payment-shared.ts`, `rules/mana-engine.ts` (`canActivateManaAbility`, `activateManaAbility`, `paymentSources`)
- AST nodes: `manaAbilityCostKinds` gains `'mana'`; only generic and single-color symbols (no X, hybrid, Phyrexian or snow) are supported on a mana ability
- Tests: `scenario-xx-mana-cost.test.ts`, `mana-payment-costed.test.ts` (the planner against an independent brute force over 600 seeded instances)
- Example cards: the Signets ("{1}, {T}: Add ..."; a rock with no cost in its mana ability is not one)
- Cards unlocked: about 31 in the corpus (signets and the other "{M}, {T}: Add" shapes; the first corpus run counts them as one shape family)
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2, 2026-10-21
- Notes: By hand the cost comes out of the mana pool (CR 605.3a: tap other sources first); a Signet is offered at priority only once the pool can pay, and a manual tap that cannot pay is refused with the cost named, before anything changes. The planner supports exactly ONE level: a costed ability is used by auto-pay only when its cost is paid from the pool or from sources whose own abilities have no mana cost, and the ability never pays for itself or for another costed ability. It tries each subset of the costed sources (the lowest six object ids) as "activated" (their costs become extra demand that only free mana can pay, a chosen source that ends up unused invalidates the subset) and keeps the best plan under the usual order, so lands are tapped before a Signet and a Signet only when it is needed. A chain (a Signet paid by a Signet) is not planned by auto-pay; the player can still do it by hand. Spell-only restricted pool mana cannot pay an ability cost (CR 106.6).

### Conditional "enters tapped" ("~ enters tapped unless you control two or more basic lands.")

- CR: 614.1c, 614.12, 205.4a (basic), 109.4
- Gap class: P
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Condition`, `Replacement` `entersTapped.unless`), `rules/conditions.ts` (`evaluateCondition`), `rules/enters.ts` (`entersTapped`), `rules/zones.ts`, `rules/tokens.ts`
- AST nodes: `Condition` `controlsAtLeast { filter, count }`, `opponentsAtLeast { count }`, `and`/`or { terms }`, `not { term }`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.conditionKinds`
- Tests: `scenario-xx-enters-tapped-unless.test.ts`
- Example cards: check lands ("basic lands", "other lands"), fast lands ("unless you control two or fewer other lands" is `not` of "three or more"), "unless you have two or more opponents" lands (always tapped in a two-player game)
- Cards unlocked: about 30 in the corpus
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The condition is read as the permanent enters, with it not yet on the battlefield (CR 614.12), by the player it enters under; the filter always counts that player's own battlefield permanents (`controller` must be left out or `you`). Tokens read it too. "Unless you control a Plains or an Island" needs `anyOf` inside the filter (`ObjectFilter.anyOf` exists; the subtypes of a disjunction are written as members).

### Pay life or enter tapped ("As ~ enters, you may pay 2 life. If you don't, it enters tapped.")

- CR: 614.1c, 614.12, 119.4, 305.1, 608.3
- Gap class: S-adjacent (a decision inside the zone-change pipeline; no new decision kind)
- Code: `rules/zones.ts` (`MoveRequest.entersPaid`, the question before the commit), `rules/enters.ts`, `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionEntersPaid`, `ResolutionContext.entersPaid`), `api.ts` (`answerConfirm`, `playLand`), `state/decisions.ts` (`ConfirmOptionalDecision.cost`)
- AST nodes: `Replacement` `{ kind: 'entersTappedUnlessPays', cost: Cost[] }` (validation: only `payLife` costs of at least 1); not supported on tokens
- Tests: `scenario-xx-enters-pay.test.ts`; AI: `packages/ai/test/mana-lands.test.ts` (the shock questions of 60 whole games)
- Example cards: the shock lands
- Cards unlocked: about 26 shock lands in the corpus
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2 (additive optional fields only), 2026-10-21
- Notes: The question is the existing `confirmOptional` decision with `cost` set; `source` is the object about to enter (the land in the hand, or the spell on the stack, or the card in the graveyard an effect returns). Nothing is on the battlefield while it is asked, so landfall and the like see the land only after the answer. The pipeline (`moveObject`) answers `needsDecision`, like the commander replacement; the three callers resume it: `playLand` (the land waits in the hand, the answer plays it again with `entersPaid` set), a resolving permanent spell (`finish` keeps the resolution with an empty queue, the answer finishes the move) and the `move` instruction of an effect (`ResolutionContext.entersPaid`, cleared once that move is done). Not asked when the permanent enters tapped anyway (another "enters tapped" applies, or the effect says "tapped") or when the player has less life than the cost (CR 119.4); it then enters tapped. Accepting pays the life and emits `lifeChanged`, then the zone change, then (for a land) `landPlayed`.

### No maximum hand size ("You have no maximum hand size.")

- CR: 402.2, 514.1, 604.1
- Gap class: P (the first static ability; layers stay a later slice)
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Ability` `static`, `StaticEffect`), `rules/statics.ts` (`hasNoMaximumHandSize`), `rules/flow.ts` (`runCleanup`)
- AST nodes: `Ability` `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'noMaximumHandSize' } }`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.staticKinds`
- Tests: `scenario-xx-no-max-hand-size.test.ts`
- Example cards: Reliquary Tower, Thought Vessel (when the grammar reads them)
- Cards unlocked: about 23 in the corpus
- Added in: ENGINE_VERSION 2, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The cleanup step skips the discard when the active player controls a battlefield permanent with the ability (any number of them, and it wins over a lowered `handSizeMax`); an opponent's permanent or one outside the battlefield does nothing. Nothing else static exists: `derive` still applies only base characteristics and counters.

## Library search (slice S5: engine)

- CR: 701.23 (Search), 701.24 (Shuffle), 701.20 (Reveal), 401.2 (Library), 614.12 and 603.6a (what enters), 608.2d (choices as an effect resolves)
- Gap class: S-adjacent (a new decision kind, `chooseCards`; the shapes were decided in the scaling plan)
- Code: `rules/search.ts` (`runSearch`, `searchCandidates`), `rules/search-prompt.ts` (`describeSearch`), `rules/effects.ts` (the `search` case), `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionCards`), `api.ts` (the `chooseCards` branch of `dispatch`), `legal-actions.ts`, `state/decisions.ts` (`ChooseCardsDecision`), `state/events.ts` (`librarySearched`, `cardsRevealed`), `view.ts` (`pendingCards`, `waitingOn`)
- AST nodes: `Effect` `{ kind: 'search', player, filter, max, destinations: SearchDestination[], reveal, shuffle }`; `SearchDestination` `{ zone: 'battlefield' | 'hand' | 'graveyard', tapped?, count: number | 'rest' }`. `player` must be `you`; `filter` sets neither `zone` nor `controller`; the destination counts add up to `max`, or end in `'rest'` with fewer fixed cards than `max`. `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.searchDestinationKinds`, `FeatureRef` dimension `searchDestination:<zone>` (superset: `library` and `exile` are known but not run)
- Tests: `scenario-xx-search.test.ts`, `ast-search-validate.test.ts`, `ast-features.test.ts`; AI: `packages/ai/test/search-handlers.test.ts`, `search-games.test.ts` (100 games, every path), `hidden-info.test.ts` (search decks)
- Example cards: Rampant Growth, Cultivate, Kodama's Reach, Farseek, Nature's Lore, Three Visits; fetch lands (`{T}, Sacrifice ~:` and a search). The parser reads these since stage P2 (`effect.search`, `noun.library-card`, `cost.sacrifice`).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; the parser slice turns the count into a number
- Added in: no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump of its own (new decision, instruction, events and view fields are additive; old states load), 2026-10-21
- Notes: The search is two instructions in the resolution queue. `search` asks the controller (`chooseCards`, `purpose: 'search'`, `zone: 'library'`, `min` 0 when the filter states a quality (CR 701.23b) and the whole count when it does not, so a search for "a card" must find one (CR 701.23d), `max` capped by the matching cards), or does not ask at all when nothing matches (the player still searches, so `librarySearched { found: 0 }` and the shuffle happen). The answer is the cards in the order the player gives them: the first `count` go to the first destination, the next to the second, and `'rest'` takes the remainder; the decision's `prompt` says so in words ("Choose up to two basic land cards; the first goes onto the battlefield tapped, the second into your hand."). Then, in this order: `librarySearched`; `cardsRevealed` (`cards`, `names`, `from`) when `reveal` and at least one card was found; one `move` instruction per card (so cards entering the battlefield get their replacement effects, the shock land's question included, and trigger by their events, landfall too, CR 603.6a); a `shuffle` effect when `shuffle` (CR 701.24b: the found cards have already left the library). The shuffle is the existing `libraryShuffled` event over the state's generator, so it is deterministic. `legal` is sorted by name and then by creation order, never by library position (CR 401.2). Hidden information: the view of the searcher lists the matching cards in `GameView.pendingCards` (the library is no part of the view); everyone else gets `GameView.waitingOn { seat, purpose, sourceName }` and nothing about a card or a zone. The log of the opponent must show only `librarySearched` and, with a reveal, `cardsRevealed`: the `zoneChanged` events of the search carry the object ids of the cards. Not built: another player's library, "top N cards", searching graveyard or hand, library destinations ("put it on top"), "reveal and the opponent chooses", search for a card by name, finding fewer than `max` forced by an undefined quality (CR 701.23c) or a quantity (CR 701.23d), where the answer is `min` 0 anyway.

## Discard (slice S5: engine)

- CR: 701.9 (Discard), 608.2d, 117.3b
- Gap class: P (the `discard` effect existed in the AST and was not run)
- Code: `rules/search.ts` (`runDiscard`), `rules/effects.ts` (the `discard` case), the `chooseCards` decision with `purpose: 'discard'`, `zone: 'hand'`, `min = max = count`
- AST nodes: `Effect` `{ kind: 'discard', player: PlayerSelector, count: Amount }`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.effectKinds` lists it
- Tests: `scenario-xx-discard.test.ts` (draw then discard, fewer cards than asked, an opponent discarding, each player discarding, rejected answers), `ast-search-validate.test.ts`; AI: `search-handlers.test.ts`, `search-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: Mind Rot ("Target player discards two cards"); the "draw a card, then discard a card" of Merfolk Looter once activated abilities and the grammar read it (read by the parser since stage P2)
- Added in: no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump of its own, 2026-10-21
- Notes: Each player the selector names becomes one `discard` instruction, in the selector's order (the controller first for "each player"). A player with no more cards than `count` discards the whole hand without a question. Otherwise the discarding player (not always the controller, CR 701.9b) gets the decision and the answer must be exactly `count` distinct cards of their hand. A card goes from the hand to the graveyard through the zone-change pipeline and emits `zoneChanged` then `discarded { seat, object }` (the id the card had in the hand). The cleanup-step discard keeps its own decision kind, `discard`; `chooseCards` is the effect's. Not built: random discard, "an opponent chooses which card is discarded", discard as a cost (`Cost` `discard`), "discard your hand", "discard a card at random" and replacements other than madness (built since D-128: every discard goes through `discardOne`, `rules/madness.ts`).

## Activated abilities (slice S3: engine)

- CR: 602.1 and 602.1a (cost and effect), 602.2 and 602.2a-b (activating, the ability on the stack, the steps of 601.2b-i), 602.5a and 302.6 (summoning sickness and {T}), 602.5d (sorcery speed), 118.3 and 118.3b (paying costs, life), 119.4 (life paid is at most the total), 701.21 (sacrifice as a cost), 113.6 and 113.6b (the zone an ability functions in), 113.7a and 608.2b (the ability on the stack, targets that became illegal), 733 (cancelling an activation)
- Gap class: S-adjacent (a priority option and a casting-like transaction; the shapes were decided in the scaling plan, slice S3)
- Code: `packages/engine/src/rules/activate.ts` (the transaction: `beginActivate`, `payCosts`, `afterManaPaid`, `answerSacrificeCost`), `rules/activate-legality.ts` (`activationRefusal`, `activatableAbilities`, which feed `priorityOptions` and the answer check in `api.ts`), `rules/activate-costs.ts` (`breakDown`, `payImmediateCosts`, `costRefusal`, the mana plan), `rules/ability-object.ts` (the stack object, shared with triggered abilities), `rules/cast.ts` (`answerTargets` and `answerPayment` continue an activation), `rules/stack.ts` (an ability's own targets and text on resolution), `state/types.ts` (`ActivationContext` in `CastingContext`, `StackData.ability` widened to `StackAbility`)
- AST nodes: `Ability` `{ kind: 'activated', cost: Cost[], targets?, effect, timing?: 'instant' | 'sorcery', functionsIn?: 'battlefield' | 'hand' }`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.abilityKinds` lists `activated`, `costKinds` is `tap`, `mana`, `payLife`, `sacrifice` (`this` or an `ObjectFilter`), `removeCounters` and `discardThis`. A `PriorityOption` and `Action` `{ kind: 'activate', object, abilityIndex }` (`abilityIndex` is the index among the object's derived abilities; mana abilities keep `activateAbility`, which the AI and the web already use). `ObjectView.activated` lists `{ abilityIndex, ability }` for display and for agents. Event: `abilityActivated { seat, source, abilityIndex, stackObject }` (it existed; mana abilities do not emit it).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-activated-abilities.test.ts` (the ability on the stack, windows and timing, who may activate, summoning sickness, targets before costs, payment by hand and cancel), `scenario-xx-activated-costs.test.ts` (each cost kind, the order of payment, the sacrifice choice with cancel, an unpayable cost never offered, a fizzling ability, an ability after its source died, `activate` options against `applyAction` over 16 states), `scenario-xx-activated-triggers.test.ts` (triggers set off by costs, replay determinism), `ast-activated-validate.test.ts`; AI: `activated-games.test.ts` (100 games, every path), `casual-activations.test.ts`
- Example cards: any "{cost}: effect" permanent whose cost and effect are in the lists (pingers, `{1}: ~ gets +1/+1 until end of turn`, sacrifice outlets, `{T}, pay 2 life: draw`), Treasure-like artifacts that are not mana abilities (Food and Clue, whose presets are enabled, see "Food and Clue" below); the parser reads them since stage P2 (`lower/activated.ts`; its `ability:activated` and `cost:*` gates pass)
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 3 (together with `GameState.continuousEffects`), 2026-10-21
- Notes: The order of payment is the engine's choice (CR 601.2h allows any): targets first (602.2b), then {T}, life, counters and "discard this card", then mana, then the sacrifice. {T} first means auto-payment cannot spend the source's own mana ability on the cost; a source that is sacrificed as a cost is treated as tapped when auto-payment plans (a player paying by hand may tap it first: it can still be sacrificed). The sacrifice is last so a Treasure can be tapped for mana and then sacrificed. Everything is paid inside one casting-like transaction (`GameState.casting` with an `activation`): `cancel` at the targets, payment or sacrifice decision restores the state from before the activation (the sacrifice decision carries `forCost` and lists `cancel`); once the activation finishes the costs stay paid. The offer check (`costRefusal`) runs the same steps on a copy of the state, so a listed ability can start; the one gap is a sacrifice cost whose only candidate was spent as mana by auto-payment, which the engine refuses with an error and the player can cancel (`docs/ROADMAP.md`). Paying life is allowed down to 0 (119.4), so an agent can kill itself; the casual agent does not use such abilities. A creature may target itself with its own ability (`SelectContext.stackObject` keeps the ability from targeting itself). Cost events (`tapped`, `sacrificed`, `counterRemoved`, `discarded`) are scanned for triggers when the activation pauses for a decision and when it finishes, so "whenever this becomes tapped" and "whenever you sacrifice" see them; the triggers go on the stack above the ability.

## Until-end-of-turn power and toughness (slice S3: engine)

- CR: 611.2a (duration), 611.2c (the set of objects is fixed when the effect begins), 400.7 (a new object is not in it), 613.4c (layer 7c), 514.2 (cleanup), 704.5f (toughness 0 or less)
- Gap class: P (the `modifyPt` effect existed in the AST and was not run) plus state (the first continuous effect)
- Code: `rules/continuous.ts` (`runContinuous`, `expireEndOfTurnEffects`), `rules/derive-layers.ts` (`applyPtLayers` applies the effects in layer 7c with the counters), `rules/flow.ts` (`afterDiscard` expires them with damage removal), `rules/effects.ts` (the `modifyPt` case), `state/types.ts` (`ContinuousEffect`, `GameState.continuousEffects`). The record's shape changed in part 3 slice 1 (see "Continuous effects in layers" below); this section describes the first use of it.
- AST nodes: `Effect` `{ kind: 'modifyPt', objects, power, toughness, duration }`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.effectKinds` lists `modifyPt` and `durationKinds` is `['untilEndOfTurn']`; `duration: 'permanent'` is rejected by `validateCard`. State (as built in `ENGINE_VERSION` 3; since version 6 the record is `{ id, timestamp, objects, until, change: { kind: 'modifyPt', power, toughness } }`, see below).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-pump.test.ts` (resolving, lasting through the turn, two pumps stacking, counters in layer 7c, combat before and after, the cleanup moment of damage removal, a fixed set, a pumped creature that leaves, a target that left, -2/-2 killing, a permanent duration rejected); AI: `activated-games.test.ts` (effects made and ended in 100 games)
- Example cards: `{1}: ~ gets +1/+1 until end of turn`, `{T}: target creature gets +2/+2 until end of turn`, "target creature gets -2/-2 until end of turn", "creatures you control get +1/+1 until end of turn" (a spell or an ability, any selector the interpreter reads), triggered pumps ("whenever ~ attacks, it gets +1/+0 until end of turn"); the 226-card shape of the corpus report. The parser reads the pumps since stage P2 (`effect.pump`, `effect.pump-all`, `duration.until-end-of-turn`); triggered pumps lower through the same clause.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 3, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The effect is recorded as the ability or spell resolves, for the permanents the selector names at that moment that are still on the battlefield; a target that left is skipped and an effect with no object is not recorded. The effects are listed in timestamp order and applied by addition, so their order does not matter for +N/+N. The effect does not copy the creature's identity: an object that changes zones is a new `ObjectId` and is not affected (CR 400.7); a creature that enters later is not either (611.2c). At cleanup damage and the effects go in one step (514.2), then state-based actions run, so a creature that took lethal damage only because of its pumped toughness survives the turn. Not built: any other duration, keyword grants, static "+1/+1 to your creatures", "becomes" effects.

## Cycling (slice S3: engine)

- CR: 702.29a (an activated ability that functions only from the hand), 702.29b (it exists elsewhere, and cannot be activated there), 113.6b, 602.2, 903.9a (a cycled commander)
- Gap class: K (keyword)
- Code: `rules/derive.ts` (`expandKeywords` appends the activated ability after the printed abilities), the activation path above, `rules/activate-costs.ts` (`discardThis`)
- AST nodes: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'cycling', cost: Cost[] }` (`mana` and `payLife` costs run; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.keywordKinds` lists `cycling`); the expansion is `{ kind: 'activated', cost: [...cost, { kind: 'discardThis' }], effect: draw 1, functionsIn: 'hand' }`; `Cost` `{ kind: 'discardThis' }` discards the source with no choice and is valid only on a hand ability.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-cycling.test.ts` (discard then draw, instant speed on either turn, needs its cost, hand only, owner only, an empty library, the derived abilities and indexes, the view, a life cost, a commander), `ast-activated-validate.test.ts`, `ast-supported.test.ts`; AI: `activated-games.test.ts` (Cycling occurs in most of the 100 games), `casual-activations.test.ts` (the casual agent cycles a land with four lands in hand)
- Example cards: Cycling {2} creatures and spells, cycling lands ("Cycling {1}"), "Cycling-Pay 2 life". Typecycling and landcycling are not built. The parser reads `Cycling {M}` and `Cycling—Pay N life` since stage P2 (`keyword.cycling`).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 3, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The expansion is added by `derive`, so it also exists for tokens and for cards outside the hand, and `functionsIn` is what keeps it out of play from there. Printed abilities keep their indexes (the expansion is last), so a land with a mana ability and Cycling has the mana ability at index 0, the keyword at 1 and the cycling ability at 2. The discard is paid before the mana (the order described under Activated abilities); `discarded` is emitted for it. A cycled commander lands in the graveyard and the usual state-based action asks about the command zone (CR 903.9a). The web app lists the cycling option through `ObjectView.activated` on hand cards.

## Auras (slice S4a: engine)

- CR: 303.4 and 303.4a-d (an Aura's enchant ability defines its target and what it can enchant, an illegal host, itself), 303.4e (the Aura's controller is not the enchanted object's), 608.3b-c (an Aura spell enters attached to its target, or does not resolve), 608.2b (an illegal target), 702.5a and 702.5c (Enchant), 704.5m (an illegal or missing host), 701.3 (Attach), 702.11b (hexproof limits targeting, not attachment)
- Gap class: K (keyword) plus state (`GameObject.attachedTo`, which the state already had and nothing used)
- Code: `rules/attach.ts` (`auraTargetSpec`, `canAttach`, `attachEntered`, `illegalAuras`), `rules/legality.ts` (`targetSpecsOf` adds the Aura's target slot, so `canCast`, `beginCast` and the targets decision need no new path), `rules/stack.ts` (`finish` attaches the permanent the Aura became to the target it had; `resolveTop` fizzles it as any spell), `rules/sba.ts` (704.5m), `rules/derive.ts` (`Derived.attachedTo`, `Derived.attachments`), `view.ts` (`ObjectView.attachedTo`, `ObjectView.attachments`)
- AST nodes: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'enchant', filter: ObjectFilter }` ("Enchant creature" is `{ types: ['creature'] }`, "Enchant creature you control" adds `controller: 'you'`, "Enchant land" `{ types: ['land'] }`, "Enchant permanent" `{}`); `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.keywordKinds` lists `enchant`. A face with the subtype Aura must be an enchantment with exactly one enchant ability and only an Aura has one (structural, `validate-attach.ts`). Event: `attached { object, to }`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-auras.test.ts` (the targets offered by each enchant filter, entering attached with the bonus, the view, an opponent's creature, cancel, not castable without a host, hexproof, fizzle on an illegal target, the host dying, changing type or controller, attached to nothing or to itself, replay), `ast-attachments-validate.test.ts`, `ast-supported.test.ts`; AI: `attachment-games.test.ts` (100 games), `casual-attachments.test.ts`
- Example cards: the 892 cards of the corpus report whose only blocker is `Enchant creature`, and the other "Enchant [permanent type]" Auras with a "+N/+N" or "has [keyword]" static ability (read since stage P3).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 4, 2026-10-21
- Notes: An Aura spell has one more target slot than its text declares, with id `enchant`, first in order; hexproof, shroud and the controller filter are checked by the ordinary target legality. On resolution the spell fizzles when that target is illegal (it goes to the graveyard and no `attached` event happens); otherwise the permanent is created and attached in the same step, so its enters triggers see it attached. State-based actions then keep the pair legal: the check uses the enchant filter relative to the Aura's controller but ignores hexproof and shroud (they restrict targeting only), so a creature that gains hexproof keeps its Aura, and an Aura on "creature you control" falls off when the creature changes controller. An Aura that leaves for the graveyard emits only the zone change, no `unattached`. An Aura that enters the battlefield without being cast has no host (CR 303.4f lets its controller choose one, which the engine does not ask): `validateCard` rejects a `search` or `moveToZone` to the battlefield whose filter could match an Aura (it names no type, or enchantment, or the Aura subtype), and an Aura token; see `docs/ROADMAP.md`.

## Equipment and Equip (slice S4a: engine)

- CR: 301.5 and 301.5a-c (an Equipment attaches to a creature, enters unattached, and an illegal host unattaches it), 702.6a (Equip), 701.3b-d (attach: nothing happens if it cannot or is already there, a new timestamp, unattaching), 602.5d (sorcery speed), 704.5n and 704.5p (an Equipment attached to an illegal permanent stays on the battlefield unattached)
- Gap class: K (keyword) plus P (the `attach` effect)
- Code: `rules/derive.ts` (`equipAbility`, `expandKeywords` expands Equip like Cycling), `rules/effects.ts` (the `attach` case), `rules/attach.ts` (`attachObject`, `canAttach`, `illegalAttachments`, `unattach`), `rules/sba.ts` (704.5n, 704.5p)
- AST nodes: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'equip', cost: Cost[] }` (`mana` and `payLife` costs run); `Effect` `{ kind: 'attach', object: ObjectRef, to: ObjectRef }`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES` lists `equip` and `attach`. The expansion is `{ kind: 'activated', cost, targets: [{ id: 't1', what: creature you control }], effect: attach this to t1, timing: 'sorcery' }`, appended after the printed abilities. Events: `attached { object, to }`, `unattached { object, from, reason: 'left' | 'illegal' | 'replaced' }`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-equipment.test.ts` (the expansion, the targets, equipping and the view, unattached on entering, sorcery timing and the legal list against `applyAction`, re-equipping, the same creature, two Equipment, the creature dying, bounced, retyped, stolen, a lost subtype, an equip target that died in response, a life cost), `ast-attachments-validate.test.ts`; AI: `attachment-games.test.ts`, `casual-attachments.test.ts`
- Example cards: the 568 cards of the corpus report whose only blocker is `Equip {M}`, with "Equipped creature gets +N/+N" or "Equipped creature has [keyword]" (read since stage P3).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 4, 2026-10-21
- Notes: Control of the creature matters only when the ability is activated and when it resolves (301.5b): a creature that changes controller keeps its Equipment. Attaching to a different creature emits `unattached` (reason `replaced`) then `attached` and gives the Equipment a new timestamp (701.3c); equipping the creature it is already on does nothing and emits nothing. An Equipment attached to a creature that left the battlefield becomes unattached with reason `left`; to one that is no longer a creature, `illegal`. 704.5p also unattaches any permanent that is neither an Aura nor an Equipment (an Equipment that lost its subtype, or a creature). Equip with a restriction ("Equip legendary creature", CR 702.6c), "Equip [cost]" that is not mana or life, reconfigure and Fortifications are not built.

## Static abilities with a subject: +N/+N and keyword grants (slice S4a: engine)

- CR: 604.1 and 611.3 (a static ability generates a continuous effect), 611.3a (not locked in: whatever the text describes at any moment), 611.3b (only while the source is on the battlefield), 613.1f (layer 6, abilities), 613.4c (layer 7c), 613.7 (timestamps)
- Gap class: S-adjacent (the first layered static effects; shapes decided in the scaling plan, slice S4a)
- Code: `rules/derive-statics.ts` (`staticEffectsOn`: one scan of the battlefield per game state, cached in a `WeakMap` keyed by the state's object table and checked against the battlefield and the definitions), `rules/derive.ts` (`finish`: layer 6 then layer 7c; `withGrantedKeywords`), `rules/filter-match.ts` (the one filter test, shared with `select.ts`), `ast/validate-attach.ts`, `ast/validate.ts`, `ast/supported.ts`
- AST nodes: `StaticEffect` `{ kind: 'modifyPt', subject, power, toughness }` and `{ kind: 'grantKeyword', subject, keyword }` beside `noMaximumHandSize`; `StaticSubject` `{ kind: 'attached' }` ("Equipped creature", "Enchanted creature": the permanent the source is attached to) or `{ kind: 'filter', filter, other? }` ("Creatures you control", "Other creatures you control", "Creatures your opponents control"). `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.staticKinds` lists `modifyPt` and `grantKeyword`; the new dimension `staticSubjectKinds` lists `attached` and `filter` (feature reference `staticSubject:attached`). Grantable keywords: flying, trample, haste, vigilance, lifelink, deathtouch, first strike, double strike, menace, reach, hexproof, shroud, indestructible, defender.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-static-abilities.test.ts` (the number in the view and in combat, a bonus that ends with its source, derived abilities and view keywords, a keyword not added twice, activation indexes kept, flying in blocking both ways, hexproof against targeting, an Anthem for creatures that enter later and only on the battlefield, "you" following the controller, "other", counters plus Aura plus Anthem plus pump, replay), `ast-attachments-validate.test.ts`, `ast-features.test.ts`
- Example cards: the 172 `Equipped creature gets +N/+N` and 129 `Enchanted creature gets +N/+N` cards of the corpus report, `Enchanted creature has flying`, "Creatures you control get +1/+1", "Other creatures you control have flying".
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 4, 2026-10-21
- Notes: Nothing is stored. `derive` asks, for a battlefield permanent, which permanents' printed static abilities affect it: an `attached` subject matches when the source's `attachedTo` is this permanent; a `filter` subject matches by the permanent's characteristics (read after layers 4 and 5 since part 3 slice 1; before that no effect could change types, colors or subtypes) and the source's controller, so "you" follows control. Only a permanent on the battlefield is affected; a creature spell on the stack or a card in hand shows its printed numbers. Granted keywords are appended after the printed and expanded abilities, so every ability keeps its index (triggers and activations refer to indexes), and a keyword the object already has is not added again. The "+N/+N" effects are all additions in sublayer 7c, so their timestamp order does not change the sum (613.4c). Everything that reads abilities (`hasKeyword`, evasion, hexproof targeting, haste, first strike, lifelink, last-known information of a creature that dies) goes through `derive`, so a granted keyword is seen everywhere. Not built: characteristic-defining abilities (7a; setting power and toughness by an effect, 7b, exists since part 3 slice 1), granting abilities other than keywords ("has '{T}: add {G}'"), protection and ward grants, control changes (layer 2; type and color changes exist since part 3 slice 1 as effects, not statics), "can't be blocked" and "can't attack" statics, statics that count things ("gets +1/+1 for each creature you control"), conditional statics ("as long as"), and dependency (613.8).

## Conditional effects (engine slice: Kicker, Flashback, `conditional`)

- CR: 608.2c (instructions are followed in the order written, so "If [condition], [effect]. Otherwise, [else]." is read when its instruction comes up), 603.4 (the intervening "if" of a triggered ability: not this node, see "Intervening if" below)
- Gap class: P (primitive)
- Code: `rules/effects.ts` (`runEffect`, case `conditional`: evaluates the condition at that moment with `rules/conditions.ts`, then puts the chosen branch at the front of the instruction queue), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkEffect`), `ast/supported.ts` (`walkEffect` walks both branches)
- AST nodes: `Effect` `{ kind: 'conditional', condition: Condition, effect: Effect, else?: Effect }`; `Condition` is the one the `entersTapped.unless` replacement already used (`controlsAtLeast`, `opponentsAtLeast`, `and`, `or`, `not`) plus `{ kind: 'kicked' }`. Registry: `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.effectKinds` lists `conditional`, `conditionKinds` lists `kicked`; feature references `effect:conditional` and `condition:kicked`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-conditional.test.ts` (true, false, false with an else, a condition made false by an earlier instruction and one made true, the combinators, validation paths and the limits of an effect inside the else branch)
- Example cards: "If you control two or more creatures, draw two cards. Otherwise, you gain 2 life." (SYNTHETIC Rally Cry), "~ deals 2 damage to target creature. If this spell was kicked, it deals 4 damage instead." (SYNTHETIC Kicker Bolt)
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 5, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The condition is read when the instruction runs, not when the spell was cast, so an earlier instruction of the same spell can change it (`SYNTHETIC Test Wipe Then Check` in the test). "If you do" (a result of the earlier instruction) is not this node. A branch can be a `sequence`. Targets and X are those of the whole spell or ability (see Kicker for the one case this matters).

## Kicker (engine slice)

- CR: 702.33a (an optional additional cost), 702.33d (a spell is "kicked" when its controller declared the intention to pay it), 601.2b (the intention is announced as the spell is cast, before targets, CR 601.2c), 601.2f (the total cost includes it), 118.9d (it applies to an alternative cost too), 119.4 (paying life), 113.7a (an ability of a permanent that left reads its last-known information), 733 (cancelling rewinds the cast). Not built: 702.33b-c (two kicker costs, Multikicker), 702.33f (several kicker costs), 702.33g (targets chosen only if kicked), 702.33h (sticker kicker)
- Gap class: K (keyword), with the primitive `condition:kicked`
- Code: `rules/cast.ts` (`beginCast` asks the question, `answerKicker`, `toTargets`, `toPayment` adds the cost and pays the life), `rules/alt-costs.ts` (`kickerCostOf`, `manaSymbolsOf`), `rules/conditions.ts` (`kicked`), `rules/zones.ts` (`moveObject` copies `StackData.kicked` to `GameObject.kicked` when a spell becomes a permanent), `api.ts` (`answerConfirm`, `cancel`), `legal-actions.ts`, `view.ts`, `ast/validate-cast.ts`, `ast/supported.ts`
- AST nodes: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'kicker', cost: Cost[] }` (`mana` and `payLife` costs, no X; one per card); `Condition` `{ kind: 'kicked' }`; state `StackData.kicked: boolean`, `GameObject.kicked?: true`; event `spellCast` gains `kicked?: true`; `ConfirmOptionalDecision.purpose?: 'kicker'`; `ObjectView.kicked?: true`.
- Flow: `castSpell` puts the card on the stack (601.2a). When the card has kicker and the kicked total is payable, the engine asks a `confirmOptional` with `purpose: 'kicker'`, `source` the spell on the stack, `cost` the kicker cost, `sourceName` and `sourceOracleText` of the spell. `acceptOptional` kicks it, `declineOptional` casts it unkicked, `cancel` rewinds the cast; then targets, then the total cost (mana cost, or flashback cost, plus commander tax, plus the kicker mana) goes to the `payMana` decision, and a kicker life cost is paid as the total is locked in. The question has no event of its own; `spellCast` says `kicked: true`. When the spell becomes a permanent it keeps the fact (`GameObject.kicked`), so "When ~ enters, if it was kicked" reads the permanent, and its last-known information once it has left.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-kicker.test.ts` (the question and when it is asked, accept and decline, a kicked sorcery doing more, a kicked creature entering with counters, a permanent that left before its trigger resolved, `entersTapped.unless` kicked, life and mana-and-life kickers, cancel at the question and later, legal actions against `applyAction`, replay), `ast-kicker-validate.test.ts`; AI: `kicker-games.test.ts` (100 games), `casual-kicker.test.ts`
- Example cards: "Kicker {2}" spells and creatures, "Kicker-Pay 3 life". The parser reads `Kicker {M}` and `Kicker—Pay N life` since stage P4.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 5, 2026-10-21
- Notes: (1) The question comes before targets, as CR 601.2b-c order them; it is asked only when the kicked total is payable, otherwise the spell is cast unkicked without asking, so `getLegalActions` and `applyAction` agree. (2) A kicker life cost that would leave the caster at 0 life is treated as not payable: CR 119.4 allows it, but the cast goes on to pay mana, which the planner never does at 0 life, and the caster loses at once. (3) A target that exists only if the spell was kicked (702.33g) is not supported: `validateCard` refuses a spell whose target is used only inside a branch of a `kicked` conditional. (4) The `kicked` condition without a kicker ability on the card is refused as a structural error. (5) "When ~ enters, if it was kicked, ..." is written as an enters trigger with a `conditional` effect: an unkicked permanent still puts the trigger on the stack and it does nothing (that is the `conditional` form; `interveningIf`, CR 603.4, is the form that does not trigger at all, see "Intervening if" below; both stay valid).

## Flashback (engine slice)

- CR: 702.34a (cast from the graveyard by paying the flashback cost rather than the mana cost; exiled instead of going anywhere else when it leaves the stack), 118.9 and 118.9a (alternative costs, one at a time), 118.9d (additional costs, here kicker, apply to it), 601.2b-f (casting), 608.2b (a spell with only illegal targets is removed from the stack: here exiled), 701.6 (a countered spell: here exiled), 117.1a and 304.5 (timing)
- Gap class: K (keyword)
- Code: `rules/legality.ts` (`castZone` with an alternative, `totalCostSymbols`, `canCast`, `flashbackObjects`), `rules/priority.ts` (the `castSpell` option with `alternative: 'flashback'`), `api.ts` (`isOffered` compares the alternative), `rules/cast.ts` (`beginCast` takes the alternative; `StackData.flashback`), `rules/zones.ts` (`moveObject`: a stack object with `flashback` goes to exile whatever zone was asked), `rules/alt-costs.ts`, `view.ts`, `ast/validate-cast.ts`
- AST nodes: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'flashback', cost: Cost[] }` (mana only, no X; one per card; only on an instant or sorcery); `PriorityOption` and `Action` `castSpell` gain `alternative?: 'flashback'` (the object is then in the owner's graveyard); `StackData.flashback: boolean`; event `spellCast` gains `alternative?: 'flashback'`; `ObjectView.flashback?: true`; registry `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.castAlternatives` lists `flashback` and `keywordKinds` lists `flashback`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-flashback.test.ts` (what is offered and refused: hand, no keyword, cost, the opponent's graveyard, timing, targets; cast and exile on resolution, on a counter and on a fizzle; the whole life of the card; cancel; kicker together with flashback; replay and invariants), `ast-kicker-validate.test.ts`; AI: `kicker-games.test.ts`, `casual-kicker.test.ts`
- Example cards: "Flashback {2}{B}" instants and sorceries. The parser reads `Flashback {M}` since stage P4.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 5, 2026-10-21
- Notes: The graveyard card is not a play without `alternative: 'flashback'`, and a hand card cannot be cast with it. The exile is a replacement in the zone-change pipeline, not code of `stack.ts`, so every way a flashback spell leaves the stack (resolving, `counter`, a fizzle, a `moveToZone` effect) is covered; it applies before the commander-zone replacement (CR 903.9b), which would otherwise ask about a commander spell going to hand or library. The first cast from the hand is an ordinary cast and puts the card in the graveyard. The AI: `CasualAgent` casts every worthwhile spell in hand first, then the dearest worthwhile flashback card it can afford; `RandomLegalAgent` picks among the legal actions as always.

## Intervening if (engine follow-up)

- CR: 603.4 (the ability triggers only if the condition is true when the event occurs, and checks it again as it resolves; if it is false then it is removed from the stack and does nothing; it mirrors the target check of 608.2b), 603.10a (leaves-the-battlefield triggers look back in time), 113.7a (last-known information), 702.33d (kicked)
- Gap class: P (primitive)
- Code: `rules/triggers.ts` (`findTriggers` evaluates the condition with `rules/conditions.ts` against the battlefield the event is matched on, and makes no `PendingTrigger` when it is false), `rules/stack.ts` (`resolveTop` checks it again before targets and effects), `ast/validate.ts` (the condition is checked like any other), `ast/supported.ts` (`conditionIssues`, and the look-back restriction), `ast/features.ts` (`LOOK_BACK_TRIGGER_KINDS`, `TRIGGER_FEATURES`)
- AST nodes: `Ability` `triggered` gains optional `interveningIf?: Condition` (`controlsAtLeast`, `opponentsAtLeast`, `kicked`, `and`, `or`, `not`: `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.conditionKinds`). Registry: `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.triggerFeatures` is `['interveningIf']`; the feature reference the parser names is `trigger:interveningIf` (plus the `condition:` references of the condition itself, for example `condition:kicked`). Event `fizzled` gains optional `reason: 'interveningIf'`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-intervening-if.test.ts` (an unkicked creature makes no stack object, no `abilityTriggered` and no `orderTriggers` question while a kicked one does; "if you control no other creatures" with `not` and `other`; a count that can never hold; "whenever you cast a spell, if ..." true at the cast and false on resolution after removal in response, and true on both; a kicked source that left still reads its last-known information; two triggers of one event where the first to resolve falsifies the other's "if"; replay and legal actions), `ast-intervening-validate.test.ts`; AI: `food-clue-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: "When ~ enters, if it was kicked, ..." (SYNTHETIC Kicker Warden), "Whenever you cast a spell, if you control two or more creatures, ..." (SYNTHETIC Crowd Pleaser), "When ~ enters, if you control no other creatures, ..." (SYNTHETIC Lone Wolf)
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; the parser lowers the segmenter's intervening-if slot since stage P4
- Added in: no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump (no state shape change; the `PendingTrigger` and `StackData` already carry the whole ability, which now includes the condition), 2026-10-21
- Notes: (1) The condition is read by the ability's controller: "you control" counts their permanents, and "other" leaves the source out. (2) At the trigger it is read on the battlefield as the event is matched (right after the event, so an entering creature counts itself unless `other` is set). (3) For `dies` and `permanentSacrificed` triggers, which look back in time (603.10a), a condition that counts permanents (`controlsAtLeast`, also inside `and`/`or`/`not`) is refused by `validateCard`, because the engine would read the battlefield after the event; `kicked` and `opponentsAtLeast` are fine. (4) On resolution the ability is removed through the same path as a fizzled one (`fizzled`, then the object leaves the stack), so no `resolved` event follows and no effect runs, including an "optional" question. (5) What the parser produces since stage P4: for `When ~ enters, if it was kicked, put two +1/+1 counters on it.` the engine shape is `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger: { kind: 'entersBattlefield', what: { kind: 'this' } }, interveningIf: { kind: 'kicked' }, effect: { kind: 'addCounters', objects: { kind: 'this' }, counter: '+1/+1', count: 2 } }`, with `needs` `trigger:entersBattlefield`, `trigger:interveningIf`, `condition:kicked`, `effect:addCounters`; the effect is NOT wrapped in `conditional` (that would be the old shape and would still put the trigger on the stack). The card also needs a kicker keyword (`validateCard` refuses `kicked` without one).

## Host type of an attached subject (engine follow-up)

- CR: 303.4m ("enchanted [object]" is whatever the permanent is attached to), 301.5a (the "equipped creature"), 303.4a and 702.5a (what an Aura may enchant is its enchant ability), 613.1f and 613.4c (layers 6 and 7c)
- Gap class: P (primitive)
- Code: `rules/derive-statics.ts` (`affects`: the `attached` case checks `types` and `supertypes` against the host's characteristics before layers 6 and 7), `ast/validate-attach.ts` (`checkStaticSubject`; `GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS` is now exported from the engine's public API), `index.ts`
- AST nodes: `StaticSubject` `{ kind: 'attached', types?: CardType[], supertypes?: Supertype[] }`: the host must have every listed type and supertype; left out, any host (the old behavior). Both lists must be non-empty when present and hold known names.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-attached-host-type.test.ts` (an `Enchant permanent` Aura on a creature gives +1/+1 and on a land nothing, casting it at a land, one Aura with a land clause and a creature clause, "enchanted legendary creature", the view, `GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS`), `ast-intervening-validate.test.ts` (malformed lists); AI: `attachment-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: "Enchant permanent. Enchanted creature gets +1/+1." style Auras (SYNTHETIC Growth Aura, SYNTHETIC Land Ward Aura, SYNTHETIC Legend Aura)
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump (the AST gained optional fields only), 2026-10-21
- Notes: (1) What the parser produces since stage P4 for `Enchanted creature gets +1/+1.` on an Aura whose enchant line is `Enchant permanent`: `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'modifyPt', subject: { kind: 'attached', types: ['creature'] }, power: 1, toughness: 1 } }`, so a land host gets nothing. Producing `types: ['creature']` for every "Enchanted creature" is always right, also for `Enchant creature` Auras and for "Equipped creature" (an Equipment is attached only to creatures, CR 301.5c, so the check never fails there). "Enchanted land" is `types: ['land']`, "Enchanted legendary creature" adds `supertypes: ['legendary']`; "Enchanted permanent" has no `types`. The parser's grantable keyword list can now import `GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS` from the engine. (2) The host's types are read after layers 4 and 5, like a `filter` subject (done in part 3 slice 1; "Enchanted creature" stops applying when the host stops being a creature). (3) The Aura stays attached to a host of the wrong type for its static: only the enchant ability decides legality (CR 704.5m), so an `Enchant permanent` Aura on a land is legal and simply gives nothing from the "creature" static.

## Food and Clue (engine follow-up)

- CR: 111.10b (Food: "{2}, {T}, Sacrifice this token: You gain 3 life."), 111.10f (Clue: "{2}, Sacrifice this token: Draw a card."), 111.2 (the creator owns and controls the token), 111.7 and 704.5d (a token that leaves the battlefield ceases to exist), 602.2 (activation), 701.21 (sacrifice as a cost), 603.10a ("whenever you sacrifice" looks back), 113.7a
- Gap class: P (primitive; no new engine code, the activation path and the sacrifice cost already existed)
- Code: `cards/tokens.ts` (`TOKEN_PRESETS.Food` and `.Clue`, checked against the CR text with `pnpm rule 111.10`; both already matched), `ast/features.ts` (`SUPPORTED_FEATURES.tokenPresets` and `tokenKinds` list `Food` and `Clue`); AI: `packages/ai/src/casual-tokens.ts`, `casual-activations.ts`
- AST nodes: none new. `createToken { token: { kind: 'preset', preset: 'Food' | 'Clue' }, count }`; the abilities are ordinary `activated` abilities with costs `mana`, `tap` (Food only) and `sacrifice { what: this }`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-food-clue.test.ts` (the presets against the CR, a spell or a creature making a Food, eating it with the costs paid before the ability is on the stack, the token ceasing to exist, not offered with one mana or to the opponent, a tapped Food refused and a tapped Clue not, two Foods, a Clue cracked in response to a spell, "whenever a player sacrifices a permanent" seeing the cost and a creature-only watcher not, replay), `ast-triggers-validate.test.ts`, `ast-supported.test.ts`; AI: `casual-food-clue.test.ts`, `food-clue-games.test.ts` (100 games of the 'food' deck style)
- Example cards: any "create a Food token" and "create a Clue token" card (the parser reads the token phrase since stage P1; its `token:Food` and `token:Clue` references are now runnable)
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; every card whose only gap was `token:Food` or `token:Clue`
- Added in: no `ENGINE_VERSION` bump, 2026-10-21
- Notes: (1) The AI heuristic (`casual-tokens.ts`): by the shape of the ability, never the card name. A non-creature permanent whose activated ability costs only mana, {T} and "sacrifice this", has no target, and gains life for the agent is eaten when the agent's life is below 15 (`EAT_BELOW_LIFE`); the same costs with "draw a card" for the agent are cracked when its hand has fewer than 3 cards (`CRACK_BELOW_CARDS`). Both only on spare mana: the engine lists the option only when the cost is payable, spells are cast first, and the agent uses them only in the second main phase or an end step of either player's turn, so mana needed for combat tricks or later spells is not spent. The 15 and 3 are untuned guesses. SYNTHETIC Snack has the shape of a Food and is eaten the same way. (2) `token:Food` and `token:Clue` are runnable now, so a parser test that needs a missing token feature must use `token:Map` or `token:Blood`. (3) The Food's {T} is paid first, then the mana, then the sacrifice (D-075), so a Food is never tapped for mana by auto-payment.

## Continuous effects in layers (part 3 slice 1: engine; SPEC scenario test 17)

- CR: 613.1 (the layers and their order: 4 types, 5 colors, 6 abilities, 7 power and toughness), 613.1d, 613.1e, 613.1f, 613.4b (7b: set power and toughness), 613.4c (7c: counters and "+N/+N"), 613.7 and 613.7a-b, 613.7e (timestamps: an effect gets one when it is created, a static ability has its permanent's, an Aura or Equipment gets a new one each time it is attached), 611.2a (duration), 611.2c (the set of objects is fixed when the effect begins), 205.1a and 205.3d (a "becomes a Frog" effect replaces creature types; a type that is gone takes its subtypes), 105.3 (a new color replaces the old ones), 208.3 and 208.3a (a noncreature permanent has no power or toughness; an effect setting them is still created), 514.2 (cleanup). Not built: 613.8 (dependency), layers 1 to 3, 7a, 7d.
- Gap class: P (new effect nodes) plus S-adjacent (the layer system in `derive`; shapes decided by the lead, no new subsystem proposal)
- Code: `rules/derive-layers.ts` (one function per layer: `applyTypeLayer`, `applyColorLayer`, `applyAbilityLayer`, `applyPtLayers`; `effectsOn`, `hasLostAbilities`), `rules/derive.ts` (`finish` calls them in the order of CR 613.1), `rules/derive-statics.ts` (static abilities: a source that lost all abilities gives nothing; each keyword grant carries the timestamp of its permanent; the filter and `attached` subjects are matched after layers 4 and 5), `rules/continuous.ts` (`runContinuous` makes one `ContinuousEffect` per effect node, `expireEndOfTurnEffects`), `rules/effects.ts`, `ast/validate-continuous.ts`, `ast/supported.ts`, `characteristics.ts` (`isNonCreatureSubtype`, from CR 205.3g-205.3i, 205.3k), `state/types.ts`
- AST nodes: `Effect` `{ kind: 'setPt', objects, power, toughness, duration }`, `{ kind: 'loseAllAbilities', objects, duration }`, `{ kind: 'setTypes', objects, types?, subtypes?, duration }`, `{ kind: 'setColors', objects, colors, duration }` (empty list: colorless), `{ kind: 'grantKeyword', objects, keyword, duration }` (a keyword of `GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS`), beside `modifyPt`. `Duration` is `'untilEndOfTurn' | 'permanent'`; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.durationKinds` stays `['untilEndOfTurn']`, `validateCard` refuses `permanent`. Registry: `effectKinds` lists the five new kinds (feature references `effect:setPt`, `effect:loseAllAbilities`, `effect:setTypes`, `effect:setColors`, `effect:grantKeyword`). State: `ContinuousEffect { id: 'ce<timestamp>', timestamp, objects: ObjectId[], until: 'endOfTurn' | 'permanent', change }` with `ContinuousChange` `{ kind: 'modifyPt' | 'setPt', power, toughness } | { kind: 'loseAllAbilities' } | { kind: 'grantKeyword', keyword } | { kind: 'setTypes', types?, subtypes? } | { kind: 'setColors', colors }`. Deviation from the shape the lead gave: `setTypes.types` is optional (at least one of `types` and `subtypes` is required), because "becomes a Frog" sets only a creature type and keeps the card types (CR 205.1a): giving `types: ['creature']` would strip an artifact creature of being an artifact.
