- How the layers are applied (`derive`, for one object, from its base characteristics each time, CR 613.5): (1) layer 4: `setTypes` effects in timestamp order; card types are replaced (an instant or sorcery keeps its type), creature types are replaced by `subtypes` (other subtypes stay, CR 205.3d), and without creature or kindred no creature type stays; (2) layer 5: the last `setColors` wins; (3) the static abilities of battlefield permanents are matched against the result of layers 4 and 5 (a "Frogs you control get +2/+2" lord affects a creature that became a Frog); (4) layer 6: the printed abilities with the expansions of their keywords (Cycling, Equip, Prowess), then the keyword grants of static abilities and of `grantKeyword` effects and the `loseAllAbilities` effects, merged by timestamp: "loses all abilities" empties the list as it stands at its timestamp (printed, expanded and granted abilities alike) and a grant with a later timestamp is added after it; a keyword already present is not added twice; (5) layer 7b: the last `setPt` wins, even for an object with no power and toughness yet; (6) layer 7c: counters (+1/+1 minus -1/-1), static "+N/+N" and `modifyPt` effects are added (addition commutes, so their order does not matter, CR 613.4c). A noncreature permanent on the battlefield has no power or toughness (CR 208.3). A permanent that lost all abilities gives nothing through its own static abilities (`hasLostAbilities`). An Equipment or Aura gets a new timestamp whenever it is attached (`attachObject`), so an Equipment moved onto a creature after "loses all abilities" grants its keywords again.
- Tests: `scenario-17-layers.test.ts` (SPEC test 17: Giant Growth then Turn to Frog is a 5/5 blue Frog with no abilities, in either order and with Turn to Frog cast in response; the abilities truly gone: no {T} ability offered, no attack trigger; the effects ending at cleanup; the records' shape and timestamps; set then modify in both orders; two sets with the later timestamp winning; -1/-1 counters on a set value; a grant after "loses all abilities" applies and one before does not; flying by an effect in combat (the blocker is not even asked); a fixed set (a creature cast after "each creature loses all abilities" keeps its abilities); a lord and an Equipment losing their statics, an Equipment attached again after the effect granting again; a Frog lord reading layer 4; becoming an artifact (no creature type, no power, back at the next turn); colors; a permanent duration; replay; legal actions), `ast-continuous-validate.test.ts`, `scenario-xx-pump.test.ts` (the record's shape); AI: `layers-games.test.ts` (100 games of the 'layers' deck style: effects of every kind, flickers, prowess, effects ended at cleanup, replay hashes), `casual-helpful.test.ts`
- Example cards: Turn to Frog ("Target creature loses all abilities and becomes a blue Frog with base power and toughness 1/1 until end of turn."), Giant Growth, "Target creature gains flying until end of turn.", "Target creature has base power and toughness 4/4 until end of turn.", "Target creature becomes an artifact until end of turn." (replacing types, not "in addition"). Names in the tests are real for Giant Growth and Turn to Frog (texts and the mana cost of Turn to Frog from memory, UNVERIFIED against Scryfall) and SYNTHETIC elsewhere (`cards/fixtures/synthetic-layers.ts`).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 6, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce (the grammar stage for this slice is not built): (1) `Target creature gets +3/+3 until end of turn.` (Giant Growth): the existing `effect.pump` shape, `{ kind: 'modifyPt', objects: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' }, power: 3, toughness: 3, duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' }` with `t1` a creature on the battlefield; unchanged. (2) `Target creature gains flying until end of turn.`: `{ kind: 'grantKeyword', objects: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' }, keyword: { kind: 'flying' }, duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' }`; `gets +2/+2 and gains first strike until end of turn` is a `sequence` of a `modifyPt` and a `grantKeyword` on the same target and duration; two keywords are two `grantKeyword` nodes; needs `effect:grantKeyword`, `duration:untilEndOfTurn`. Only the keywords of `GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS` (a keyword with a parameter, flash and prowess are not). (3) Turn to Frog: a `sequence` in the order of the text of `loseAllAbilities`, `setTypes { subtypes: ['Frog'] }`, `setColors { colors: ['U'] }` and `setPt { power: 1, toughness: 1 }`, all with the same `objects` and `duration: 'untilEndOfTurn'`; needs `effect:loseAllAbilities`, `effect:setTypes`, `effect:setColors`, `effect:setPt`. "Becomes a [creature type]" with no card type sets only `subtypes`; "becomes an artifact" sets `types: ['artifact']` and replaces the card types; any "in addition to its other types", "is still a land" and "becomes an artifact creature" wording is the additive form (CR 205.1b), which is not built and must be left unread. "Becomes a [color]" is `setColors` with that one color, "becomes colorless" an empty list. "Has base power and toughness N/N" is `setPt`. A creature type must not be one of the non-creature subtypes (`validateCard` refuses `Island`, `Equipment` and the rest of CR 205.3g-205.3k).

## Flicker and the `moved` reference (part 3 slice 1: engine; SPEC scenario test 3)

- CR: 400.7 (a zone change makes a new object), 400.7j (other parts of an effect can find the object an effect moved to a public zone), 111.8 (a token that left the battlefield cannot move again), 110.2 (under whose control a permanent enters), 608.2b (a spell whose target became a new object fizzles), 603.6a (the returned permanent's enters abilities trigger again), 302.6 (it is summoning sick)
- Gap class: P (primitive: a back-reference between instructions)
- Code: `rules/effects.ts` (`moving` resets `lastMoved` when an `exile`, `destroy` or `moveToZone` effect starts; each `move` instruction adds the new object; the `controller` option; the CR 111.8 guard), `rules/stack.ts` (`continueResolution` stores `lastMoved` in the resolution context), `rules/select.ts` (`moved` in `resolveObjects`, `SelectContext.moved`), `rules/step-outcome.ts`, `state/types.ts` (`ResolutionContext.lastMoved`, the `move` instruction's `controller`), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`moved` only after a move effect in the same sequence), `ast/supported.ts` (the Aura check follows `moved` back to the filter of the latest move effect before it)
- AST nodes: `ObjectRef` `{ kind: 'moved' }` ("it": the new objects the previous `exile`, `destroy` or `moveToZone` effect of this resolution produced; an empty set when it moved nothing); `Effect` `moveToZone` gains optional `controller: 'owner' | 'you'` (battlefield only: "under its owner's control" or "under your control"; left out it means `you`, the controller of the spell or ability, since the correction of CR 110.2a below; it first meant the control the object had where it was, the owner for a card). Registry: `selectorKinds` lists `moved` (feature reference `selector:moved`).
- Tests: `scenario-03-object-identity.test.ts` (SPEC test 3 through a real "Exile target creature, then return it to the battlefield under its owner's control": a new object id, no counters, no damage, no Aura, Murder fizzles, the enters trigger again, summoning sick; the exile step really happens as two zone changes; a token does not come back; "each creature you control"; nothing exiled, nothing returned; a commander ends on the battlefield with no question; "under your control" takes a creature from its owner; a stolen creature returns to its owner), `ast-continuous-validate.test.ts` (validation and the Aura restriction); AI: `layers-games.test.ts` (flickers in 100 games)
- Example cards: the flicker family, for example Cloudshift ("Exile target creature you control, then return that card to the battlefield under your control." from memory, UNVERIFIED) and "Exile target creature, then return it to the battlefield under its owner's control." (the SYNTHETIC Flicker Spell). No real Oracle text was fetched in this slice.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 6, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: `Exile target creature, then return it to the battlefield under its owner's control.` is `{ kind: 'sequence', effects: [{ kind: 'exile', objects: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } }, { kind: 'moveToZone', objects: { kind: 'moved' }, to: 'battlefield', controller: 'owner' }] }` with `t1` the creature target; "under your control" is `controller: 'you'` and "return it to the battlefield" with no control clause leaves `controller` out, which now means `you` (CR 110.2a; see "The default controller" below). The pronoun "it", "that card" or "that creature" after an `exile`, `destroy` or move is `{ kind: 'moved' }`; the same word after a target in an effect that does not move (a `modifyPt`) stays the target reference. `tapped` goes on the same `moveToZone` ("returns tapped"). Needs `effect:exile`, `effect:moveToZone`, `selector:moved`. A target that could be an Aura (a permanent or an enchantment) makes the card unsupported (CR 303.4f), which `validateCard` reports.
- Notes: The new object is found through the instruction queue, not through an id lookup: `exile` expands to `move` instructions, each emits `zoneChanged { from, to }` and adds `to` to the resolution's `lastMoved`, and the later `moveToZone` resolves `moved` to those ids. A target that was already gone when the effect ran is skipped, so nothing is exiled and nothing returns. The exiled object was removed from the battlefield, so its attachments fall off (CR 704.5m for Auras, 704.5n for Equipment) and its counters and damage are gone (CR 400.7). Two triggers of one event from the new object are new triggers (CR 603.6a). `groupIdentical` (`rules/trigger-stack.ts`) now also treats triggers with the same text from one source as identical, which matters for two instances of prowess.

## Prowess (part 3 slice 1: engine; SPEC scenario test 20)

- CR: 702.108a (Prowess: "Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, this creature gets +1/+1 until end of turn."), 702.108b (each instance triggers separately), 603.2c (each spell cast is a separate trigger), 405.5 (the trigger goes on the stack above the spell, so it resolves first), 613.1f (a creature that lost all abilities lost prowess), 611.2a and 514.2 (until end of turn)
- Gap class: K (keyword)
- Code: `rules/derive-keywords.ts` (`expandKeywords`, moved out of `derive.ts`: Cycling, Equip and Prowess), `rules/derive.ts`, `ast/types.ts`, `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate.ts`
- AST nodes: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'prowess' }`, no parameters; `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.keywordKinds` lists `prowess` (feature reference `keyword:prowess`). `derive` appends the triggered ability `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger: { kind: 'castSpell', filter: { stackKind: 'spell', notTypes: ['creature'] }, by: 'you' }, effect: { kind: 'modifyPt', objects: { kind: 'this' }, power: 1, toughness: 1, duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' } }` after the printed abilities, and the keyword stays on the object. Not grantable by an effect or a static ability (`GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS`).
- Tests: `scenario-20-prowess.test.ts` (SPEC test 20: the keyword expands, the trigger goes above the spell and resolves first, the +1/+1 appears at resolution, a lethal burn on the creature itself does not kill it, two spells are two triggers, a creature spell or the opponent's spell or a land does not trigger it, the bonus ends at cleanup; two instances give two triggers and +2/+2; a creature that lost all abilities does not trigger), `ast-continuous-validate.test.ts`, `ast-supported.test.ts`; AI: `layers-games.test.ts` (prowess triggers in 100 games)
- Example cards: any creature whose Oracle text has a `Prowess` line (the keyword with its reminder text in parentheses).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 6, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: the keyword line `Prowess` (also with the reminder text `(Whenever you cast a noncreature spell, this creature gets +1/+1 until end of turn.)`, and each of `Prowess, prowess` as one keyword) is `{ kind: 'keyword', keyword: { kind: 'prowess' } }`; the reminder text is dropped like that of the other keywords. Needs `keyword:prowess`. The parser's keyword vocabulary (`packages/cards/src/convert/parser/rules/keyword/vocabulary.ts`, `SimpleKeyword` and `SIMPLE_KEYWORDS`) is a hand-written list of the keywords without parameters, so `prowess` has to be added to it, but not to the parser's `GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS`.

## Ward (part 3 slice 2: engine; SPEC scenario test 19)

- CR: 702.21a (Ward: "Whenever this permanent becomes the target of a spell or ability an opponent controls, counter that spell or ability unless that player pays [cost]."), 601.2c and 602.2b (a permanent becomes a target as the spell is cast or the ability activated), 603.2c (once per object targeted), 603.3 and 405.5 (the trigger goes on the stack above the spell or ability and resolves first), 701.6 (counter), 118.3 and 119.4 (a cost that cannot be paid is not paid; life up to the whole life total), 605.3a (mana is paid by tapping sources as part of the payment), 106.6 (restricted mana does not pay for a ward cost), 608.2b (an object that already left the stack is skipped), 613.1f (a creature that lost all abilities lost ward). Not built: 702.21b (a ward cost with X).
- Gap class: K (keyword) plus P (the `becomesTarget` trigger, the `counterUnlessPays` effect, the `triggerSubject` reference); shapes decided by the lead, no new subsystem proposal
- Code: `rules/derive-keywords.ts` (`wardAbility`), `rules/becomes-target.ts` (`becameTargetEvents`, called by `finishCast` in `rules/cast.ts` and `finishActivation` in `rules/activate.ts`), `rules/triggers.ts` (the `becomesTarget` occurrence and match), `rules/trigger-subject.ts` and `rules/trigger-stack.ts` (the subject is stored on the ability's stack object and joins the identity of its triggers), `rules/ability-object.ts`, `rules/pay-unless.ts` (the question and the payment), `rules/pay-manual.ts` (paying by tapping chosen sources, shared with casting), `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionUnlessConfirm`, `answerResolutionUnlessPayment`; it runs the instruction because `effects.ts` cannot import the mana engine), `rules/effects.ts`, `rules/select.ts` (`triggerSubject`), `api.ts` (`answerConfirm`, `answerWardPayment`), `ast/types.ts`, `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate.ts`, `ast/validate-parts.ts`, `ast/supported.ts`, `state/types.ts`, `state/decisions.ts`, `state/events.ts`
- AST nodes: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'ward', cost: Cost[] }` (`keyword:ward`; costs `mana` without X and `payLife`); `Trigger` `{ kind: 'becomesTarget', what: { kind: 'this' } | ObjectFilter, by: 'you' | 'opponent' | 'any' }` (`trigger:becomesTarget`); `Effect` `{ kind: 'counterUnlessPays', objects: ObjectSelector, cost: Cost[] }` (`effect:counterUnlessPays`; `objects` must be `{ kind: 'triggerSubject' }` of a trigger whose subject is a spell or ability: a cast or becomes-the-target trigger; "counter target spell unless its controller pays {3}" is therefore still unsupported); `ObjectRef` `{ kind: 'triggerSubject' }` (`selector:triggerSubject`; valid only as that object and as a copy source, see "Token copies"). `derive` appends `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger: { kind: 'becomesTarget', what: { kind: 'this' }, by: 'opponent' }, effect: { kind: 'counterUnlessPays', objects: { kind: 'triggerSubject' }, cost } }` after the printed abilities for each instance of the keyword; the keyword stays. Events: `becameTarget { object, by, seat }` (once per object a spell or ability targets, before `spellCast` or `abilityActivated`); the answer is `optionalAnswered` and `countered`.
- Flow: the spell is cast (or the ability activated) and the `becameTarget` events reach `grantPriority`, which puts the ward trigger on the stack above it. It resolves first: for each spell or ability named (`StackData.triggerSubject`, `ResolutionContext.triggerSubject`) a `counterUnlessPays` instruction runs. If the controller of the spell cannot pay (life at least the life cost, mana planned from the pool and the untapped sources after the life is paid, restricted mana excluded) it is countered at once and nobody is asked. Otherwise that player (not the ward permanent's controller) gets a `confirmOptional` with `purpose: 'ward'`, `cost` and `subject` (the spell or ability, `source` is the ward ability on the stack). Decline: countered. Accept: the life is paid now; with a mana part they get a `payMana` with `purpose: 'ward'` and `subject`, answered by `payAuto` or `payManual` (the same payment code as a cast) or `cancel`, which means "do not pay" and counters it. Two ward permanents targeted by one spell are two triggers of one player, so that player orders them (603.3b); once the first one counters the spell the second finds nothing and asks nothing.
- Tests: `scenario-19-ward.test.ts` (SPEC test 19: the trigger on the stack above the spell and the events, the question asked of the spell's controller, paying {2} by the planner and by chosen sources, a payment that does not cover the cost is refused, declining and cancelling counter, an unaffordable cost counters without a question, pool mana pays, the controller's own spells and abilities and spells that target something else or a player do not trigger it, a two-target spell is countered as a whole and resolves for both when paid, two warded targets, an ability countered with its costs paid, ward with a life cost accepted, declined and unaffordable, the spell countered in response, a creature that lost all abilities, the view of the trigger, legal actions, determinism), `ast-ward-copy-validate.test.ts`, `ast-supported.test.ts`, `ast-features.test.ts`; AI: `casual-ward-legend.test.ts`, `ward-games.test.ts` (100 games: triggers, the question both ways, mana and life paid, spells and abilities countered, replay)
- Example cards: any permanent whose Oracle text has a `Ward {N}` or `Ward—Pay N life.` line (the keyword with its reminder text in parentheses).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 6 (additive: new optional fields and nodes, no behavior change for an existing card), 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: `Ward {2}` is `{ kind: 'keyword', keyword: { kind: 'ward', cost: [{ kind: 'mana', symbols: ['2'] }] } }`; `Ward—Pay 3 life.` is `cost: [{ kind: 'payLife', amount: 3 }]`; the reminder text is dropped like that of the other keywords. Needs `keyword:ward`. A cost the engine does not pay when the ability resolves (`Ward—Discard a card.`, `Ward—Sacrifice a permanent.`, `Ward {X}`) still parses to the keyword with that cost and `validateCard` reports `cost "<kind>" is not implemented`, so the card stays unsupported; the parser's gate should name `cost:<kind>` for it. Not grantable by an effect or a static ability (`GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS`).

## Token copies and "becomes a copy" (part 3 slice 2: engine; SPEC scenario test 14)

- CR: 707.2 (a copy acquires the copiable values: printed characteristics as modified by copy effects; not counters, damage, status, other effects), 707.2a (abilities come with the text), 707.2b (later changes of the original do not reach the copy), 707.3 (a copy of a copy has the first copy's values), 707.4, 707.5 (a token that is a copy enters with the copied "enters tapped" and "enters with counters"), 111.4 (the token has the original's name), 111.12 (nothing is created when there is nothing to copy; last-known information is used), 608.2h, 613.1a and 613.2a (layer 1, applied before every other layer whatever the timestamps), 514.2. Not built: 707.9 ("except ..."), 707.10 and 111.13 (copying spells), "enters as a copy" (a replacement effect, Clone), merging.
- Gap class: P (token source and effect node) plus a layer; the layer-1 `copy` change was cheap (a base for `derive`), so it was built; shapes decided by the lead
- Code: `rules/copiable.ts` (`copiableValues`), `rules/derive.ts` (the base is now `copiableValues`), `rules/derive-statics.ts` (`printedAbilities` reads the copy), `rules/continuous.ts` (`runBecomeCopy`), `rules/effects.ts` (`tokenDefinitionOf`), `rules/select.ts`, `view.ts` (`oracleTextOf`), `ast/types.ts`, `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkCopySource`, `checkSubjectRef`), `ast/supported.ts` (`copySourceIssues`, `copiesThisIssues`), `state/types.ts` (`ContinuousChange` `copy`, `CopiableValues`)
- AST nodes: `TokenSpec` `{ kind: 'copyOf', object: ObjectRef }` with `object` `this`, `{ kind: 'target', id }` or `{ kind: 'triggerSubject' }` (a permanent: an enters, dies, attacks, tapped, landfall or sacrifice trigger); `Effect` `{ kind: 'becomeCopy', objects: ObjectSelector, of: ObjectRef, duration: Duration }` (`effect:becomeCopy`, until end of turn only); `ContinuousChange` `{ kind: 'copy', values: CopiableValues }` (layer 1). Feature references: `token:copyOf`, `effect:becomeCopy`, `selector:triggerSubject`.
- Flow: `createToken` with `copyOf` resolves the source (live, or last-known information), reads `copiableValues` and creates the token through `createTokens` (so the replacement effects in the copied text apply as it enters, and `tokenCreated` is emitted with the original's name). `becomeCopy` records a `ContinuousEffect` with `change: { kind: 'copy', values }` for the permanents named, fixed when it resolves; `derive` starts from the values of the last `copy` effect on an object, then applies layers 4 to 7, so counters, pumps, "loses all abilities" and "becomes a Frog" effects apply on top whatever their timestamps, and the effect ends at cleanup.
- Refusals in `validateCard` (honest, not guessed): a token copy of a permanent that could be an Aura (CR 303.4f: the copy would not ask what it enchants) or a land (CR 614.12: a shock land's copy would skip the payment question) is unsupported: the target's filter, or for `triggerSubject` the trigger's filter, must name a type that excludes both; a land or an Aura that copies `this` is unsupported; `becomeCopy` of a target not on the battlefield and a duration other than until end of turn are unsupported.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-copy-effects.test.ts` (the token has the original's name, types, colors, P/T and controller; counters, damage, tapped status, pumps, granted keywords and a "becomes a Frog" effect are not copied; a copy of a token; a copy of a ward creature has ward; copied "enters tapped" and "enters with a counter"; "a copy of this creature" from an ability; a copy from last-known information on a dies trigger; a copy of an attacking creature that is not attacking; `becomeCopy` values, counters on top, a dies ability copied, a pump and "loses all abilities" from before the copy still applying, the end at cleanup, values fixed at resolution, a copy of a copy), `scenario-14-legend-rule.test.ts`, `ast-ward-copy-validate.test.ts`; AI: `ward-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: Spitting Image ("Create a token that's a copy of target creature.", the example of CR 111.4); the other forms are the texts "Create a token that's a copy of it.", "{N}: Create a token that's a copy of this creature." and "Target creature becomes a copy of another target creature until end of turn." (no card is named for them: none was checked against Oracle text)
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 6 (additive), 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Create a token that's a copy of target creature." is `{ kind: 'createToken', token: { kind: 'copyOf', object: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } }, count: 1 }` with a target slot `t1` of the creature filter (and `copy of target nonland permanent` is `notTypes: ['land']`); "Create a token that's a copy of it." is `object: { kind: 'this' }` when "it" is the permanent the ability sits on ("When ~ enters ..., create a token that's a copy of it", "{2}: Create a token that's a copy of ~") and `object: { kind: 'triggerSubject' }` when "it" is the object of the trigger ("Whenever another creature you control enters, ..."); "two tokens that are copies of ..." is `count: 2`. Needs `effect:createToken`, `token:copyOf`, and `selector:triggerSubject` for the second form. Anything with "except" (CR 707.9), a copy of a spell, or "enter as a copy" stays unread. "Becomes a copy of target creature until end of turn" is `becomeCopy` with `of: { kind: 'target', id }`.

## The legend rule (part 3 slice 2: engine; SPEC scenario test 14)

- CR: 704.5j (Legend rule), 704.3 (state-based actions), 117.5 (before any player receives priority), 700.4 and 603.10a (the permanents put into graveyards trigger "dies" as they were), 704.5d (a token that left ceases to exist afterwards), 201.2 (same name, read after the layers), 707.2 (a copy has the original's name and supertypes)
- Gap class: S-adjacent (a state-based action with a player's choice, like the commander question of 903.9a); shapes decided by the lead
- Code: `rules/legend-rule.ts` (`legendRuleDecision`, `applyLegendRule`), `rules/sba.ts` (the legendary permanents are collected in the same pass that derives every permanent; the decision is returned like the commander one, when nothing else was performed), `api.ts` (`answerLegendRule`), `state/decisions.ts` (`ChoosePermanentDecision.purpose`), `state/events.ts` (`legendRule`)
- Decision: `choosePermanent { seat: the controller, source: the first legal permanent, purpose: 'legendRule', min: 1, max: 1, legal: the duplicates by age }`; the answer is `choosePermanents { objects: [kept] }`; the others go to their owners' graveyards together, the `legendRule { seat, name, kept }` event is emitted first, then their `zoneChanged` and `died` events, then priority is given (the triggers of the departed are put on the stack). Several names or controllers are asked one after the other (the oldest group first). It is never automatic: two permanents with one name may differ in summoning sickness, tapped status, counters, damage, attachments or effects, and the rule says the player chooses. The question is asked after the other state-based actions of the same check were performed, so a creature dying in the same check as a legend-rule loser is a separate departure for "dies" look-back (`docs/ROADMAP.md`).
- Tests: `scenario-14-legend-rule.test.ts` (SPEC test 14: a token copy of a legendary creature gives two of them and its controller is asked before priority; keeping either one, the other goes to the graveyard and its "dies" ability triggers, the token ceases to exist; the answer must be exactly one of the duplicates; the legal actions; different controllers, a nonlegendary copy and two different legends are fine; casting a second copy of the card, three at once, a creature that becomes a copy, two names asked one after the other, each controller answering for their own; determinism), `ast-ward-copy-validate.test.ts`; AI: `casual-ward-legend.test.ts`, `ward-games.test.ts` (legend rule asked in 100 games, casual keeps the best permanent)
- Example cards: any legendary permanent (the rule is the reason for the supertype); copies of one are the way to meet it in the games.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; every legendary card now plays as printed
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 6 (a new decision purpose: no state change), 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: nothing; the rule needs no card text.

## The default controller of an object put onto the battlefield (CR 110.2a; correction of D-083)

- CR: 110.2a (Permanents: "If an effect instructs a player to put an object onto the battlefield, that object enters the battlefield under that player's control unless the effect states otherwise."), 108.3 (the owner of a card), 400.3 (a card goes to its owner's hand, library or graveyard)
- Gap class: P (a wrong default in a primitive); no new node
- Correction: D-083 recorded that `moveToZone.controller` accepts `'owner' | 'you'` and, in the code and in this file's flicker section, that an omitted `controller` made the object enter "under the control it had in the zone it leaves, which for a card is its owner". That was wrong: CR 110.2a makes the player performing the effect the controller unless the effect says otherwise. An omitted `controller` now means `'you'` for `to: 'battlefield'` (the resolving effect's controller, `res.controller`); `'owner'` is the explicit exception ("under its owner's control"). DECISIONS.md is not edited; this line is the correction of D-083's consequences.
- Code: `rules/effects.ts` (the `moveToZone` case: `controller = to === 'battlefield' ? (effect.controller ?? 'you') : undefined`, with the CR comment), `ast/types.ts` (the doc comment of `moveToZone.controller`). Other destinations never take a controller: a card goes to its owner's zone (`validateCard` refuses `controller` there as before). A `search` that puts a card onto the battlefield is unchanged: it searches its own library, so the owner and the controller are one player.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-enters-control.test.ts` (a spell that puts a card from the opponent's graveyard onto the battlefield: with no control stated the caster controls it and the opponent still owns it, `'you'` says the same, `'owner'` gives it to the opponent, a card from one's own graveyard is one's own either way, a move to the hand ignores the control). The two fixtures that returned a land from the caster's own graveyard (SYNTHETIC Land Recall, a test card in `scenario-xx-enters-pay.test.ts`) now say `controller: 'you'` explicitly; nothing else in the fixtures or tests relied on the old default.
- Cards unlocked: none directly; reanimation and "put onto the battlefield" effects that take a card from another player's zone (Animate Dead-like text on an opponent's graveyard) now give the right controller as soon as a parser rule reads them.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` stays 6 (a behavior change only for a `moveToZone` to the battlefield with no `controller` that moves an object whose owner is not the effect's controller). The flicker rule of the parser always writes `controller`. The generic `lower.return` (`packages/cards/src/convert/parser/lower/effects.ts`) does emit a bare `moveToZone` to the battlefield for "Return target creature card from your graveyard to the battlefield [tapped]" and "... from a graveyard to the battlefield": those now give the caster control, which is what CR 110.2a says for them (it gave an opponent's card back to its owner before), and from your own graveyard nothing changes. No change is needed in the parser; writing `controller: 'you'` there would only make it independent of the default.
- What the parser must produce: "Return it to the battlefield under its owner's control" is `controller: 'owner'`; "under your control" and a bare "put/return it onto the battlefield" (CR 110.2a: you) are `controller: 'you'`. A rule may leave `controller` out for the bare form (it means `you`), but writing it is clearer and keeps the lowering independent of the default.

## Vehicles and Crew (engine slice; CR 301.7, 702.122)

- CR: 301.7 (Vehicles; 301.7a a Vehicle has its printed power and toughness only if it is a creature, 301.7b it has them as soon as it becomes one), 702.122a (Crew N: "Tap any number of other untapped creatures you control with total power N or greater: This permanent becomes an artifact creature until end of turn."), 702.122b and 702.122c (a creature "crews" the Vehicle it was tapped for), 205.1b ("artifact creature" and "in addition to its other types" keep all prior types and subtypes), 613.1d (layer 4), 208.3 (a noncreature permanent has no power or toughness), 302.6 (summoning sickness: a creature that crews is not using its own {T} ability, but a Vehicle that came under control this turn cannot attack), 602.2b and 733 (costs are paid as the ability is activated; cancelling rewinds), 514.2 (cleanup ends the effect), 704.5n (an Equipment on a permanent that stopped being a creature becomes unattached)
- Gap class: K (keyword) plus P (a cost, an additive type change)
- Code: `rules/derive-keywords.ts` (`crewAbility`), `rules/crew.ts` (`crewCandidates`, `totalPower`, `crewRefusal`), `rules/crew-choices.ts` (the answers `legalActionsForDecision` lists), `rules/activate-costs.ts` (`breakDown` reads `tapCreatures`; `costRefusal` asks `crewRefusal`), `rules/activate.ts` (`payCrew`, `crewChosen`, `answerCrewCost`), `api.ts` (the `choosePermanent` answer of purpose `crew`), `rules/derive-layers.ts` (`applyTypeLayer` and `additive`), `rules/continuous.ts` (`changeOf`), `rules/mana-engine.ts` (`wouldBeSummoningSick`), `view.ts` (`printedPt`, `wouldBeSummoningSick`), `ast/types.ts`, `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate.ts`, `ast/validate-parts.ts`, `ast/validate-continuous.ts`, `ast/supported.ts`, `state/types.ts`, `state/decisions.ts`, `state/events.ts`, `cards/fixtures/synthetic-vehicles.ts`
- AST nodes: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'crew', power: number }` (`keyword:crew`, `power` a positive integer); `Cost` `{ kind: 'tapCreatures', totalPower: number }` (`cost:tapCreatures`, a positive integer; alone in an ability's cost: with a mana cost, a sacrifice or a second creature-tapping cost `validateCard` says it is not implemented); `Effect` `setTypes` gains `additive?: true` (no `FeatureRef`: it is an option of `effect:setTypes`; the registry has a comment at `setTypes` in `effectKinds`) and `ContinuousChange` `setTypes` carries it; `ChoosePermanentDecision` gains `purpose: 'crew'`, `minTotalPower?: number` and `powers?: Record<ObjectId, number>`; `permanentsChosen.purpose` may be `'crew'`; `CastingContext.stage` may be `'crew'`; `ObjectView` gains optional `printedPt` and `wouldBeSummoningSick`.
- Flow: `derive` appends to a Vehicle's abilities, after the printed ones and keeping the keyword, the activated ability `{ cost: [tapCreatures N], effect: setTypes { objects: this, types: [artifact, creature], additive: true, duration: untilEndOfTurn } }`. `activate` is offered when `crewRefusal` finds untapped creatures the seat controls, other than the Vehicle, whose derived powers (counters and effects included) add up to N. Paying: targets (none), then the costs that need no mana and no choice, then `payCrew`: with exactly one candidate the engine taps it and emits `permanentsChosen { purpose: 'crew', automatic: true }`; otherwise a `choosePermanent { purpose: 'crew', forCost: true, min: 1, max: legal.length, legal, minTotalPower: N, powers }` asks, `cancel` rewinds the activation (CR 733), and an answer must be distinct creatures of `legal` with total power at least `minTotalPower` (more than needed is legal: "any number"). The chosen creatures are tapped (`tapped` events, so "whenever ~ becomes tapped" triggers see them), the ability goes on the stack, and on resolution `runContinuous` records a layer 4 effect for the Vehicle as it is now (CR 611.2c). `applyTypeLayer` adds the types (and any subtypes) to what the object has when `additive` is set; layer 7 then gives the printed 4/4 at once because the object is now a creature (CR 301.7b, 208.3). The effect ends with every until-end-of-turn effect at cleanup (CR 514.2), after which the Vehicle is an artifact with no power or toughness again.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-crew.test.ts` (the expansion and its shape; one creature crewing alone with an engine-made choice and its events; power from counters; two small creatures, with the decision's fields; an insufficient total refused with the state unchanged; more than needed; tapped, the Vehicle itself and the opponent's creatures never candidates, a crewed Vehicle crewing another; cancel rewinding to the same position; a summoning-sick creature crewing; types and the printed 4/4 as soon as the ability resolves and not before; printed keywords kept and an Equipment on top; attacking the turn it is crewed, and not when it arrived this turn; an uncrewed Vehicle neither attacking nor blocking; blocking, taking lethal damage and dying; the effect ending at cleanup; crewing again later; two crews in one turn; an Equipment targeting it only while it is a creature and falling off at cleanup; what the view shows; legal actions agreeing with `applyAction`, and the activation on offer exactly when payable; replay determinism), `scenario-xx-additive-types.test.ts` (a land becomes a land creature with its land type and the set 3/3, a creature keeps its creature types when one is added and loses them to the replacing form, the effect ending at cleanup, additive and replacing effects applying in timestamp order), `ast-crew-validate.test.ts`, the registry cases of `ast-supported.test.ts` and `ast-features.test.ts`; `packages/ai/test/casual-vehicles.test.ts` and the 100-game soak `packages/ai/test/vehicle-games.test.ts` (the `'vehicles'` deck style: replay hashing, no fallback, crews by choice and by the engine, crewed attacks, deaths and bounces while a creature, the effect ending at cleanup, Equipment on a Vehicle, crews by the casual agent and the random one).
- Example cards: any Vehicle with `Crew N` (the reminder text in parentheses follows the keyword). None was fetched in this slice; the SYNTHETIC Vehicles (Rattle Wagon 4/4 crew 3, Scout Skiff 2/2 flying crew 1, Dread Barge 6/5 trample crew 4) carry the tests.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; every Vehicle whose other text parses (`Crew N` blocks 180 corpus cards)
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 6 (additive: new optional fields, nodes and a decision purpose; no behavior change for an existing card), 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: (1) `Crew 2` (and `Crew 2 (Tap any number of untapped creatures you control with total power 2 or more: This Vehicle becomes an artifact creature until end of turn.)`) is `{ kind: 'keyword', keyword: { kind: 'crew', power: 2 } }`, needs `keyword:crew`; the reminder text is dropped like that of the other keywords. The card's type line `Artifact — Vehicle` gives `types: ['artifact']`, `subtypes: ['Vehicle']`, and its printed power and toughness stay on the face (numbers; a Vehicle's `*` values are not supported). (2) "becomes an artifact creature in addition to its other types until end of turn" (and the shorter "becomes an artifact creature until end of turn", CR 205.1b: the same retention) is `{ kind: 'setTypes', objects: <selector>, types: ['artifact', 'creature'], additive: true, duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' }`, needs `effect:setTypes`; "becomes a 3/3 artifact creature" adds `setPt` in a sequence. "Becomes a creature in addition to its other types" is `types: ['creature'], additive: true` (a land stays a land). "Becomes a [creature type] artifact creature" (CR 205.1b: retains prior types but replaces creature types) has no node yet: leave it unparsed with a named gap (ROADMAP). (3) An explicit crew-style ability in text ("Tap any number of untapped creatures you control with total power N or greater: ...") has no grammar need: use the keyword. (4) A restriction or a trigger that mentions "crewed" ("Whenever this Vehicle becomes crewed", CR 702.122e) and "can't crew Vehicles" (CR 702.122d) have no node: unparsed.
- Notes: (1) The Vehicle excludes itself from its own crew candidates ("other"), but a Vehicle that is already a creature (crewed) can crew another. (2) A creature with negative power lowers the total (CR 702.122a says "total power"); creatures with no power add nothing and are left out of the answers `legalActionsForDecision` lists, though the engine accepts them. (3) `legalActionsForDecision` lists the subsets that reach the cost as soon as their last creature is added, most powerful creatures first (up to 500), from `powers` and `minTotalPower`, so agents and the UI need no state; `getLegalActions` is the same list. (4) The cost stays alone in its ability (`breakDown` refuses it with mana or a sacrifice, `validateCard` says so): the creatures are chosen after the other costs, so a mana plan could not promise they are still untapped. (5) AI: `RandomLegalAgent` answers the crew question with one of the listed subsets. `CasualAgent` (`casual-vehicles.ts`) crews only to attack: in its own precombat main phase, once per Vehicle per turn, a Vehicle that did not arrive this turn and would be a safe attack with its printed power and toughness (`printedPt`), when spare creatures (untapped creatures of its own it would not attack with: summoning sick, defender, no power or not a safe attack, never Vehicles) with total power at least N, each with less power than the Vehicle, can pay; it taps the fewest of them, the most powerful first, and never crews to block. It does not weigh the blockers it leaves tapped through the opponent's turn. The thresholds are untuned. (6) The web does not need `printedPt` to show a crewed Vehicle, but an uncrewed one has `power: null` and the card face shows the printed pair only from `printedPt`.

## Damage prevention shields (engine slice; CR 615, 616.1, 120.3)

- CR: 615.1 (prevention effects are shields that apply as damage would be dealt), 615.4 (a shield must exist before the damage), 615.6 (prevented damage never happens), 615.7 ("prevent the next N": reduced by what it prevents, gone at 0), 615.11 (one shield per permanent for "each creature you control"), 609.7a (a source chosen when the effect is created), 611.2c (an effect that changes the rules of the game, not characteristics, reaches objects that were not there when it began), 616.1 (the affected player chooses the order of several shields: D-191, next section), 120.3 (what damage does), 514.2 (cleanup ends it). The numbers are as `pnpm rule` prints them for the CR text of this repository: "can't be prevented" is 615.12 there, not 615.11.
- Gap class: P (a primitive: one effect node, one new continuous-effect kind, one hook in the damage routine)
- Code: `rules/prevention.ts` (`runPreventDamage` makes the shields; since D-191 `rules/damage-order.ts` applies them, see the next section), `rules/damage.ts` (`dealDamage` settles the shields first: the single point every damage event goes through: combat damage, `dealDamage` effects, the rider of a mana ability), `rules/effects.ts` (the `preventDamage` case), `rules/effect-support.ts` (helpers split out of `effects.ts`), `rules/continuous.ts` (`expireEndOfTurnEffects` removes the shields at cleanup like every until-end-of-turn effect), `state/types.ts` (`ContinuousChange` `preventDamage`, `ContinuousEffect.players`), `state/events.ts` (`damagePrevented`), `ast/types.ts` (`preventDamage`, `PreventScope`, `PreventRecipient`), `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate-prevent.ts` (reached through `Scope.checkContinuous`), `ast/validate-base.ts` and `ast/validate-mana.ts` (split out of `validate-parts.ts`), `cards/fixtures/synthetic-prevention.ts`
- AST node: `{ kind: 'preventDamage', scope: 'all' | 'combat' | 'noncombat', amount: number | 'all', to: PreventRecipient, by?: ObjectRef, duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' }` (`effect:preventDamage`). `to` is `{ kind: 'any' }` (every creature, planeswalker, battle and player), a player selector (`you`, `eachPlayer`, `eachOpponent`, `controllerOf`, `ownerOf`), an object selector (`this`, `thisSpell`, `each`) or `target` (a creature, planeswalker, battle or player: an "any target" slot, a player slot or an object slot). `by` is `this`, `thisSpell` or a `target` reference (one source, never `each`).
- State: `ContinuousEffect { objects, players?, until: 'endOfTurn', change: { kind: 'preventDamage', scope, amount, recipients?, sources? } }`. `objects` and `players` are the protected permanents and seats, fixed when the spell resolves. `recipients` widens that: `{ kind: 'any' }` for Fog, `{ kind: 'each', filter, controller }` for "creatures you control", checked as damage would be dealt (CR 611.2c: a creature that enters later is covered; one that changes controller is judged by its controller then). `sources` limits it to damage from those objects (a `by` reference, fixed when it resolves). `amount` is what is left of the shield.
- Flow: `runPreventDamage` makes one effect for a shield that prevents everything (`'all'`), and for a number one shield per protected permanent or player (CR 615.11), each with its own timestamp. Nothing is made when the named recipient or source is gone (a shield with no recipient would protect everything). `dealDamage(state, source, to, amount, combat)` first calls `applyShields`: the shields that apply (scope, source, recipient; a recipient must be a permanent on the battlefield or a player) are applied, the ones that prevent everything first, then the numeric ones oldest first (CR 616.1: the chooser would always pick an "all" shield first, since it costs nothing; the engine does not ask, `docs/ROADMAP.md`). A numeric shield is reduced by what it prevents and removed at 0, and a shield that prevents nothing is not used up. Prevented damage emits `damagePrevented { source, to, amount, combat }` (the prevented part; a partly prevented hit then emits `damageDealt` for the rest); when nothing is left the routine returns there: no life loss, no marked damage, no loyalty or defense counters removed, no lifelink gain, no deathtouch mark, no commander damage (CR 615.6, 120.3). The spell or ability that dealt it still resolves ("deals 2 damage to each opponent, then you gain 2 life" still gains).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-prevention.test.ts` (Fog: nothing dealt either way, the three `damagePrevented` events with their fields, lifelink gains nothing, deathtouch does not kill, no commander damage, a planeswalker keeps its loyalty, noncombat damage still hurts, a shield made after the damage prevents nothing, the shield lasts to cleanup and is gone next turn; "to you": a Bolt Two prevented but the spell still gains life, creatures are not protected, a pain-land rider prevented, a combat-only shield of 2 absorbing 2 of 3 and leaving spell damage alone; numeric shields: 2 then 1 then nothing, on a creature and not on its controller, partly prevented damage marks the rest, a shield that prevents nothing is not used up, two equal shields oldest first (nothing asked, D-191), an "all" shield before a numeric one; noncombat scope; creatures you control: a creature that enters later is covered, the opponent's are not, the numeric form makes one shield per creature and none for later ones; "to and by" a creature as two shields; legal actions; determinism), `ast-prevent-validate.test.ts`; AI: `casual-prevention.test.ts`, the 100-game soak `prevention-games.test.ts` (the `'prevention'` deck style: replay hashing, no fallback, combat and noncombat prevention, a numeric shield used up, partly prevented damage, shields expiring at cleanup, every card cast, the casual agent casting only a Fog or "to you" shield, only in the declare blockers step of the opponent's turn). Two behaviours were mutation-checked (ignoring the scope, not reducing a numeric shield, applying numeric shields first, lifelink on prevented damage): each mutant fails the tests that pin it.
- Example cards: the shapes of Fog, Holy Day, Healing Salve and Safe Passage (named from memory of their Oracle text, UNVERIFIED: no card was fetched in this slice and none is marked supported by it); the SYNTHETIC cards of `synthetic-prevention.ts` carry the tests.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 7 (a new continuous-effect kind and an optional `players` field), 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce:
  1. "Prevent all combat damage that would be dealt this turn." is `{ kind: 'preventDamage', scope: 'combat', amount: 'all', to: { kind: 'any' }, duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' }`.
  2. "Prevent all damage that would be dealt to you this turn." is `scope: 'all', amount: 'all', to: { kind: 'you' }`; "to target player" is `to: { kind: 'target', id }` with a player target slot; "to each player" is `eachPlayer`. "Prevent all noncombat damage that would be dealt this turn." is `scope: 'noncombat', to: { kind: 'any' }`. "Prevent all combat damage that would be dealt to you this turn." is `scope: 'combat', to: you`.
  3. "Prevent all combat damage that would be dealt to and dealt by target creature this turn." is a `sequence` of two nodes: `{ scope: 'combat', amount: 'all', to: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } }` and `{ scope: 'combat', amount: 'all', to: { kind: 'any' }, by: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } }`, with a creature target slot `t1`. "Dealt by target creature" alone is the second node only; "dealt to target creature" alone the first.
  4. "Prevent the next 3 damage that would be dealt to any target this turn." is `scope: 'all', amount: 3, to: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' }` with `{ kind: 'anyTarget' }` as the slot (a player can be chosen: the targets decision lists players for `anyTarget` and `player` slots). "Prevent the next 1 damage that would be dealt to target creature" is the same with a creature slot. "Prevent the next 2 combat damage that would be dealt to you this turn" is `scope: 'combat', amount: 2, to: you`.
  5. "Prevent all damage that would be dealt to creatures you control this turn." is `scope: 'all', amount: 'all', to: { kind: 'each', filter: { types: ['creature'], controller: 'you' } }`. "Prevent the next 1 damage that would be dealt to each creature you control this turn." is the same with `amount: 1` (one shield per creature). "... to you and creatures you control" is a `sequence` of the `you` node and the `each` node.
  Anything that says "can't be prevented", "that source of your choice", "the next time a source of your choice would deal damage" (CR 609.7a, 615.8: a chosen source), "instead" (a replacement, CR 614, such as redirecting damage or "prevent that damage and ... instead") or a prevention shield with a duration other than this turn stays unparsed. Needs `effect:preventDamage`; a target form also needs the target selector kind it already uses.
- Notes: (1) AI: `RandomLegalAgent` casts these like any spell, with random targets. `CasualAgent` (`casual-prevention.ts`) casts only a no-target Fog or "to you" shield read from its text, only in the declare blockers step of the opponent's turn, when the unblocked and trampling combat damage aimed at it (double strike counted twice) would leave it `NEAR_LETHAL_LIFE` (5) life or less, and at most once a turn; it never casts a numeric, targeted, noncombat or creature-only shield. Burn and noncombat damage in response are not read. (2) The view carries no list of shields: `ObjectView` and `GameView` do not show that a shield exists (`docs/ROADMAP.md`).
- Parser: since stage P7a the card grammar reads shapes 1 to 5 above (`effect.prevent-all`, `effect.prevent-next`).


## The order of prevention effects on a damage event (engine slice; CR 616.1, 616.1f, 510.2, D-191)

- CR: 616.1 (when two or more replacement or prevention effects try to modify one event, the affected player chooses one to apply), 616.1e (for damage, any applicable effect may be chosen: the self-replacement, "under whose control", copy and "back face up" effects of 616.1a to 616.1d cannot modify a damage event), 616.1f (the process repeats with only the effects that are now applicable), 615.7 (a numeric shield is reduced by what it prevents and is gone at 0), 510.2 (combat damage is dealt simultaneously), 510.3 (the active player then gets priority). Read with `pnpm rule 616.1`.
- Code: `rules/damage-order.ts` (`damageModifiers(state, event)` lists what applies, in the default order; `applyModifier` applies one; `settleDamage(state, event, flow)` runs the rounds; `damageOrderMatters` is the exact search; `OrderFlow` carries the answers), `rules/damage-modifier.ts` (the types), `rules/damage-order-text.ts` (the words of an effect), `rules/prevention.ts` (the shields: `isShield`, `shieldApplies`, `staticPreventions`), `rules/damage.ts` (`dealDamageAsking` is the one damage event, `dealDamage` the default-order wrapper, `dealDamageWithExcess`), `rules/damage-effects.ts` (`dealDamage` and `dealDividedDamage` as a resolving instruction), `rules/fight.ts`, `rules/combat.ts` and `rules/combat-flow.ts` (`dealCombatDamage`, `dealBatch`, `answerCombatReplacement`), `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionReplacement`, the answers cleared when the instruction is done), `api.ts` (`answerReplacement`), `state/decisions.ts` (`ChooseOptionDecision.purpose` `replacement`, `ReplacementQuestion`, `ReplacementChoice`, `DamageResume`), `state/events.ts` (`replacementChosen`), `state/types.ts` (`ResolutionContext.damageAnswers`), `cards/fixtures/synthetic-damage-order.ts`.
- Decision and action: `chooseOption` with `purpose: 'replacement'`, asked of the affected player. `options` are the keys of the effects (`shield:<continuous effect id>`, `static:<object id>:<ability index>`) in the default order, so the first is what a fixed policy answers; `replacement.event` is the damage as it is now (source, recipient, what is left, combat or not), `replacement.choices` each effect with its words (`text`) and `outcome` (what reaches the recipient and what is prevented if it goes first and the rest follow the default order); `replacement.resume` is set for combat damage. The answer is `{ kind: 'chooseOption', option }`, refused unless it is one of the options; the event is `replacementChosen`. No cancel: damage is not undone.
- When it asks: with two or more applicable effects, only when the order can change what the player ends with. Two shields for all damage, two equal shields, a shield for all damage with a numeric one (the first leaves the numeric one whole) and shields that leave the same shields in every order (a 3 and a 2 against 3 damage) ask nothing; a 3 and a 5 against 3 damage, or a 3, a 2 and a 5 against 6, ask (the second case twice). More than six applicable effects ask without searching.
- How it pauses: nothing is dealt, and no trigger collected, before the last answer. A spell or ability that deals damage returns the question from its instruction and runs again with the answers so far; a combat damage step simulates the whole batch and carries the plan in the decision. The rider of a mana ability cannot wait and takes the default order.
- Tests: `damage-order.test.ts` (the list, the default order, the search: commuting and non-commuting cases, the cap of six, the flows), `scenario-xx-damage-order.test.ts` (a 3 and a 5 on a player ask, each answer's result, the refusal of an answer that is not an option, the equal and prevent-all cases that ask nothing, a question per round with three shields, the creature's controller asked, combat damage asked before any is dealt with the second question, a fight, excess damage, divided damage, the rider of a pain land, the same moves giving the same log and state and the log replaying), `scenario-xx-prevention.test.ts` (two equal shields: older first, nothing asked); AI: `packages/ai/test/damage-order-agents.test.ts` (every agent answers legally for a spell and for combat damage, casual and greedy take the first option, the heuristic reads the outcome, random picks among the options), `damage-order-games.test.ts` (whole games begun at the question, three starting states, every agent kind, replay); web: `apps/web/test/damage-order-render.test.tsx` and the browser test `apps/web/e2e/damage-order.e2e.ts` (the hidden SYNTHETIC deck `synthetic-damage-order`).
- Example cards: Ancient Adamantoise (below, D-192) puts a redirection among the effects; before it the question was reached by two shields of different sizes on one player or creature.
- Notes: (1) AI: the casual and greedy agents take the first option (prevent first), the heuristic agent the choice with the least damage reaching its own player or creature and then the most prevented, ties to the first; random picks among the options. (2) The web says it as "Damage replacement" with one button for each effect and "Then: N damage" under it, and logs "You apply "..." first (source)". (3) The words of an effect are what it does, not whose it is: a shield records no source.

## Damage redirection and damage that stays: Ancient Adamantoise (engine slice and parser stage P43; CR 614.9, 614.5, 616.1, 616.2, 120.3, 514.2, D-192, D-193)

- CR: 614.9 (a redirection effect replaces damage dealt to a battle, creature, planeswalker or player with the same damage dealt to another; it does nothing when a permanent involved has left the battlefield or is no longer a creature, planeswalker or battle, or a player has left), 614.1a ("instead" makes it a replacement effect), 614.5 (a replacement effect gets one opportunity to affect an event and the modified events that replace it), 616.1, 616.1e, 616.1f, 616.2 (the affected player chooses; the process repeats with what is now applicable), 120.3 (the results of damage depend on its source and its final recipient), 514.2 (damage marked on permanents is removed in the cleanup step), 704.5g and 704.5h (lethal damage, deathtouch). Read with `pnpm rule`.
- Gap class: P for the two nodes, on top of the S flow of D-191.
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Replacement` `redirectDamage`, `StaticEffect` `keepsDamage`), `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate-redirect.ts`, `rules/damage-redirect.ts` (`redirectsFor`, `redirectKey`), `rules/damage-modifier.ts` (the `redirect` modifier), `rules/damage-order.ts` (`damageModifiers(state, event, used)`, `Settling` with the recipient, `settleDamage` returning `to`), `rules/damage.ts` (`dealDamageAsking` deals to the recipient settling leaves), `rules/statics.ts` (`keepsDamage`), `rules/flow.ts` (`afterDiscard`), `state/events.ts` (`damageRedirected`), `state/decisions.ts` (`ReplacementChoice.kind` `redirect`, `outcome.toName`), `cards/fixtures/synthetic-adamantoise.ts`; parser: `rules/replacement/adamantoise.ts`, `rules/static/adamantoise.ts`, `lower/adamantoise.ts`; AI: `heuristic-replacement.ts`; web: `game/redirect-text.ts`, `game/describe.ts`, `ui/decisions/option-names.ts`.
- Shape: `{ kind: 'replacement', replacement: { kind: 'redirectDamage', you: true, permanents: { controller: 'you' } } }` and `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'keepsDamage', subject: { kind: 'this' } } }`. The redirection is read from the abilities the creature has now, for damage dealt to its controller (`you`) and to the other permanents the filter matches that are controlled by that player, each once for an event, and is one more effect of the order of D-191: the key is `redirect:<object>:<ability index>`, it comes after the prevention effects in the default order, and the affected player is asked when the order matters (a shield for a number on the player; a shield on the player and one on the creature; two creatures). The damage is then dealt by the same source to the creature (CR 120.3): marked damage, or -1/-1 counters from wither and infect, deathtouch, lifelink; no life loss, poison, commander damage or "dealt combat damage" record for the player. A shield on the creature applies after the redirection (CR 616.2).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-damage-redirect.test.ts`, `damage-order.test.ts`, `ast-redirect-validate.test.ts`, `packages/cards/test/parser-p43.test.ts`, `parser-p43-needs.test.ts`, `convert-grammar-engine-p43.test.ts`; AI: `damage-order-agents.test.ts`, `adamantoise-games.test.ts` (the `adamantoise` style); web: `redirect-text.test.ts`.
- Example cards: Ancient Adamantoise (real wording from the owner's request file of 2026-10-29, through the grammar; `SYNTHETIC Ancient Adamantoise` carries the tests, its 6/8 is invented because the file gives none).
- Cards unlocked: Ancient Adamantoise.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 45, `PARSER_VERSION` 48, 2026-11-02
- What the parser must produce: "All damage that would be dealt to you and other permanents you control is dealt to ~ instead." is the replacement above (`permanents` absent for "to you", `you: false` for "to other creatures you control"); "Damage isn't removed from ~ during cleanup steps." is the static. Both only as a whole line; the redirection only on a creature face.
- Notes: (1) Limits: a redirect to another player's creature is refused; only a creature face; the damage of a combat step is simulated one event after another, so a creature that took lethal damage in the batch is still a recipient for the rest of it. (2) No official ruling could be read (Scryfall is blocked).

## Split cards (engine slice; CR 709)

- CR: 709.1 and 709.2 (two faces, one card), 709.3 (the half is chosen before the card goes on the stack), 709.3a (only the chosen half is evaluated), 709.3b (on the stack only that half exists), 709.4 (in every other zone both halves combined), 709.4a (two names), 709.4b (the combined mana cost: the mana value is the sum, the colors are both), 709.4c (every card type and ability of either half), 601.2 (casting), 117.1a and 304.5 (timing is that of the half cast). Not: 709.5 (permanent split cards, Rooms: refused), 702.102 (Fuse) and 702.127 (Aftermath): refused.
- Gap class: S in the scaling plan's sense (a layout), built as a general rule: no card is named in the engine.
- Code: `rules/layouts.ts` (`castableFaces`, `choosesFace`, `asFace`, `combinedValues`, `hasCombinedFaces`), `rules/copiable.ts` (`printedValues`), `rules/legality.ts` (`canCast`, `castableObjects`, `canPlayLand`), `rules/cast.ts` (`beginCast`), `rules/zones.ts` (`moveObject`, `faceAfter`), `rules/priority.ts`, `api.ts` (`isOffered`), `state/actions.ts`, `state/decisions.ts`, `state/events.ts`, `view.ts`, `ast/validate-layouts.ts`, `ast/features.ts`, `cards/fixtures/synthetic-layouts.ts`
- Shape: `CardDefinition { layout: 'split', faces: [A, B] }`, each face an instant or sorcery with its own `manaCost`, `spell`, `abilities`. The card name is `A // B` (Scryfall). `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.layouts` lists `split`.
- Flow: `priorityOptions` lists, for a split card in hand (or the command zone), one `{ kind: 'castSpell', object, face }` per half that can be cast now: `canCast` evaluates the half alone (its timing, its targets, its own mana cost), so a sorcery half is not offered in the opponent's turn while the instant half is, and a half with no legal target is not offered. `beginCast` moves the card to the stack with `face` up; the spell then has only that half's name, mana cost, colors, types and text, and resolves with that half's effect. In the graveyard, exile, hand and library `derive` returns `combinedValues`: name `A // B`, the cost of both halves in order, the colors and types of both (colors in WUBRG order), the abilities of both. A countered or fizzled half goes to the graveyard like any spell.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-layout-split.test.ts` (combined characteristics in hand and the other zones, the options and their `face`, a half not offered without a target or mana, a bad face refused, the stack object being one half with its mana value, the second half, the first half's effect, the `spellCast` event's `face`, timing per half, a countered split spell, cancelling, an opponent unable to cast from your hand), `layout-validate.test.ts` (face counts, Fuse, Aftermath and Room refused), `layout-legal-actions.test.ts` (listed and accepted agree), `packages/ai/test/layout-games.test.ts` (the soak)
- Example cards: the shapes of the split instants and sorceries (Fire // Ice, Turn // Burn, Assault // Battery, Dead // Gone); none fetched in this slice and none marked supported by it. The SYNTHETIC cards `SYNTHETIC Singe // Mend` and `SYNTHETIC Study // Zap` carry the tests.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; every split card whose halves parse (without Fuse or Aftermath)
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 8 (additive: optional `face` on `castSpell` and `playLand`, `ObjectView.face`, `faces` and `layout`; no behavior change for a single-faced card), 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: both halves as `faces[0]` and `faces[1]` with `layout: 'split'` (`face.ts` already converts every face); `name` `A // B`; `colorIdentity` of both halves (CR 903.4). A split card whose text has a line starting with `Fuse` or `Aftermath`, or whose halves are permanents (Rooms), stays unsupported: `validateCard` says so by layout.
- Notes: (1) A fused split spell (CR 702.102) has no option: it needs a way to choose both halves. (2) Flashback on a split card is refused. (3) The AI casts the first affordable half of a split card that it judges worth casting; random agents choose among the options.

## Adventure cards (engine slice; CR 715)

- CR: 715.2 (the Adventure has alternative characteristics, an instant or sorcery), 715.3 (play normally or as an Adventure), 715.3a (only the alternative characteristics are evaluated to cast it), 715.3b (on the stack as an Adventure only those exist), 715.3d (a resolved Adventure is exiled instead of going to the graveyard, and its owner may play the card from exile, but not as an Adventure again), 715.4 (in every zone but the stack, and on the stack not as an Adventure, only the normal characteristics), 608.2b (a fizzled spell is not "resolved"), 701.6 (a countered spell goes to the graveyard), 903.8 and 903.9a (a commander).
- Gap class: a layout (see Split cards)
- Code: `rules/layouts.ts` (`isAdventureSpell`), `rules/stack.ts` (`finish`), `rules/zones.ts` (`MoveRequest.onAdventure`, `GameObject.onAdventure`), `rules/legality.ts` (`castZone`: exile; `castableObjects`), `rules/cast.ts`, `view.ts` (`onAdventure`), `invariants.ts`, `ast/validate-layouts.ts` (`layoutStructureIssues`), `cards/fixtures/synthetic-layouts.ts`
- Shape: `CardDefinition { layout: 'adventure', faces: [card, adventure] }`: `faces[0]` the creature (or other permanent), `faces[1]` an instant or sorcery with `subtypes: ['Adventure']` (validated: `faces[1]` must be an instant or sorcery).
- Flow: from the hand (or the command zone) `castSpell { face: 0 }` casts the card and `{ face: 1 }` the Adventure; each is offered only when that face alone can be cast now (the Adventure needs its own target and mana). The Adventure on the stack has the Adventure's name, type, cost and text. If it resolves, `stack.ts` `finish` moves it to exile with `onAdventure: true` (`MoveRequest.onAdventure`); a counter, a fizzle or any other way off the stack puts it in the graveyard, with no flag. While the flag is set `castZone` returns `'exile'` for the owner, `castSpell { face: 0 }` is offered and `face: 1` is not; casting from exile pays the card's mana cost (no commander tax: it is not the command zone). The new stack object has no flag, so the flag never matters again; any zone change drops it (CR 400.7). A commander that resolves as an Adventure is exiled and asks the owner the CR 903.9a question like any commander in exile.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-layout-adventure.test.ts` (the creature's characteristics in hand and other zones, the two options, each face judged alone, the Adventure on the stack, the exile with the flag, casting the creature from exile and not the Adventure, the whole path, a card in exile without the flag and the opponent's card not castable, a normal cast never exiling, a countered Adventure, a fizzled Adventure, a sorcery Adventure at instant speed refused, flying kept), `scenario-xx-layout-commander.test.ts` (tax by face, the exile question, the cast from exile), `layout-legal-actions.test.ts`, the soak
- Example cards: the shape of Bonecrusher Giant // Stomp, Brazen Borrower // Petty Theft, Lovestruck Beast // Heart's Desire (named from memory of their layout, UNVERIFIED; none fetched). SYNTHETIC: `SYNTHETIC Brawler Bear // Chop`, `SYNTHETIC Wise Owl // Peek`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; every Adventure card whose two faces parse
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 8, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: `layout: 'adventure'` with the creature face first and the Adventure face second; the Adventure face's `spell` from its text without the reminder "(Then exile this card. You may cast the creature later from exile.)", with `Adventure` among its subtypes (Scryfall's type line "Instant — Adventure"); `colorIdentity` of both faces. The Adventure's own kicker and costs are as for any spell.
- Notes: (1) Effects that "cast a card from exile" and let the player cast it as an Adventure (CR 715.3d's last sentence) are not built. (2) References to a card that "has an Adventure" (CR 715.2a) have no filter. (3) A copy of an Adventure spell (CR 715.3c) is not built (no copy of a spell exists). (4) The AI casts the Adventure first when its text names a target (and `worthCasting` found one), otherwise the creature; the creature later from exile as any spell.

## Modal double-faced cards (engine slice; CR 712.3, 712.8, 712.11 to 712.14)

- CR: 712.3 (two faces, usually independent), 712.8a (outside the stack and the battlefield only the front face), 712.8f (on the stack or the battlefield only the face that is up), 712.11b and 712.11c (the face to cast is chosen first and is the only one evaluated), 712.12 (a back-face land is played as a land with that face up), 712.13 (a resolving spell keeps its face), 712.14 (any other way onto the battlefield: front face up), 305.1 and 305.2 (playing a land is a land drop), 614.12 (a shock-land face asks as it enters), 400.7.
- Gap class: a layout (see Split cards)
- Code: `rules/layouts.ts`, `rules/legality.ts` (`canPlayLand(face)`, `playableLands`), `api.ts` (`playLand`, `answerConfirm`: the question keeps the face), `rules/zones.ts` (`faceAfter`), `rules/copiable.ts`, `state/decisions.ts` (`ConfirmOptionalDecision.face`), `state/events.ts` (`landPlayed.face`), `testing/*` (`PermanentOptions.face`, `playLand(card, { face })`)
- Shape: `CardDefinition { layout: 'modal_dfc', faces: [front, back] }`; either face may be any card type, a land face is played, any other is cast.
- Flow: `priorityOptions` lists `castSpell { face }` for each face that can be cast and `playLand { face }` for each land face that can be played (land drop left, main phase, empty stack, own turn). A land face is never cast (CR 305.1) and a spell face is never played. The chosen face is on the stack or enters the battlefield face up (`GameObject.face`), and `derive` gives that face's name, types, cost, power, abilities (so the back-face land taps for mana, a creature face has its keywords). In hand, graveyard, exile and library the card is its front face (`face` 0). A shock-land back face asks "pay 2 life?" as it enters; the decision carries `face`, so the answer plays the same face. A permanent that leaves the battlefield is a new object with face 0 (CR 400.7, 712.8a).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-layout-modal-dfc.test.ts` (the front face outside the stack, a land face played and a spell face cast, the front cast as a creature, the back cast as the permanent with its own keywords and cost, each face judged alone, a sorcery face's timing, the back-face land as the land drop with its mana ability, the land drop used up, the shock question and both answers, the opponent's turn refused, dying and bouncing back to the front face, an effect transforming a modal permanent, replay), `scenario-xx-layout-commander.test.ts`, `layout-legal-actions.test.ts`, the soak
- Example cards: the shape of the Zendikar Rising spell-and-land cards (Agadeem's Awakening // Agadeem, the Undercrypt) and the Kaldheim and Streets of New Capenna ones; none fetched. SYNTHETIC: `SYNTHETIC Ridge Drake // Ridge Pass`, `SYNTHETIC Zeal Charm // Shock Pass`, `SYNTHETIC Twin Knight // Twin Wall`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; every modal double-faced card whose faces parse
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 8, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: `layout: 'modal_dfc'` with both faces; each face's own `manaCost`, `types`, abilities and `spell`; `colorIdentity` of both faces (Commander: the identity of the whole card, CR 903.4).
- Notes: (1) A modal double-faced permanent can transform: the CR allows it (CR 712.3: "they may have an ability that allows them to transform or convert on either face"; 712.9 limits transforming to double-faced cards, which these are), so the `transform` effect also turns a modal double-faced permanent over (tested). No card in the supported set has such an ability. (2) Playing the front face of a land-front modal card (Pathway-style) is a `playLand { face: 0 }` option. (3) A commander that is a modal double-faced card is cast as either face from the command zone; its land face is not played from there. (4) Casting a double-faced card "transformed" (CR 712.8c, 712.11a) is not built.

## Transforming double-faced cards and the `transform` effect (engine slice; CR 712.2, 701.27, 712.18)

- CR: 712.2 (nonmodal double-faced cards), 712.8a, 712.8d and 712.8e (the face up; the back face's mana value is the front face's), 202.3b (a copy of a back face has mana value 0), 712.9 (only a double-faced card transforms; a copy that is not one does not), 712.10 and 701.27d (nothing happens when the other face is an instant or sorcery), 712.11 (cast front face up; the back is never cast), 712.13 and 712.14 (enter front face up unless cast or put "transformed"), 712.18 (the permanent is the same object: counters, attachments, effects stay), 701.27a (to transform is to turn it over), 701.27f (an ability of a permanent transforms it only if it has not transformed since the ability was put on the stack), 707.8 (a copy copies the face that is up), 712.8a and 400.7 (a permanent that leaves is the front face again).
- Gap class: P (a primitive, `transform`) plus a layout
- Code: `rules/transform.ts` (`runTransform`), `rules/layouts.ts` (`transformedFace`, `backFaceManaCost`, `isDoubleFaced`), `rules/derive.ts` (`manaCostOf`), `rules/copiable.ts` (`isCopying`), `rules/effects.ts`, `state/types.ts` (`GameObject.transformedAt`), `ast/types.ts`, `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate-parts.ts`, `ast/validate-layouts.ts`
- AST node: `{ kind: 'transform', objects: ObjectSelector }` (`effect:transform`): `this`, a `target`, `each`, `triggerSubject`, `moved`. Shape of a transforming card: `layout: 'transform'`, `faces: [front, back]`; only `faces[0]` is cast (no `face` in its options). The upkeep trigger "At the beginning of your upkeep, transform ~" is `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger: { kind: 'upkeep', whose: 'you' }, effect: { kind: 'transform', objects: { kind: 'this' } } }`; "{1}: Transform ~" is an `activated` ability with the same effect.
- Flow: `runTransform` resolves the selector, skips what is not a battlefield permanent that is a card (a token, CR 712.9), what is not a double-faced card (CR 712.9: a Clone of one) and a transformation into an instant or sorcery face (CR 712.10), skips an ability of the permanent itself that was put on the stack before it last transformed (CR 701.27f: `GameObject.transformedAt` against the ability object's timestamp), and flips `GameObject.face` between 0 and 1, setting `transformedAt` from the state's timestamp counter. `derive` then gives the other face's characteristics and abilities; the back face's mana cost is replaced by the front face's for mana value (CR 712.8e) unless the permanent is copying something. A copy effect or a token copy takes `copiableValues`, which reads the face that is up (CR 707.8), so a copy of a back face has the back face's values and mana value 0. When the permanent leaves the battlefield the new object has face 0.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-layout-transform.test.ts` (front face only castable and no face in the option, the front face on the stack and the battlefield, the back face's name, types, keywords, power and toughness, the same object with counters, damage, tapped status and its Equipment, an until-end-of-turn pump carried over, the mana value of the front face, transforming back, the back face's abilities and activated costs, the second of two abilities ignored (CR 701.27f), the upkeep trigger each turn, a single-faced creature and a token untouched, a copy that is not a double-faced card untouched, a transformed permanent that dies or is bounced being the front face, a "becomes a copy" and a token copy of a back face, mana value 0, replay), `layout-validate.test.ts`, the soak
- Example cards: the shape of the Innistrad werewolves and the Magic Origins flip walkers (Delver of Secrets // Insectile Aberration, Huntmaster of the Fells // Ravager of the Fells; day and night, meld, planeswalker back faces are not built). Names from memory, UNVERIFIED; none fetched. SYNTHETIC: `SYNTHETIC Moon Cub // Moon Beast`, `SYNTHETIC Squire // Grown Knight`, `SYNTHETIC Turn Over`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; transforming cards whose abilities parse and whose triggers need only the upkeep, end-step or activated shapes
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 8, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: `layout: 'transform'` with both faces; the back face has no mana cost and its color from the color indicator (`colors`); `Transform ~` (and "transform this creature", "transform it", "transform target creature") as `{ kind: 'transform', objects: ... }` with `this`, `target` or `triggerSubject`; a trigger "At the beginning of your upkeep, if ..., transform ~" needs the condition (day and night, "no spells were cast last turn") which the engine does not have. "Daybound" and "nightbound" (CR 702.145) are not supported.
- Notes: (1) A transformed permanent keeps its `timestamp` (the engine also uses it for summoning sickness), so CR 613.7g is not applied (`docs/ROADMAP.md`). (2) No `transformed` event: a trigger "whenever this transforms" (CR 701.27e) is not built. (3) Entering the battlefield transformed and casting "transformed" (CR 712.14a, 712.8c) are not built. (4) A token that is a copy of a double-faced permanent is a single-faced token with the face's values (CR 707.8a would make it double-faced). (5) A transform layout whose back face is a planeswalker or battle is refused (it would need counters as it transforms).

## Saga cards (engine slice; CR 714, 704.5s)

- CR: 714.1 (a Saga is an enchantment with chapter symbols), 714.2a (a chapter symbol includes a Roman numeral), 714.2b ("{rN}-[Effect]": when one or more lore counters are put onto the Saga, if their number was less than N and became at least N), 714.2c ("{rN1}, {rN2}-[Effect]" is two abilities), 714.2d and 714.2e (the final chapter number and ability), 714.3a (a Saga enters with a lore counter, a replacement effect), 714.3c (as a player's precombat main phase begins, that player puts a lore counter on each Saga they control with chapter abilities; a turn-based action, no stack), 714.4 and 704.5s (a Saga with at least its final chapter number of lore counters that is not the source of a chapter ability that has triggered and not left the stack is sacrificed by its controller), 122 (counters), 400.7 (a Saga that leaves and returns is a new object), 113.7a (an ability on the stack resolves without its source). Rule numbers are those of `docs/rules/comprehensive-rules.txt` of this repository (checked with `pnpm rule`); 714.3 holds the enters-with-counter and precombat-main rules and 714.2b the trigger.
- Gap class: K (the chapter keyword abilities) plus a layout, a trigger kind and one turn-based action; no new primitive
- Code: `rules/saga.ts` (`LORE`, `isSaga`, `finalChapterOf`, `addLoreCounters`, `loreChange`, `chaptersReached`, `sagaIsDone`), `rules/enters.ts` (`entersAsSaga`), `rules/zones.ts` and `rules/tokens.ts` (the entering counter), `rules/flow.ts` (the turn-based counter at the precombat main step), `rules/triggers.ts` (the `chapter` trigger from `counterAdded` events), `rules/trigger-stack.ts` and `rules/ability-object.ts` (the chapter number carried to the stack), `rules/sba.ts` (the sacrifice), `ast/types.ts` (`Trigger` `chapter`), `cards/definition.ts` (`CardFace.finalChapter`), `ast/validate.ts`, `ast/validate-layouts.ts`, `ast/supported.ts`, `ast/features.ts`, `view.ts` (`ObjectView.finalChapter` and `chapter`), `state/types.ts` (`PendingTrigger.chapter`, `StackData.chapter`), `state/events.ts` (`abilityTriggered.chapter`)
