- AST node: `layout: 'saga'`, one face with `types: ['enchantment']`, `subtypes: ['Saga']` (plus other types for a Saga creature), `finalChapter: <n>` on the face, and one triggered ability per chapter line: `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger: { kind: 'chapter', from: 1, to: 1 }, effect }` for "I", `from: 1, to: 2` for "I, II", `from: 3, to: 3` for "III". Every chapter from 1 to `finalChapter` has exactly one ability. Other abilities (a keyword, a static ability) sit next to them in `abilities`. A Saga's enters-with-a-lore-counter is not written: the engine adds it for every permanent with the subtype Saga.
- Flow: the spell resolves and `moveObject` makes the permanent with `counters.lore = 1` and emits `zoneChanged` then `counterAdded`; `findTriggers` reads the `counterAdded` (`loreChange` recovers the count before and after from the state's total and the later events of the batch) and makes a pending trigger for each chapter ability whose range holds a number in (before, after], with `chapter` set to that number. At the start of its controller's precombat main step `beginStep` puts a counter on each Saga they control that has a chapter ability and passes the events to `grantPriority`, which scans them like any other. The chapter ability goes on the stack above everything; the state-based action check sacrifices the Saga when its counters are at least `finalChapterOf(abilities)` and no chapter ability of it is pending or on the stack (checked on `pendingTriggers` and on the `StackData.source` of stack abilities), so it stays while its last chapter is on the stack, through every response, and goes when that ability has resolved, been countered or removed. The ability resolves without its source (CR 113.7a), so a Saga destroyed in response still gives its chapter's effect.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-sagas.test.ts` (enters with one counter and chapter I at once, the stack object and the view saying which chapter, no second counter in the entering main phase, the precombat counter with no stack, chapters II and III on the next turns and the sacrifice after the last, all three chapters over three turns, a Saga advancing only on its controller's turn, two Sagas of one player ordered by the player, "I, II", a final chapter that makes a creature, a Saga sacrificed at the first check when nothing waits, a response that does not make it go early, a Saga without chapter abilities never sacrificed), `scenario-xx-sagas-effects.test.ts` (a lore counter from an effect, two counters reaching two chapters with the ordering decision, one "I, II" ability triggered twice, a Saga destroyed before the last chapter, destroyed with its last chapter on the stack, flickered back as a new object with one counter, a token copy, legal plays against `applyAction`, replay), `saga-rules.test.ts` (final chapter, chapters reached, the before and after counts of a batch), `ast-saga-validate.test.ts`, the soak `packages/ai/test/saga-games.test.ts` (100 games with the `'sagas'` deck style, replayed by hash). Two behaviours were mutation-checked (dropping the precombat counter, and letting the sacrifice ignore a waiting chapter ability): each makes several of those scenario tests fail.
- Example cards: no real card is used in a test; the shapes are a three-chapter Saga whose last chapter makes a creature, and an "I, II" Saga. The fixtures are SYNTHETIC: `SYNTHETIC Rite of Three`, `SYNTHETIC Echo Verse` ("I, II"), `SYNTHETIC Rising Golem` (a Golem token on chapter III), `SYNTHETIC Brief Tale`, and the instants `SYNTHETIC Lore Surge`, `SYNTHETIC Lore Flood`, `SYNTHETIC Saga Breaker`, `SYNTHETIC Saga Flicker`, `SYNTHETIC Saga Mirror`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; Sagas whose chapters are effects the grammar already reads (life, cards, tokens, counters, pumps, sacrifice, destroy and exile without a target). A chapter that targets stays unsupported (CR 603.3d, `docs/ROADMAP.md`); read ahead is an unsupported keyword.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 8 (additive optional fields only), 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: `layout: 'saga'` with one face; the face's type line gives `types: ['enchantment']` and `subtypes: ['Saga']`; `finalChapter` is the greatest chapter number. The chapter lines of Scryfall's `oracle_text` are `I — <effect>`, `II — <effect>`, `I, II — <effect>`, `III — <effect>`: a Roman numeral (or several, separated by a comma and a space, or "and") followed by an em dash (U+2014) at the start of a line. The reminder text in parentheses ("(As this Saga enters and after your draw step, add a lore counter. Sacrifice after III.)") is stripped as for any reminder text. Each line lowers to `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger: { kind: 'chapter', from, to }, effect }` with `from` and `to` the least and greatest numeral of the line (the engine reads a range as every number between them, so a list that skips a number, such as "I, III", is not expressible); the effect is the rest of the line, parsed as the effect of an ordinary triggered ability, "this Saga" being `this`. A numeral list that is not consecutive, a chapter number that repeats, a gap, or an effect the parser does not read makes the whole card unsupported (all of its text dropped, `finalChapter` may still be set). A Saga creature's other text (a line without a numeral) is an ordinary ability. Keywords before the chapters ("Read ahead") are unsupported.
- Notes: (1) The turn-based counter is put on every Saga the active player controls that has at least one chapter ability; a Saga at its final chapter has been sacrificed by the state-based actions before the next precombat main phase, unless its last chapter is still waiting, which cannot last a turn. (2) A chapter ability does not read the chapter that triggered it from the effect: there is no reference for it; `chapter` is for display only (`ObjectView.chapter`, `abilityTriggered.chapter`). (3) Several chapters reached at once (an effect adds two counters) are separate pending triggers; a player orders different abilities, and the same "I, II" ability reached twice is one group of identical triggers (A18). (4) No `lore counter` event kind: `counterAdded` and `counterRemoved` carry the counter name. (5) A permanent that becomes a Saga after it entered (a type-changing effect) gets no lore counter; no supported card can do it.

## Targets for triggered abilities (engine slice; CR 603.3d)

- CR: 603.3d (the rest of putting a triggered ability on the stack is the process of casting a spell, rules 601.2c-d; if a choice is required but no legal choice can be made, the ability is simply removed from the stack), 115.1d (a triggered ability is targeted if it uses the phrase "target"; the targets are chosen as it is put on the stack), 601.2c (targets are announced, "up to" and variable numbers, the same target not twice for one instance of "target", the chosen objects become targets and abilities that trigger from that wait until the process is done), 603.3b (abilities that trigger during the process of putting triggers on the stack go on the stack afterwards, in the next round; each player in APNAP order, in the order they choose), 603.3a (the controller is who controlled the source as it triggered), 608.2b (targets are checked again on resolution; all illegal means the ability does not resolve, some means it resolves for the rest), 603.4 (intervening "if", checked as it triggers and as it resolves), 603.5 ("you may" is asked on resolution), 603.10a and 113.7a (a dies trigger and an ability whose source left: last-known information), 714.2 (a chapter ability is a triggered ability), 702.21a (Ward). Rule numbers are those of `docs/rules/comprehensive-rules.txt` (checked with `pnpm rule`).
- Gap class: S (a subsystem: the target choice of the casting transaction made available to a second kind of object), small enough to be built without a proposal because D-089 decided it
- Code: `rules/trigger-stack.ts` (`putTriggersOnStack`, `runQueue`, `putSeatTriggers`, `answerTriggerOrder`, `answerTriggerTargets`, `targetsChosen`, `endRound`), `rules/target-choice.ts` (`targetChoiceSpecs`, `targetAnswerError`, shared with `rules/cast.ts` and `rules/activate.ts`), `rules/becomes-target.ts` (`becameTargetEvents`), `rules/ability-object.ts` (`AbilityObjectRequest.targets`), `rules/saga.ts` (a chapter ability waiting in the queue counts as waiting), `api.ts` (`answerTriggerTargetChoice`, `afterPutting`; `chooseTargets` goes to the trigger path when `GameState.triggering` is set, `cancel` is refused there, concede clears it), `legal-actions.ts` (no `cancel` for `forTrigger`), `ast/supported.ts` (the refusal is gone), `state/types.ts` (`TriggeringContext`, `GameState.triggering`, `PendingTrigger.deferred`), `state/decisions.ts` (`TargetsDecision.forTrigger`), `state/events.ts` (`abilityRemoved`), `view.ts` (`ObjectView.ability`), `testing/` (`Actor.chooseTargets`), `version.ts` (`ENGINE_VERSION` 9); AI: `packages/ai/src/casual-triggers.ts`
- AST node: none new. A triggered ability may carry `targets: TargetSpec[]` (ids `t1`, `t2`, ...; `min` and `max` for "up to one": `min: 0, max: 1`) and its effect names them with `{ kind: 'target', id }` as for a spell or an activated ability. "Up to one" with `min: 0` is never removed for lack of a target.
- Flow: `putTriggersOnStack` goes through the seats in APNAP order; a seat with several different triggers is asked `orderTriggers` first (CR 603.3b); then `putSeatTriggers` takes that seat's triggers off `pendingTriggers`, sets `GameState.triggering = { seat, queue, awaiting: null }` and runs `runQueue`. For each trigger: no `targets` pushes it (`abilityTriggered`); with `targets` the legal targets of every slot are computed for the trigger's controller with the source as it triggered; a slot with fewer legal targets than its `min` emits `abilityRemoved { seat, source, abilityIndex, reason: 'noLegalTargets', chapter? }` and goes on to the next trigger (the ability never becomes an object); when every slot has no legal target (and may stay empty) or exactly as many legal targets as it requires, the engine pushes it with those targets chosen and emits `becameTarget` for each object; otherwise it pushes it, sets `awaiting` to the new stack object and returns a `targets` decision (`seat` the controller, `source` the stack object, `forTrigger: true`). The answer (`answerTriggerTargets`) is checked like a spell's (`targetAnswerError`), stored on the stack object, and the `becameTarget` events are made; what they trigger (Ward) is noted as `deferred` pending triggers and waits for the next round; the queue is resumed, then the other seats, then `endRound` clears the flags and priority is given (`settle` then puts the deferred triggers on the stack, CR 117.5). On resolution `resolveTop` checks the targets again (CR 608.2b): a trigger whose only target is gone emits `fizzled` and does nothing, one with two slots and one illegal target resolves for the other.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-triggered-targets.test.ts` (an enters trigger asks its controller with the ability's own stack object as the source and no cancel, the state while it waits, the events, the effect; an illegal target refused; a single legal target and "target opponent" chosen without a question; no legal target removes the ability with the event and nothing on the stack; "up to one" with no artifact not removed and with one asked with the empty choice listed; a dies trigger of a creature that left asked of its last-known controller, and removed with no target; a trigger whose only target died in response fizzles, two slots with one illegal resolve for the other; "you may" with a target; an intervening "if" with a target, not triggered without it and `fizzled { reason: 'interveningIf' }` when it stops holding; legal actions agree with `applyAction`, replay and hash), `scenario-xx-triggered-targets-flow.test.ts` (two targeted triggers ordered then each asked in turn, both choices forced after the order question, several removed, both seats asked in APNAP order, Ward on a trigger's target for each way the controller answers and a waiting trigger of the other seat that goes on before the ward trigger, Saga chapters I and II asked and chapter I removed, answers of the wrong shape refused, concede while a trigger waits), `ast-triggers-validate.test.ts`, `ast-saga-validate.test.ts`, the AI `casual-trigger-targets.test.ts` and the soak `packages/ai/test/trigger-target-games.test.ts` (100 games with the `'triggerTargets'` deck style, replayed by hash). Three behaviours were mutation-checked: skipping the removal (five scenario tests fail), dropping the `becameTarget` events (nine fail), and not deferring what the targets trigger (the test with a waiting trigger of the other seat fails): each is caught.
- Example cards: no real card is used for the trigger; the shapes are "When this creature enters, destroy target creature an opponent controls", the same on a dies trigger, two slots (a counter on one's own creature and damage to an opponent's), "up to one target artifact", "target opponent loses 2 life", "you may destroy target creature ...", "whenever this creature attacks, it deals 1 damage to any target" and a Saga whose chapters I and II target. The fixtures are SYNTHETIC (`cards/fixtures/synthetic-trigger-targets.ts`): `SYNTHETIC Reaper Imp`, `Last Gasp Imp`, `Twin Spark Imp`, `Tinker Imp`, `Drain Imp`, `Eager Imp`, `Pinger Imp`, `Mentor Imp`, `Bounce Imp`, `Careful Imp`, `Ember Watcher`, `Growth Totem`, `Purge Saga`.
- Cards unlocked: every triggered ability, chapter ability included, whose targets are object or player slots the engine already runs (the effect has to be supported as for any ability): ETB and dies "destroy/exile/damage/bounce target ...", "exile target player's graveyard" shapes the grammar already reads, most real Sagas whose chapters destroy, exile, return or put counters on a target. Modes (603.3c) and "any number of target ..." stay unsupported.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 9 (`GameState.triggering` is new state), 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: no new node. A triggered ability line "When/Whenever [event], [effect with target ...]" lowers as before to `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger, targets: [{ id: 't1', what: ... }, ...], effect }` with the effect naming the slots by `{ kind: 'target', id }` (`validateCard` checks that every id the effect references is declared on the ability; a declared slot no effect uses is not an error, but the player would still be asked for it); "up to one target X" is `min: 0, max: 1`; "target creature an opponent controls" is `controller: 'opponent'` in the object filter; "target opponent" is `{ kind: 'player', filter: 'opponent' }`. For a chapter ability the same `targets` sit on the `chapter` triggered ability. `PARSER_VERSION` must be bumped by the cards agent because cards change status (D-089); the parser needs no new rule.
- Notes: (1) The question comes after the ordering question and one at a time, in the order the player chose (the first named is put on the stack first and asked first). (2) A decision of a trigger has no `cancel`; `legalActionsForDecision` leaves it out for `forTrigger`, and `applyAction` refuses it. (3) `abilityTriggered` is emitted when the ability goes on the stack, before its targets are chosen, and `becameTarget` after the answer; for a spell `becameTarget` comes first. (4) The ability's controller, not the active player, is asked; a seat that is not the deciding one has no legal actions. (5) A player never "becomes the target" of any event; `becameTarget` is for objects only. (6) `ObjectView.ability` carries the ability's AST for any ability on the stack, which `CasualAgent` reads to tell a harmful trigger (aimed at the opponent's best creature, damage at a creature only if it kills it) from a helpful one (a counter or a pump, on its own best creature) and to leave an "up to" slot empty when only its own object is legal.

## Scry (engine slice; CR 701.22)

- CR: 701.22a (look at the top N cards, put any number on the bottom in any order and the rest on top in any order), 701.22b (scry 0 is no scry event), 701.22d (an ability that triggers on scrying triggers even if the actions were impossible), 401.2 (nobody may know the order of a library), 400.7 (not applied: a scried card does not change zone). Rule numbers are those of `docs/rules/comprehensive-rules.txt` (checked with `pnpm rule 701.22`).
- Gap class: S-adjacent (a new decision kind, `arrangeCards`, decided by D-091 and refined by D-093)
- Code: `rules/arrange.ts` (`runArrange`, `isArrangement`), `rules/arrange-choices.ts` (`arrangementActions`, the answers `legalActionsForDecision` lists), `rules/effects.ts` (the `scry` case queues an `arrange` instruction per player), `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionArrange`), `api.ts` (the `arrangeCards` branch of `dispatch`), `legal-actions.ts`, `state/decisions.ts` (`ArrangeCardsDecision`), `state/actions.ts` (the `arrangeCards` action), `state/events.ts` (`scried`), `state/types.ts` (the `arrange` instruction), `view.ts` (`pendingCards`, `waitingOn`), `testing/` (`Actor.arrange`), `ast/types.ts`, `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate-parts.ts`, `version.ts` (`ENGINE_VERSION` 10); AI: `packages/ai/src/casual-library.ts`, `random-agent.ts`
- AST node: `Effect` `{ kind: 'scry', player?: PlayerSelector, count: Amount }`. `player` is left out or `{ kind: 'you' }`; any other selector is refused by `validateCard` (another player's scry is not built). `count` is a number (or `x` where the spell has an X). `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.effectKinds` lists `scry`; `FeatureRef` `effect:scry`.
- Decision and action: `ArrangeCardsDecision { kind: 'arrangeCards', seat, source, purpose: 'scry', cards, piles: ['top', 'bottom'] }` where `cards` are the top N cards of the library, top first (fewer when the library is smaller); the answer is `{ kind: 'arrangeCards', top, other }`: `top[0]` becomes the top card, `other` goes to the bottom in this order (the last becomes the bottom card). Every card must be placed exactly once; a card twice, a card missing or a card that was not looked at is refused with the decision still pending.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-scry-surveil.test.ts` (the decision and its piles; one card on top and one at the bottom with the library order checked and no zone change; both on top in the order chosen; all to the bottom in the order chosen; scry 3 with orders in both piles; "player: you" written out; a one-card library; scry 0 with no question and no event; an empty library with the event and no question; "When this creature enters, scry 2" as a trigger; the legal actions all accepted by `applyAction` for 3 and 5 cards; same answers give the same hash, a replayed log reproduces the state), `scenario-xx-arrange-hidden.test.ts` (what the other seat's view shows, the answers that do not place every card once, the wrong seat, the wrong action kind), `ast-arrange-validate.test.ts`; AI: `library-handlers.test.ts`, `random-agent.test.ts` (the crafted decision), the soak `library-games.test.ts` (100 games with the `'library'` deck style, replayed by hash, the other seat's view checked at every arrangement). Behaviours mutation-checked: reversing the order of the cards sent to the bottom (two scenario tests fail), showing `pendingCards` to the other seat (three fail), dropping the check that a card is placed once (two fail).
- Example cards: the shape "When ~ enters, scry 1." (68 cards in the owner's backlog, D-091) and "Scry 2." as a spell. The fixtures are SYNTHETIC (`cards/fixtures/synthetic-library.ts`): `SYNTHETIC Scry Two`, `Scry Three`, `Scry Five`, `Scry One You`, `Scry Zero`, and the creature `SYNTHETIC Seer Of The Door` ("When this creature enters, scry 2.").
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; every card whose scry is the controller's and whose count is a number, as a spell, an enters or attack trigger or an activated ability, once the parser reads it.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 10, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Scry N." (also "scry N" after a comma or "then", "you scry N") lowers to `{ kind: 'scry', count: N }` (leave `player` out, or `{ kind: 'you' }`); N is a digit, or a number word ("scry two") read by the amount rule. Reminder text in parentheses ("(Look at the top card of your library. You may put that card on the bottom.)") is stripped as for any reminder text. "Target player scries N", "each opponent scries N" and "that player scries" are not lowered (validation refuses them). A triggered ability whose trigger is "whenever you scry" is unsupported (no trigger kind).
- Notes: (1) A scry moves no card between zones: the library list is rewritten (`[...top, ...rest, ...other]`), so the cards keep their ids and nothing triggers on a zone change. (2) The event `scried { seat, top, bottom }` has counts only, so the log of the other seat names no card; it is emitted after the cards were arranged, also with both counts 0 for an empty library (CR 701.22d), never for scry 0. (3) Hidden information: the deciding seat's view has `pendingCards` (the cards looked at, top first) and the decision itself; every other seat has `waitingOn { seat, purpose: 'scry', sourceName }` and no card or id. (4) `legalActionsForDecision` lists every arrangement for up to four cards looked at (identity first, all to the bottom second) and six representative ones beyond; the engine accepts every valid arrangement. (5) `RandomLegalAgent` picks one of the listed arrangements. `CasualAgent` keeps a land on top when it has fewer than four lands in play and none in hand (two lands when fewer than two are in play), keeps spells whose mana value is at most its lands plus one, in order of mana value with lands first when lands are wanted, and bottoms the rest.

## Surveil (engine slice; CR 701.25)

- CR: 701.25a (look at the top N cards, put any number into the graveyard and the rest on top in any order), 701.25c (surveil 0 is no surveil event), 701.25d (an ability that triggers on surveilling triggers even if the actions were impossible), 400.7 (a card put into the graveyard is a new object), 401.2. The brief and D-091 cite 701.25b for the no-event rule; in the CR text of this repository 701.25b is the rule about additional cards and 701.25c is surveil 0 (D-093).
- Gap class: S-adjacent, the same decision as scry with a different second pile
- Code: as for scry; `rules/arrange.ts` `runArrange` moves the cards of `other` to the graveyard with `moveObject` after emitting `surveiled`; the `surveil` case of `rules/effects.ts`; `state/events.ts` (`surveiled`)
- AST node: `Effect` `{ kind: 'surveil', player?: PlayerSelector, count: Amount }`, validated like `scry`. `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.effectKinds` lists `surveil`.
- Decision and action: `ArrangeCardsDecision` with `purpose: 'surveil'` and `piles: ['top', 'graveyard']`; the answer `{ kind: 'arrangeCards', top, other }` puts `top` back in order (the first is the top card) and `other` into the graveyard in this order.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-scry-surveil.test.ts` (one card on top and one into the graveyard, with the new object in the graveyard and the `zoneChanged` event after `surveiled`; surveil 3 with the order of the cards kept; every card into the graveyard in the order chosen; a creature card surveilled is in the graveyard and nothing "died"; surveil 0; an empty library; a one-card library; "When this creature enters, surveil 2."; legal actions with the all-graveyard answer), `scenario-xx-arrange-hidden.test.ts` (a surveil is hidden the same way; a surveiled card is public once in the graveyard), `ast-arrange-validate.test.ts`; AI: `library-handlers.test.ts`, `library-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: the shape "Surveil 1." as a spell and "When ~ enters, surveil 1." (46 cards in the owner's backlog, D-091). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Surveil Two`, `Surveil Three`, `Surveil Zero`, and the creature `SYNTHETIC Grave Watcher`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; the same shapes as scry.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 10, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Surveil N." lowers to `{ kind: 'surveil', count: N }` (no `player`, or `you`); reminder text ("(Look at the top card of your library. You may put that card into your graveyard.)") is stripped. "Whenever you surveil" is unsupported.
- Notes: (1) The cards that stay on top keep their ids; the others become new objects in the graveyard, so the ids in the answer are not the ids in the graveyard afterwards (the `zoneChanged` events give both). (2) The graveyard cards are public through those events and the graveyard in every view; the cards that stay on top are not. (3) A surveilled commander in the library goes to the graveyard and is watched by the state-based action of CR 903.9a like any commander there (the commander replacement of CR 903.9b applies to the hand and the library only, so there is no question here). (4) The AI discards (into the graveyard) the cards it would bottom on a scry.

## Look at the top N cards and put them back in any order (engine slice)

- CR: no keyword action: the effect is "look at the top N cards of your library, then put them back in any order" (CR 401.2, the library is hidden and its order is known to no one; looking is not a zone change). It is not a scry (CR 701.22), so no scry event occurs and "whenever you scry" abilities do not trigger.
- Gap class: S-adjacent, the third purpose of `arrangeCards`
- Code: `rules/arrange.ts` (`runArrange` with `purpose: 'reorder'`), the `lookAndReorder` case of `rules/effects.ts`; the decision, action and view code of scry
- AST node: `Effect` `{ kind: 'lookAndReorder', count: Amount }` (always the controller's library). `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.effectKinds` lists `lookAndReorder`.
- Decision and action: `ArrangeCardsDecision` with `purpose: 'reorder'` and `piles: ['top']`; the answer `{ kind: 'arrangeCards', top, other: [] }` lists every card, the first becoming the top card; a non-empty `other` is refused. With one card, or none, nothing is asked and nothing happens; with fewer cards than N the cards there are rearranged.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-reorder-put-on-top.test.ts` (the decision and its single pile; the order chosen is the order from the top and the rest of the library is untouched; no `scried` event and no zone change; fewer cards than asked; one card asks nothing; an empty library; every order listed and accepted and a second pile refused), `scenario-xx-arrange-hidden.test.ts` (hidden from the other seat), `ast-arrange-validate.test.ts`; AI: `library-handlers.test.ts` (the wanted card first), `library-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: the template "Look at the top three cards of your library, then put them back in any order." as the first sentence of a spell whose later sentences (a shuffle, a draw) are other effects. SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Reorder Three`, `SYNTHETIC Reorder One`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; a card is unlocked only when every other sentence of it is supported too.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 10, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: the sentence "Look at the top N cards of your library, then put them back in any order." (N a number word or digit; "Look at the top card of your library" is not this sentence) lowers to `{ kind: 'lookAndReorder', count: N }`. A following sentence such as "You may shuffle." or "Draw a card." is a separate effect in the same `sequence`; an optional shuffle ("You may shuffle") has no node yet and makes the card unsupported.
- Notes: (1) The library is rewritten as `[...top, ...rest]`; no event is emitted. (2) The same hidden-information rules as scry apply (`waitingOn.purpose: 'reorder'`).

## Put N cards from your hand on top of your library (engine slice)

- CR: 401.2 (the library is hidden), 400.7 (a card moved from the hand to the library is a new object), 903.9b (a commander put into the library may go to the command zone instead), 608.2d (the player makes the choices as the effect resolves).
- Gap class: P (a primitive over the existing `chooseCards` decision)
- Code: `rules/arrange.ts` (`runPutOnTop`), the `putFromHandOnTop` case of `rules/effects.ts`, `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionCards` also answers a `putOnTop` instruction), `rules/arrange-choices.ts` (`orderedChoices`), `legal-actions.ts` (ordered choices for `purpose: 'putOnTop'`), `state/decisions.ts` (`ChooseCardsDecision.purpose` has `'putOnTop'`); AI: `casual-library.ts` (`choosePutOnTop`)
- AST node: `Effect` `{ kind: 'putFromHandOnTop', count: Amount }`: the controller's hand and library. `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.effectKinds` lists `putFromHandOnTop`.
- Decision and action: `ChooseCardsDecision { purpose: 'putOnTop', zone: 'hand', legal: <the hand>, min = max = min(count, hand size), reveal: false, prompt }`; the answer is `{ kind: 'chooseCards', objects }` in the order from the top (the first card ends up on top). The decision is skipped for an empty hand and for a hand of exactly one card (the card goes on top); with a hand of two or more it is asked even when the hand holds no more than N cards, because the order is a choice (D-093).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-reorder-put-on-top.test.ts` (draw three then put two: the decision with its prompt and hidden cards; the first card chosen on top and the second under it, the new objects and the `zoneChanged` events from the hand; the other order; answers with one card, three, a card twice or a library card refused; every ordered pair listed and accepted; a one-card hand, an empty hand and exactly two cards; "put a card from your hand on top"; a commander put on top asking its owner, accepted into the command zone and declined), `ast-arrange-validate.test.ts`; AI: `library-handlers.test.ts` (spare lands and uncastable spells go back), `library-games.test.ts`. One behaviour was mutation-checked: queueing the moves in the order chosen instead of last-first (four scenario tests fail).
- Example cards: Brainstorm ("Draw three cards, then put two cards from your hand on top of your library in any order."). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Brainstorm Like`, `Put Back One`, `Put Back Two`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; Brainstorm once the parser reads the sentence.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 10, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Draw three cards, then put two cards from your hand on top of your library in any order." lowers to a `sequence` of `{ kind: 'draw', player: { kind: 'you' }, count: 3 }` and `{ kind: 'putFromHandOnTop', count: 2 }`; "Put a card from your hand on top of your library." is `{ kind: 'putFromHandOnTop', count: 1 }` (the "in any order" is only said for two or more). "Put N cards from your hand on the bottom" and "from your graveyard on top" are other effects (not built).
- Notes: (1) The cards are moved by ordinary `move` instructions to the top of the library, the last chosen first, so the first chosen is on top, and a commander among them asks its owner before it moves (CR 903.9b). (2) The decision is not revealed: the other seat's view has `waitingOn { purpose: 'putOnTop' }` and no card; the events `zoneChanged` from the hand to the library carry object ids (not names). (3) `CasualAgent` puts back the cards it values least (spare lands once it has six in play, spells it cannot cast soon) and orders them with the one it would most like to draw again first.

## Amounts read from a target: power and mana value (engine slice; CR 608.2h, 202.3)

- CR: 608.2h (an effect that needs information from a specific object uses its current information while it is in the public zone it was expected to be in, and its last-known information once it left or the effect moved it there), 113.7a (last-known information), 202.3 (mana value is the total of the mana cost; 202.3a no cost is 0; 202.3e an {X} is 0 off the stack; 202.3f hybrid counts its largest part; 202.3g a Phyrexian symbol counts 1), 400.7 (a card that moved is a new object, so the old id reads from last-known information), 608.2b (a spell whose targets are all illegal does not resolve, so it never reads a target that is gone), 119.1 (life).
- Gap class: P (a primitive: the amount interpreter now sees the resolving object's targets)
- Code: `rules/effect-support.ts` (`amountOf(state, amount, ctx, targets)`; every caller in `rules/effects.ts` passes the still-legal targets), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkAmount`), `ast/types.ts` (`Amount`), `ast/features.ts` (`amount:manaValueOf`)
- AST node: `Amount` `{ kind: 'powerOf', of: ObjectRef }` and `{ kind: 'manaValueOf', of: ObjectRef }`; `of` is `this`, `thisSpell`, a declared `target` or `moved`. `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.amountKinds` lists `number`, `powerOf` and `manaValueOf`. Before this slice `powerOf` read 0 for a target (`amountOf` passed no targets).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-target-amounts.test.ts` (exile a creature and its controller gains life equal to its power: plain, with +1/+1 counters, after a -2/-2 pump, for a creature controlled by the other player than its owner; the target gone in response: the spell fizzles and nothing is gained; a power and a mana value read from a creature that stays; reanimation losing life equal to the card's mana value, a cheaper card, the card exiled in response; a countered spell's controller losing life equal to its mana value), `ast-references-validate.test.ts`. One behaviour was mutation-checked (see the slice report): reading the amount with no targets again.
- Example cards: Swords to Plowshares ("Exile target creature. Its controller gains life equal to its power."), reanimation spells that cost life ("You lose life equal to that card's mana value."). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Exile Gain Power`, `Weigh Creature`, `Reanimate Pay Life`, `Costly Counter` (`src/cards/fixtures/synthetic-references.ts`).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; each card needs the parser to read its text (stage P10).
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 11, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Its controller gains life equal to its power." after "Exile target creature." lowers to `{ kind: 'gainLife', player: { kind: 'controllerOf', of: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } }, amount: { kind: 'powerOf', of: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } } }` in a `sequence` after the `exile` of the same target ("its" and "its controller" mean the target the clause before named). "You lose life equal to that card's mana value." after "Return target creature card from your graveyard to the battlefield." lowers to `{ kind: 'loseLife', player: { kind: 'you' }, amount: { kind: 'manaValueOf', of: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } } }` ("that card" is the target; `{ kind: 'moved' }` reads the new object on the battlefield, whose mana value is the same for a card, 0 for a token). The `FeatureRef`s are `amount:powerOf` and `amount:manaValueOf`.
- Notes: (1) The target is read from `ResolutionContext.legalTargets`, the targets still legal when resolution began (CR 608.2b); a target that an earlier instruction of the same spell moved is read from `GameState.lki` (recorded just before it moved: derived power with counters and effects, derived mana cost). (2) A creature with negative power gains or loses 0. (3) An {X} counts as 0 and a split card uses its printed cost: see `docs/ROADMAP.md`.

## Effects performed by another player: tokens, searches and shuffles (engine slice; CR 111.2, 701.23a, 608.2d)

- CR: 111.2 (the player who creates a token is its owner, and it enters under their control), 701.23a (to search is to look at all cards in the zone and find a card that matches), 701.23b (with a stated quality the player is not required to find any), 608.2d (a player offered an option as the effect resolves chooses whether to do it; they announce the choices), 110.2a (an object the effect instructs a player to put onto the battlefield enters under that player's control), 701.24 (shuffle), 401.2 (a library is hidden), 608.2h and 113.7a (the controller of a gone target is read from last-known information).
- Gap class: P (primitives: the performing player is a field of the effect and of the instruction)
- Code: `rules/effects.ts` (`createToken` resolves `controller` with `resolvePlayers`; `search` makes one instruction per seat the selector names; `shuffle` uses `shuffleLibrary`), `rules/search.ts` (`runSearch` asks `instruction.seat`; the "may" question), `rules/effect-support.ts` (`shuffleLibrary`), `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionSearchMay`), `api.ts` (`answerConfirm` routes purpose `search`), `state/types.ts` (the `search` instruction has `seat` and `accepted`; a `shuffle { seat }` instruction), `state/decisions.ts` (`ConfirmOptionalDecision.purpose` has `'search'`), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkSearch`, `createToken`), AI: `casual-permanents.ts` (`confirmOptional`)
- AST nodes: `Effect` `{ kind: 'createToken', token, count, controller?: PlayerSelector }` (default: the effect's controller); `{ kind: 'search', player: PlayerSelector, optional?: true, filter, max, destinations, reveal, shuffle }` (any selector: `you`, `controllerOf`, `ownerOf`, `target`, `eachOpponent`, `eachPlayer`); `{ kind: 'shuffle', player: PlayerSelector }` (already any selector). `optional: true` is "may": the searching player is asked first and a decline does the whole search, the put and the shuffle not at all.
- Decision and action: the `confirmOptional` of a "may" search has `purpose: 'search'`, `seat` the player who would search (not always the controller of the spell or ability) and `source` the spell or ability resolving; the answer is `acceptOptional` or `declineOptional` from that seat. The `chooseCards` decision (`purpose: 'search'`) has `seat` the searcher and lists only that player's library, sorted by name then id. Nothing is asked when no card matches (the search still happens and the library is shuffled, CR 701.23b).
- Hidden information: the other seat's view shows no decision and no card: `pending` is null, `pendingCards` is absent, `waitingOn { seat, purpose: 'search', sourceName }` says who is searching, and `librarySearched { seat, found }` and `libraryShuffled { seat }` carry counts only; `cardsRevealed` appears only if the effect reveals.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-other-player-effects.test.ts` (the opponent of the caster is asked, not the caster, and the caster cannot answer; the choice is among the opponent's basic lands only, sorted by name; the land enters tapped under the opponent's control and their library is shuffled, not the caster's; finding nothing; declining does nothing at all; no basic land in the library; an empty library; the same seed gives the same shuffle; the caster's view shows no card and `waitingOn`; "you may search" asked of the controller; the Beast token goes to the target's controller after the target was destroyed, and to the controller of a stolen creature, and nothing is created when the target is gone; "its owner shuffles"), `ast-references-validate.test.ts`, `ast-search-validate.test.ts`; AI: `casual-references.test.ts` (the answers on real states), `reference-games.test.ts` (100 games, replay hashes, the other seat's view checked at every such decision). One behaviour was mutation-checked (see the slice report).
- Example cards: Path to Exile ("Exile target creature. Its controller may search their library for a basic land card, put that card onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle."), Beast Within ("Destroy target permanent. Its controller creates a 3/3 green Beast creature token."). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Banish Seek Land`, `Wild Transform`, `Optional Land Fetch`, `Seek And Shuffle`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; each card needs the parser to read its text (stage P10).
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 11, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Its controller may search their library for a basic land card, put that card onto the battlefield tapped, then shuffle." (after "Exile target creature.") lowers to `{ kind: 'search', player: { kind: 'controllerOf', of: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } }, optional: true, filter: { types: ['land'], supertypes: ['basic'] }, max: 1, destinations: [{ zone: 'battlefield', tapped: true, count: 1 }], reveal: false, shuffle: true }`; "You may search your library ..." is the same with `player: { kind: 'you' }` and `optional: true`; "Search your library ..." (no "may") has no `optional`. "Its controller creates a 3/3 green Beast creature token." lowers to `{ kind: 'createToken', token: { kind: 'custom', definition: <the 3/3 green Beast> }, count: 1, controller: { kind: 'controllerOf', of: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } } }`; "Its owner shuffles their library." is `{ kind: 'shuffle', player: { kind: 'ownerOf', of: ... } }`. The subject "its controller" / "its owner" of a clause after an effect on a target is the `controllerOf` / `ownerOf` selector of that target. "reveal" and "shuffle" follow the existing search lowering (`reveal` only when the text says to reveal).
- Notes: (1) The searcher is the owner of the cards in their library, so the land enters under their control by the ordinary move (110.2a). (2) A selector that names two players ("each opponent may search") makes them search one after the other, in seat order. (3) Declining a "may" search drops only that instruction: the rest of the sequence goes on (the exile before it has happened).

## Cards selected by owner: "Exile target player's graveyard" (engine slice; CR 108.3, 404.1)

- CR: 108.3 (the owner of a card is the player who started the game with it in their deck), 404.1 (a graveyard is its owner's discard pile), 115.1 (a player is a legal target), 603.3d (a triggered ability's target is chosen as it goes on the stack), 400.7 (each exiled card is a new object).
- Gap class: P (a filter field read where an effect knows its targets)
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`ObjectFilter.owner`), `rules/select.ts` (the `each` case of `resolveObjects` resolves the owner selector to `SelectContext.owners`), `rules/filter-match.ts` (`matchesFilterWith`; no resolved owners means no match), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkFilter` refuses `owner` outside an `each` selector, `ast/validate-prevent.ts` refuses it in a shield)
- AST node: `ObjectFilter` field `owner?: PlayerSelector`; `{ kind: 'exile', objects: { kind: 'each', filter: { zone: 'graveyard', owner: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } } } }` with a player target `t1`. `SUPPORTED_FEATURES.selectorKinds` lists `ownerRef`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-owner-filter.test.ts` (a spell: the opponent's graveyard is exiled and the caster's is not, targeting yourself, an empty graveyard, a stolen creature that died is in its owner's graveyard; an enters trigger: the target player is chosen as it goes on the stack and the graveyard is exiled when it resolves; "each opponent's graveyard"), `ast-references-validate.test.ts` (owner refused in a target slot, a search, an `anyOf` member, a sacrifice choice and a shield; a filter whose owner was not resolved matches nothing)
- Example cards: Bojuka Bog ("When ~ enters, exile target player's graveyard."), Tormod's Crypt (as the effect of its ability). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Graveyard Wipe` (an instant), `SYNTHETIC Graveyard Watcher` (when it enters).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; Bojuka Bog needs the land to enter tapped (supported) and the trigger text read.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 11, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Exile target player's graveyard." lowers to `{ kind: 'exile', objects: { kind: 'each', filter: { zone: 'graveyard', owner: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } } } }` with the target declaration `{ id: 't1', what: { kind: 'player', filter: 'any' } }`; "When ~ enters, exile target player's graveyard." is a triggered ability with that target and effect. "Exile each opponent's graveyard" would be `owner: { kind: 'eachOpponent' }` (no target). The `FeatureRef` is `selector:ownerRef`.
- Notes: (1) A card in a graveyard has no controller, so the filter must not say `controller`; the owner is read from the card. (2) `owner` is read in the `each` selector of an effect only; "target card in target player's graveyard" is a separate gap (`docs/ROADMAP.md`).

## "Counter target spell unless its controller pays" (engine slice; CR 701.6, 118.3, 608.2d)

- CR: 701.6 (counter), 118.3 (a player can't pay a cost without the resources to pay it fully, so they are not asked), 119.4 (a life payment needs at least that much life), 608.2d (the player offered the option decides as the effect resolves; here the controller of the target spell, not the caster of the counter), 605.3a (mana is paid by tapping sources), 608.2b (a target spell that left the stack is an illegal target).
- Gap class: P (an existing primitive allowed on a second kind of object)
- Code: `rules/effects.ts` (`counterUnlessPays` sets the instruction's `purpose`), `rules/pay-unless.ts` (both questions carry it), `api.ts` (`answerConfirm` and the `payMana` case route `ward` and `counterUnlessPays` to the same answers), `state/decisions.ts` (`ConfirmOptionalDecision` and `PayManaDecision` purposes), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkEffect`: a `target` or the `triggerSubject`); AI: `casual-permanents.ts` (`payToKeepSpell`)
- AST node: `Effect` `{ kind: 'counterUnlessPays', objects, cost }` with `objects` a `target` ("Counter target spell unless its controller pays {3}") or the `triggerSubject` of a trigger about a spell or ability (Ward). Only `mana` (no X) and `payLife` costs.
- Decision and action: as Ward: if the controller of the target spell cannot pay the whole cost the spell is countered at once and nobody is asked; otherwise a `confirmOptional` (`purpose: 'counterUnlessPays'`, `seat` the spell's controller, `source` the counter on the stack, `subject` the target spell, `cost`) and, after an accept with mana in the cost, a `payMana` (same purpose and subject; `cancel` means "do not pay": countered). A decline counters the spell. Ward keeps `purpose: 'ward'`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-counter-unless-target.test.ts` (the question goes to the target's controller with the cost and the subject; paying {3} and the spell resolves; declining counters it; backing out with `cancel` counters it; a player who cannot pay is not asked; exactly enough mana; the opponent's spell countered by the active player asks the opponent; paying life; too little life; the target countered in response: nobody is asked), `ast-references-validate.test.ts`, `scenario-19-ward.test.ts` (unchanged); AI: `reference-games.test.ts`, `casual-references.test.ts`. One behaviour was mutation-checked (see the slice report).
- Example cards: Mana Leak ("Counter target spell unless its controller pays {3}."), Force Spike ("... pays {1}."). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Soft Counter`, `SYNTHETIC Toll Counter` (pay 2 life).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; each card needs the parser to read its text (stage P10).
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 11, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Counter target spell unless its controller pays {3}." lowers to `{ kind: 'counterUnlessPays', objects: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' }, cost: [{ kind: 'mana', symbols: ['3'] }] }` with the target declaration `{ id: 't1', what: { kind: 'object', filter: { zone: 'stack', stackKind: 'spell' } } }`. "Counter target spell unless its controller pays N life" is a `payLife` cost; "unless its controller pays {X}" has no engine primitive.
- Notes: (1) `purpose` is chosen by the effect: a `target` object asks as `counterUnlessPays`, the `triggerSubject` as `ward`. (2) The web needs its own wording for the new purpose (the Ward dialog names the ability's source as the ward permanent).

## Return a permanent you control: the `choose` selector of `moveToZone` (engine slice; CR 608.2d, 400.3)

- CR: 608.2d (the controller chooses as the effect resolves, but not an option that is impossible), 400.3 (a card goes to its owner's hand, library or graveyard), 400.7 (the returned land is a new object), 603.3 (the trigger waits on the stack), 903.9b (a commander moved to the hand asks its owner).
- Gap class: P (the selector `sacrifice` already had, given to a second effect)
- Code: `rules/move-choice.ts` (`runChooseMove`), `rules/effects.ts` (`moveToZone` with `choose` becomes a `chooseMove` instruction), `rules/sacrifice.ts` (`controlledCandidates`, shared with `chooseSacrifice`), `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionChoice` answers both), `state/decisions.ts` (`ChoosePermanentDecision.purpose` has `'effect'`), `state/events.ts` (`permanentsChosen.purpose` has `'effect'`), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkChooseMove`); AI: `casual-permanents.ts` (`choosePermanentToReturn`)
- AST node: `Effect` `{ kind: 'moveToZone', objects: { kind: 'choose', filter, count? }, to }` with `to` `hand`, `graveyard` or `library`, and `filter.controller: 'you'` (required). The decision purpose is `effect` (not `sacrifice`: the permanent is not sacrificed, so nothing that watches sacrifices triggers).
- Decision and action: a `choosePermanent` (`purpose: 'effect'`, `min` = `max` = `count`, `legal` the matching permanents the controller controls) answered with `choosePermanents`; automatic (with `permanentsChosen { automatic: true }`) when no more than `count` qualify, nothing at all with none. The choice runs as `move` instructions, so `moved` means what was returned.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-choose-return.test.ts` (a bounce land with no other land returns itself without a question; the legal set is the controller's lands, the bounce land included, never the opponent's; returning another land by choice; returning itself by choice; an opponent's land, no land or two lands refused; the opponent cannot answer; the land destroyed in response: nothing chosen), `ast-references-validate.test.ts`; AI: `reference-games.test.ts`, `casual-references.test.ts`
- Example cards: bounce lands such as Karoo and Ravnica lands ("~ enters tapped. When ~ enters, return a land you control to its owner's hand. {T}: Add {G}{U}."), 12 cards in the replay. SYNTHETIC fixture: `SYNTHETIC Bounce Land`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; the bounce lands once the parser reads the trigger text and the two-mana ability.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 11, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "return a land you control to its owner's hand" (the effect of "When ~ enters, return a land you control to its owner's hand.") lowers to `{ kind: 'moveToZone', objects: { kind: 'choose', filter: { types: ['land'], controller: 'you' } }, to: 'hand' }`; the noun "a land you control" is the `choose` selector exactly as for "sacrifice a land" with the controller kept. "Return target land you control" is a target, not this.
- Notes: (1) The bounce land is itself a candidate (it is on the battlefield when its trigger resolves); with no other land the engine returns it. (2) A land that left in response leaves no candidate: nothing happens. (3) `CasualAgent` returns a tapped permanent first, then the one it values least.


## "[Do something] unless you return a permanent you control to its owner's hand" (Karoo; CR 118.12a, 608.2d, 400.3)

- CR: 118.12a ("[Do something] unless [a player does something else]" means "[A player may do something else]. If [that player doesn't], [do something]"), 608.2d (not an option that is impossible), 400.3 (a card goes to its owner's hand), 701.21a (the sacrifice)
- Gap class: P (existing nodes; one rule of the `may` question)
- Code: `rules/effects.ts` (the `mayEffect` instruction is not asked when the offered `moveToZone` with a `choose` selector has no candidate permanent), parser `rules/condition/bruna.ts` (`condition.unless-return`), `lower/sentences.ts` (`lowerUnlessReturn`)
- AST node: `sequence [ may(moveToZone { objects: choose { subtypes: ['Plains'], tapped: false, controller: 'you' }, to: 'hand' }), conditional(not didPrevious, sacrifice this) ]`. A permanent that cannot be chosen (a tapped Plains, no Plains, the land itself, which has no land type) is no candidate, so with none the question is not asked and the land is sacrificed.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-return-or-sacrifice.test.ts` (returns an untapped Plains; declining; only tapped Plains; no Plains; nothing else; a different card, "you may return a creature you control" with no creature, `SYNTHETIC Bounce Offer Land`), `packages/cards/test/parser-p69-bruna.test.ts`
- Example cards: Karoo. SYNTHETIC fixture: `SYNTHETIC Plains Karoo`.
- Added in: D-258 (the Bruna deck, issue 5)

## Drawing a commander from a library (engine fix; CR 903.9b, 121.1, 121.2, 504.1)

- CR: 903.9b (a commander that would be put into its owner's hand from anywhere may go to the command zone instead), 121.1 (drawing is putting the top card of the library into the hand), 121.2 (cards are drawn one at a time), 121.6b (a sequence of draws resumes after a replacement is completed), 504.1 (the draw of the draw step is a turn-based action).
- Gap class: P (an existing replacement applied to a second kind of move)
- Code: `rules/effect-support.ts` (`drawCard` returns `needsDecision` instead of throwing), `rules/draw.ts` (the `draw` instruction), `rules/effects.ts` (the `draw` effect queues one instruction per player), `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionCommanderChoice` records the answer on a `draw` as on a `move`), `rules/flow.ts` (`beginStep` pauses the draw step, `resumeDrawStep` finishes it), `api.ts` (`answerCommanderZone` tells the draw step from CR 903.9a)
- AST node: none (the `draw` effect is unchanged).
- Decision and action: the existing `commanderZone` decision (`from: 'library'`) answered with `moveToCommandZone` or `declineCommandZone`, for the owner only; the other seat's view shows `waitingOn`.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-draw-commander.test.ts` (the draw step accepted and declined, "draw two" with the commander first and second, two commanders in a row, a trigger's draw, an upkeep draw followed by the draw step, the other seat's view)
- Cards unlocked: no new card; it removes an engine error that any deck with a put-on-top effect and a commander could reach.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 12, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: nothing; no wording changes.
- Notes: (1) A replaced draw is not a card drawn (no `cardDrawn`), D-097. (2) The decision order for "draw N" is one card at a time, so the answer for the first card comes before the second is drawn.

## "~ can't block" and "~ can't be blocked": static restrictions (engine slice; CR 509.1b, 604, 611.3)

- CR: 509.1b (restrictions on blocking; an evasion ability is a restriction on what can block its creature), 604.2 and 611.3 (a static ability affects whatever its text indicates at any moment), 613.1f (a permanent that lost all abilities has no static ability left), 301.5 (Equipment).
- Gap class: P (one static effect, `restriction`, read where the rule acts)
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Restriction`, `StaticEffect` `restriction`, `StaticSubject` `this`), `rules/derive-statics.ts` (the index of static abilities collects `restriction`), `rules/derive.ts` (`Derived.restrictions`), `rules/restrictions.ts`, `rules/combat-legality.ts` (`canBlockAttacker`, `evades`), `ast/validate.ts` (`checkStatic`), `ast/features.ts` (`RESTRICTIONS`, `static:restriction`, `staticSubject:this`); `view.ts` (`ObjectView.restrictions`); AI: `casual-evasion.ts`
- AST node: `Ability` `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'restriction', subject, restriction } }` with `restriction` `cantBlock` or `cantBeBlocked` and `subject` `{ kind: 'this' }`, `{ kind: 'attached' }` or `{ kind: 'filter', filter }`.
- Decision and action: none new. A creature that can't block is not among `declareBlockers` candidates and the block is refused ("That creature cannot block"); an attacker that can't be blocked has no candidate blockers, so with nothing else to block the decision is skipped.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-restrictions-static.test.ts` (the creature itself, a filter subject of the opponent's creatures, an attached Equipment moved from creature to creature; the engine, the legal-action list and the decision's candidates agree), `ast-restrictions-validate.test.ts`; AI: `restriction-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: "~ can't block." (91 cards in the replay), "~ can't be blocked." (60), "Equipped creature can't be blocked." SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Cant Block Bear`, `SYNTHETIC Unblockable Bear`, `SYNTHETIC Muzzling Banner`, `SYNTHETIC Veil Blade`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; each card needs the parser to read its text.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 12, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "~ can't block." lowers to `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'restriction', subject: { kind: 'this' }, restriction: 'cantBlock' } }`; "~ can't be blocked." (also "This creature can't be blocked.") to the same with `restriction: 'cantBeBlocked'`. "Equipped creature can't be blocked." lowers to the same with `subject: { kind: 'attached' }` on an Equipment (an Aura: "Enchanted creature can't block." with `restriction: 'cantBlock'` and `subject: { kind: 'attached' }`). "Creatures your opponents control can't block." is `subject: { kind: 'filter', filter: { types: ['creature'], controller: 'opponent' } }`. The `FeatureRef`s are `static:restriction` and `staticSubject:this`, `staticSubject:attached` or `staticSubject:filter`. "Can't be blocked except by creatures with flying" and "can't be blocked by creatures with power 2 or less" are not built.
- Notes: (1) A static restriction is an ability: "loses all abilities" removes it; the effect form below is not. (2) "~ can't block" does not stop the creature from attacking.

## "~ attacks each combat if able" (engine slice; CR 508.1d)

- CR: 508.1d (requirements: a creature that is required to attack and can must be declared), 508.1a (only an untapped creature can attack), 302.6 (summoning sickness), 702.3b (defender).
- Gap class: P
- Code: `rules/combat-legality.ts` (`requiredAttackers`, `validateAttacks`), `rules/combat-flow.ts` (the decision carries `mustAttack`), `state/decisions.ts` (`DeclareAttackersDecision.mustAttack`), `legal-actions.ts` (every listed declaration includes the required creatures; no empty declaration), `testing/driver.ts` and `testing/expect-combat.ts` (the scenario helpers declare them); AI: `casual-combat.ts` (`chooseAttackers`), `random-agent.ts`
- AST node: `StaticEffect` `{ kind: 'restriction', subject, restriction: 'attacksEachCombat' }`, or the effect `restrict` with the same restriction and `duration: 'untilEndOfTurn'`.
- Decision and action: `declareAttackers` carries `mustAttack: ObjectId[]` (empty when none). A declaration without one of them is refused with "A creature that attacks each combat if able must attack". A creature that is tapped, summoning sick, has defender or has no target to attack is not a candidate and so not required. The listed actions: the required creatures attack each target together; "no attack" is listed only when none is required.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-attacks-each-combat.test.ts` (the decision names it; no declaration without it is accepted; every listed action is accepted; two required creatures; at a planeswalker too; tapped and summoning sick are not required; "attacks each combat and can't block"), `scenario-xx-restrictions-effects.test.ts` ("target creature attacks this turn if able"); AI: `restriction-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: "~ attacks each combat if able." (61 cards). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Berserker`, `SYNTHETIC Raging Brute`, `SYNTHETIC Goad Rage`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 12, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "~ attacks each combat if able." lowers to `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'restriction', subject: { kind: 'this' }, restriction: 'attacksEachCombat' } }`. "Target creature attacks this turn if able." lowers to `{ kind: 'restrict', objects: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' }, restriction: 'attacksEachCombat', duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' }` with a creature target. "Attacks each turn if able" is the same ability; "attacks each combat if able" on a creature that can't attack does nothing.
- Notes: (1) The CR 508.1d maximum rule for conflicting requirements is not needed yet: nothing stops an attack except removing the candidate. (2) A required creature attacks the player when it has the choice (`CasualAgent`) or the first target listed (`RandomLegalAgent`).

## "~ doesn't untap during your untap step" (engine slice; CR 502.3)

- CR: 502.3 (effects can keep permanents from untapping), 502.4 (no priority in the untap step), 613.1f (an ability goes with "loses all abilities").
- Gap class: P
- Code: `rules/flow.ts` (the untap step skips a permanent whose derived `restrictions` has `doesntUntap`), `rules/restrictions.ts`
- AST node: `StaticEffect` `{ kind: 'restriction', subject, restriction: 'doesntUntap' }`, subject `this` or `attached` (an Aura).
- Decision and action: none. The permanent stays tapped, no `untapped` event; an "untap" effect and the other permanents are unaffected.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-doesnt-untap.test.ts`; AI: `restriction-games.test.ts` (checked in every untap step of the soak)
- Example cards: "~ doesn't untap during your untap step." (35 cards), "Enchanted creature doesn't untap during its controller's untap step." SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Sleepy Giant`, `SYNTHETIC Slumber Aura`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 12, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "~ doesn't untap during your untap step." lowers to `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'restriction', subject: { kind: 'this' }, restriction: 'doesntUntap' } }`. "Enchanted creature doesn't untap during its controller's untap step." has `subject: { kind: 'attached' }` on an Aura. "You may choose not to untap ~ during your untap step" (an optional choice) is not this and is not built. An effect form ("target creature doesn't untap during its controller's next untap step") is not built.
- Notes: Only the active player's permanents untap, so a restriction on the opponent's permanent matters on the opponent's turn.

## "This spell can't be countered" (engine slice; CR 101.2, 113.6g, 701.6)

- CR: 101.2 (a "can't" effect takes precedence), 113.6g (an object's ability that says it can't be countered functions on the stack), 701.6 (counter), 702.21a (Ward counters a spell or ability unless its controller pays), 608.2b.
- Gap class: P
- Code: `rules/derive.ts` (`restrictionsOf` reads a spell's own `this` abilities on the stack), `rules/restrictions.ts` (`canBeCountered`), `rules/effects.ts` (`counter` and `counterUnlessPays` skip a spell that can't be countered)
- AST node: `Ability` `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'restriction', subject: { kind: 'this' }, restriction: 'cantBeCountered' } }` on the card face, an instant, sorcery or permanent spell.
- Decision and action: none new. A counter that resolves does nothing to it and it stays on the stack and resolves; a Ward trigger or "counter unless its controller pays" does not ask its controller (D-098) and takes no payment.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-cant-be-countered.test.ts` (Counterspell, Soft Counter and Toll Counter against an instant and a creature spell that can't be countered, with a control; Ward against a spell that can't be countered); AI: `restriction-games.test.ts`
- Example cards: "This spell can't be countered." and "~ can't be countered." (87 cards). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Stubborn Insight`, `SYNTHETIC Stubborn Bolt`, `SYNTHETIC Stubborn Beast`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 12, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "This spell can't be countered." (an instant or sorcery's first sentence) and "~ can't be countered." (a creature or other permanent spell) both lower to a static ability `{ kind: 'static', static: { kind: 'restriction', subject: { kind: 'this' }, restriction: 'cantBeCountered' } }` in the face's `abilities`, with the rest of the text as the spell or other abilities. "Spells you control can't be countered", "this spell can't be countered by blue spells" and abilities that can't be countered are not built.
- Notes: Once the permanent is on the battlefield the restriction has no meaning; it is read only for a spell on the stack.

## "{4}, {T}: Target creature can't be blocked this turn" and the `restrict` effect (engine slice; CR 613.11, 611.2)

- CR: 613.11 (effects that change the rules of the game apply after the layers), 611.2a (until end of turn), 611.2c (the set is fixed when the effect begins), 514.2 (it ends in the cleanup step), 509.1b, 508.1d.
- Gap class: P
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Effect` `restrict`, `ContinuousChange` `restrict`), `rules/continuous.ts` (`runContinuous`), `rules/derive.ts` (`restrictionsOf` adds the effects' restrictions after every layer), `ast/validate-continuous.ts`
- AST node: `Effect` `{ kind: 'restrict', objects, restriction, duration }` with `restriction` `cantBlock`, `cantBeBlocked` or `attacksEachCombat` and `duration: 'untilEndOfTurn'`.
- Decision and action: none new. It is a continuous effect in `GameState.continuousEffects` (change `restrict`); a restriction it gives survives "loses all abilities" because it is not an ability.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-restrictions-effects.test.ts` (a spell and a land's activated ability; it ends at the end of the turn; the set is fixed; cast after blockers were declared it does not undo a block; "loses all abilities" before and after; a static "can't block" goes with "loses all abilities", an effect's does not), `ast-restrictions-validate.test.ts`; AI: `restriction-games.test.ts`, `casual-evasion.test.ts`
- Example cards: Rogue's Passage ("{4}, {T}: Target creature can't be blocked this turn."), "Target creature can't block this turn." SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Rogue Gate`, `SYNTHETIC Ghost Step`, `SYNTHETIC Muzzle`, `SYNTHETIC Goad Rage`, `SYNTHETIC Mind Wipe`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 12, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "{4}, {T}: Target creature can't be blocked this turn." lowers to an activated ability with cost `[{ kind: 'mana', symbols: ['4'] }, { kind: 'tap' }]`, a creature target `t1` and effect `{ kind: 'restrict', objects: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' }, restriction: 'cantBeBlocked', duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' }`. "Target creature can't be blocked this turn." as a spell, the same effect; "target creature can't block this turn" with `cantBlock`. "Until end of turn, creatures you control can't be blocked" (a mass effect) is not built. `FeatureRef`s: `effect:restrict`, `duration:untilEndOfTurn`.
- Notes: A `permanent` duration validates but is reported as unsupported (only "until end of turn" ends).

## Landwalk, fear, intimidate and shadow (engine slice; CR 702.14, 702.36, 702.13, 702.28)

- CR: 702.14c (landwalk: can't be blocked while the defending player controls a land of the type, or without the type or supertype), 702.14d (landwalks don't cancel), 702.36b (fear), 702.13b (intimidate), 702.28b (shadow, both directions), 509.1b (evasion abilities are cumulative).
- Gap class: K (four keywords)
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Keyword` `landwalk`, `fear`, `intimidate`, `shadow`), `rules/combat-legality.ts` (`evades`, `controlsLandWalked`), `ast/validate.ts` (`checkKeyword`), `ast/features.ts` (`SUPPORTED_FEATURES.keywordKinds`, `GRANTABLE_KEYWORDS`), `view.ts` (`ObjectView.landwalks`); AI: `casual-evasion.ts` (`couldBlock`)
- AST node: `Keyword` `{ kind: 'landwalk', landType }` with `landType` a land subtype (`'Island'`, `'Desert'`) or `'nonbasic'`; `{ kind: 'fear' }`, `{ kind: 'intimidate' }`, `{ kind: 'shadow' }`. Fear, intimidate and shadow can be granted by `grantKeyword`; landwalk cannot.
- Decision and action: none new. They narrow the blockers a `declareBlockers` decision lists; the lands the defending player controls count tapped or untapped.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-landwalk.test.ts` (islandwalk, a nonbasic land with the Island type, desertwalk, nonbasic landwalk with Command Tower and a Desert, tapped lands, no cancelling, two attackers), `scenario-xx-fear-intimidate.test.ts` (black, artifact and colorless blockers; cumulative with flying), `scenario-xx-shadow.test.ts` (both directions); each checks that the engine, the legal actions and the decision agree; AI: `restriction-games.test.ts` (an independent statement of the rules over every block of 100 games)
- Example cards: Islandwalk (30 to 40 cards per land type), Nonbasic landwalk, Fear, Intimidate, Shadow. SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Islandwalker`, `SYNTHETIC Desertwalker`, `SYNTHETIC Nonbasic Walker`, `SYNTHETIC Fear Bear`, `SYNTHETIC Intimidate Bear`, `SYNTHETIC Shadow Bear`, `SYNTHETIC Dread Wraith`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; the keywords need the parser (a keyword line per word, with the land type from "[Type]walk").
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 12, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Islandwalk" lowers to `{ kind: 'keyword', keyword: { kind: 'landwalk', landType: 'Island' } }`; the same for Swampwalk, Forestwalk, Mountainwalk, Plainswalk and any "[Land type]walk" (Desertwalk). "Nonbasic landwalk" lowers to `landType: 'nonbasic'`. "Fear", "Intimidate" and "Shadow" lower to `{ kind: 'keyword', keyword: { kind: 'fear' | 'intimidate' | 'shadow' } }`. "Snow landwalk", "legendary landwalk" and "artifact landwalk" are not built. `FeatureRef`s: `keyword:landwalk`, `keyword:fear`, `keyword:intimidate`, `keyword:shadow`.
- Notes: (1) Shadow also stops a shadow creature from blocking a creature without shadow. (2) A colorless attacker with intimidate can be blocked only by artifact creatures.

## Counted amounts: "for each creature on the battlefield", "equal to the number of lands you control" (engine slice; CR 608.2c, 601.2a)

- CR: 608.2c (instructions are followed in written order, so a count is taken as its instruction runs, after everything before it), 601.2a (a spell being cast is on the stack, so it is not counted in the zone it came from), 109 and 205 (what a filter matches), 108.3 (a card in a hand, library or graveyard is its owner's).
- Gap class: P (a primitive: a count of the objects a filter matches, as an amount)
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Amount` `countOf`), `rules/effect-support.ts` (`amountOf`), `rules/select.ts` (`countMatching`, `SelectContext.excluded`), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`checkAmount`), `ast/features.ts` (`amount:countOf`)
- AST node: `Amount` `{ kind: 'countOf', filter: ObjectFilter }`, accepted wherever an effect takes an amount: `dealDamage`, `draw`, `discard`, `gainLife`, `loseLife`, `addCounters`, `createToken`, `scry`, `surveil`, `lookAndReorder`, `putFromHandOnTop`. The zone is the battlefield unless `filter.zone` says otherwise; `controller` is relative to the controller of the ability, `other` leaves out its source, `owner` is not read. `modifyPt`, `setPt` and the other continuous effects take plain numbers ("gets +1/+1 for each ..." is not built).
- Decision and action: none.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-count-amounts.test.ts` (damage equal to the number of creatures of all players, zero creatures, a noncreature permanent not counted, counted when the spell resolves after a creature died in response, "lands you control" with tapped lands and the opponent's lands left out, `other` leaves out the entering creature, "each card in your hand" with the spell not in the hand, the opponent's hand not counted, creature cards in your graveyard, an empty graveyard), `ast-costs-validate.test.ts` (every zone, every amount slot, malformed filters, `owner` refused). Behaviours mutation-checked: see the slice report.
- Example cards: "Draw a card for each card in your hand.", "You gain life equal to the number of lands you control.", "~ deals damage to target player equal to the number of creatures on the battlefield." (about 0.32 of the weight of the unread lines). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Crowd Burn`, `Land Tithe`, `Hand Refill`, `Grave Growth`, `Rally Bear` (`src/cards/fixtures/synthetic-costs.ts`).
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; each card needs the parser to read its text (stage P11).
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 13, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: "Draw a card for each card in your hand." lowers to `{ kind: 'draw', player: { kind: 'you' }, count: { kind: 'countOf', filter: { zone: 'hand', controller: 'you' } } }`; "You gain life equal to the number of lands you control." to `{ kind: 'gainLife', player: { kind: 'you' }, amount: { kind: 'countOf', filter: { types: ['land'], controller: 'you' } } }`; "~ deals damage to target player equal to the number of creatures on the battlefield." to `{ kind: 'dealDamage', amount: { kind: 'countOf', filter: { types: ['creature'] } }, to: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' } }`; "for each other creature you control" has `other: true`; "for each creature card in your graveyard" is `{ zone: 'graveyard', types: ['creature'], controller: 'you' }` and "in all graveyards" has no `controller`; "for each opponent" is not a count of objects and is not built. The noun phrase after "each" or "the number of" is the same phrase a target or a "creatures you control" subject uses. The `FeatureRef` is `amount:countOf`.
- Notes: (1) The count is taken once, when the instruction runs, and is not updated if the board changes during the effect. (2) The hand and library of a player are public in size, so counting the opponent's is allowed. (3) An {X} amount is unrelated and still unsupported.

## Additional costs to cast: sacrifice, discard, life and mana (engine slice; CR 118.8, 601.2b, 601.2f-h)

- CR: 118.8 (an additional cost is paid with the mana cost; some are optional), 601.2b (announced as the spell is cast), 601.2f (the total cost is determined and locked in), 601.2g-h (mana abilities first, then the player pays the total cost; partial payments are not allowed, unpayable costs can't be paid), 118.3 (a cost that can't be paid fully isn't paid, so the spell is not castable), 701.21 (sacrifice), 701.9 (discard), 603.10a (a creature that dies as a cost still triggers), 733 (a cast that is taken back is rewound), 119.4 (paying life).
- Gap class: S (a new step of the casting transaction; designed in D-095 and D-101, so no separate proposal)
- Code: `cards/definition.ts` (`CardFace.additionalCosts`), `rules/cast-cost.ts` (`spellCost`, `canPaySpellCost`, `castManaPlan`), `rules/cast-additional.ts` (the sacrifice and the discard after the mana), `rules/cast.ts` (`toPayment`, `answerPayment`, `answerCastSacrifice`, `answerCastDiscard`), `rules/legality.ts` (`canCast`), `rules/discard-cost.ts`, `api.ts` (routes `forCost` answers), `legal-actions.ts` (`cancel` on a `forCost` discard), `state/decisions.ts` (`ChooseCardsDecision.forCost`), `state/types.ts` (`CastingContext.stage` `'discard'`, `CastingContext.extra`), `ast/validate-cost.ts`, `ast/supported.ts` (`additionalCostIssues`), `view.ts` (`ObjectView.additionalCosts`); AI: `casual-costs.ts`
- AST node: `CardFace.additionalCosts: Cost[]`: at most one `{ kind: 'sacrifice', what: ObjectFilter }` (never "this"), at most one `{ kind: 'discard', count }`, `{ kind: 'payLife', amount }` (fixed, at least 1) and `{ kind: 'mana', symbols }` (no X). The costs are in the order the text gives them but are paid in the fixed order of CR 601.2h as built: life, mana, sacrifice, discard.
- Decision and action: after the targets, the total cost is locked in and a `payMana` decision asks for the mana as before (none when the cost is {0}). Then a sacrifice is a `choosePermanent` decision (`purpose: 'sacrifice'`, `forCost: true`, `source` the spell on the stack, `legal` the permanents the filter allows) unless exactly one qualifies, when the engine chooses and a `permanentsChosen` event with `automatic: true` says so; then a discard is a `chooseCards` decision (`purpose: 'discard'`, `forCost: true`, `min` = `max` = N, `legal` the hand without the spell) unless the hand holds exactly N other cards, which all go. `cancel` is a legal answer to both and rewinds the cast, mana, life and sacrifice included (CR 733). The spell is cast (`spellCast`) only when everything is paid; the creature's `dies` trigger goes on the stack above it. A spell is offered only when the whole cost can be paid: a candidate for the sacrifice, the cards to discard, the life (at least 1 left) and mana that does not use the only candidate up (`castManaPlan`).
- Hidden information: the discard question is visible to the caster alone; the other seat's view has `waitingOn { purpose: 'discard' }` and no card, and sees the `discarded` events once the cards are in the graveyard.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-additional-costs.test.ts` (sacrifice: choice after the mana, one candidate chosen by the engine, the dies trigger above the spell, with and without a question; "an artifact or creature" after targets; a land tapped for the mana then sacrificed; the target sacrificed as a cost; not castable with nothing to sacrifice; cancel rewinds mana and permanent; the other seat cannot answer; an illegal choice; a gem that sacrifices itself for mana is not spent on the cost, in the offer, in auto-pay and in a payment by hand; discard: choice, one card without a question, no other card not castable, cancel, exactly one card of the hand, hidden information; two costs rewound together; life: paid, needs 1 left, given back on cancel; extra mana; kicker with a sacrifice), `scenario-xx-cost-legal-actions.test.ts` (across hundreds of boards every offered cast works through to the end with the first and with the last answer, and nothing else is accepted; determinism), `ast-costs-validate.test.ts`; AI: `cost-games.test.ts` (100 games, replay hashes, an independent check of what was paid), `casual-costs.test.ts`. Behaviours mutation-checked: see the slice report.
- Example cards: "As an additional cost to cast this spell, sacrifice a creature." (58 cards), "... discard a card." (17), "... sacrifice an artifact or creature." (14), "... sacrifice a land." (6). SYNTHETIC fixtures: `SYNTHETIC Sacrifice Draw`, `Offering Bolt`, `Land Offering`, `Discard Burn`, `Double Toll`, `Blood Price`, `Extra Mana Tax`, `Sacrifice Kicker`.
- Cards unlocked: the engine side only; each card needs the parser to read its cost sentence (stage P11).
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 13, 2026-10-22
- What the parser must produce: the sentence "As an additional cost to cast this spell, sacrifice a creature." (and the `~` form older cards have: "As an additional cost to cast ~, sacrifice a creature.") is removed from the spell text and lowers to `CardFace.additionalCosts: [{ kind: 'sacrifice', what: { types: ['creature'] } }]`; "... sacrifice an artifact or creature." to `what: { anyOf: [{ types: ['artifact'] }, { types: ['creature'] }] }`; "... sacrifice a land." to `what: { types: ['land'] }`; "... discard a card." to `[{ kind: 'discard', count: 1 }]` ("discard two cards" has `count: 2`); "... sacrifice a creature and discard a card." to the two costs in that order; "... pay 3 life." to `{ kind: 'payLife', amount: 3 }`; "... pay {1}." to `{ kind: 'mana', symbols: ['1'] }`. The rest of the text lowers as the spell, unchanged. "Sacrifice another creature", "sacrifice two creatures", "discard a creature card", "exile a card from your graveyard" and "pay X life" are not built. The `FeatureRef`s are `cost:sacrifice`, `cost:discard`, `cost:payLife` and `cost:mana`, the dimension of activation costs.
- Notes: (1) The total cost, with any reduction, is locked in before the sacrifice, so a "costs {1} less for each creature" spell does not count the creature it sacrifices (CR 601.2h example). (2) Commander tax and kicker are added to the mana cost before reductions (CR 601.2f). (3) A flashback or free cast pays the additional costs too (CR 118.9d).

## Discard as a cost: "Ward—Discard a card." and "Discard a card: ..." (engine slice; CR 701.9, 702.21a, 602.2b, 118.3)
