Mutation-checked: the added combat after the postcombat main phase resumed at the wrong place fails 1 test, the condition ignoring whose turn 1, no delayed untap 2, a new phase put last instead of first 1.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 42, 2026-11-02
- Web: the gist "After this phase, there is an additional combat phase. At the beginning of that combat, untap each creature you control if it's your main phase" and the condition "it's your main phase" (`ui/decisions/ability-label.ts`, `game/condition-text.ts`, `game/dragons-text.ts`).
- What the parser must produce: `condition.your-main-phase` (`condition:yourMainPhase`) as the intervening "if" of a landfall trigger, `effect.extra-combat-after` (`effect:extraCombat`) and the delayed sentence "At the beginning of that combat, untap all creatures you control", folded by `lowerAtThatCombat` into the effect before it; any other sentence of that shape stays unread.
- Notes: the Dragons cards' `extraCombat` (D-151) is the same effect with `after: 'combat'`; the old counter is gone.

### "Exile the top two cards of target opponent's library face down. You may look at and play those cards for as long as they remain exiled."

- CR: 406.3, 406.3a, 406.3b, 701.13, 601.2, 305.1, 305.2, 400.7, 108.3, 715.3d, 611.2a
- Gap class: P (hard), as the analysis said, with the hidden information the engine already had for Hideaway and Foretell (a card some players may not look at) and a new looker
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`exileTop.from`, `exileTop.faceDown`, `mayPlay.lookAt`, `PlayUntil` `whileExiled`), `state/types.ts` (`GameObject.exiledFaceDown`, `lookers`, `adventureBy`), `rules/impulse.ts` (`runExileTop`, `runMayPlay`, `playEnd`), `rules/effects.ts` (the move marks the card), `view.ts` (`isFaceDownTo`, `faceDownView`, `ObjectView.exiledFaceDown`; `determinize` reads `isFaceDownTo`), `rules/legality.ts` (`castZone`: a card in exile another player owns may be cast by the player an effect let, and an Adventure by the player who cast it), `rules/zones.ts` (`adventureBy`), `ast/validate-dragons.ts`
- AST node: `{ kind: 'sequence', effects: [{ kind: 'exileTop', count: 2, from: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' }, faceDown: true }, { kind: 'mayPlay', objects: { kind: 'moved' }, lasts: 'whileExiled', lookAt: true }] }` with the target `{ id: 't1', what: { kind: 'player', filter: 'opponent' } }`, on the Adventure face of a card of `layout` `adventure`.
- Decision and action: the caster chooses the target opponent as the Adventure is cast; as it resolves the top two cards of that library (fewer if it has fewer) go to exile face down, still the opponent's cards; the caster sees them (`getView`) and the opponent sees two placeholders "Face-down card". The caster may cast a spell from among them at the timing of its type, paying its ordinary cost (the card turns face up as it is cast), or play a land as their land play (it enters under their control); a card that leaves the battlefield goes to its owner's zone. The permission has no end of its own: it is there in later turns, and a card that left exile is gone from the pile. The opponent may neither look at nor play them. An Adventure card among them may be cast by the caster as an Adventure and then from exile as the creature (the caster, not the owner, 715.3d).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-korvold-singles.test.ts` (the pile, what each player sees, a spell cast from it and a land played from it, a card destroyed going to its owner's graveyard, the opponent unable to cast or play, later turns, a library with one card and with none, the Adventure and the Dragon from exile with its Treasure, an Adventure of the pile cast by its taker and refused to its owner), `convert-grammar-engine-p41.test.ts` (the two-face card), `casual-korvold-singles.test.ts`, `korvold-singles-games.test.ts` (no player sees a card exiled face down that may not look at it, in 100 games). Mutation-checked: the card shown to every player fails 2 tests, `lookAt` ignored 1, a card of another player not castable 4, the permission ending with the turn 1, `adventureBy` not recorded 1, the move not marking the card 5.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 42, 2026-11-02
- Web: "Exile the top two cards of target opponent's library face down", "You may look at and play those cards for as long as they remain exiled" (`game/dragons-text.ts`), the log line "A card is exiled face down from the top of the opponent's library." naming no card (`game/describe.ts`), the mark "face down, you may look" and the preview's fact (`game/later-text.ts`), and the cards of the pile appear as "Cast from exile" chips for the player who may play them whoever owns them (`ui/board/OtherZonesStrip.tsx`).
- What the parser must produce: `effect.exile-top-face-down` (`effect:exileTop`) and `effect.may-look-and-play` (`effect:mayPlay`), the second read only after the first (the lowering requires cards exiled face down); "target player" and "target opponent", one or more cards.
- Notes: the AI plays the cards of the pile as any option from exile (a `castSpell` or `playLand` the engine lists); `determinize` treats the face-down cards that the viewer may not look at as hidden cards of their owner.

### Status of the three cards at parser version 46

Swashbuckler Extraordinaire, Moraug, Fury of Akoum and Decadent Dragon // Expensive Taste (both faces) read completely and pass `validateCard` (run from the owner's request file wording through `parser-p41.test.ts` and, for the two-face card, `convert-grammar-engine-p41.test.ts`). None is in the golden corpus (they are acceptance names for the owner's `pnpm corpus:extract`), and no card of the corpus changed status: training 162 of 192, hold-out 52 of 61, as at parser version 45.

## Korvold slice 8a: control change (D-189, D-190; `ENGINE_VERSION` 43, `PARSER_VERSION` 47)

### Control-changing effects (layer 2)

- CR: 613.1b, 613.7, 611.2a, 611.2b, 611.2c, 608.2b, 110.2, 108.3, 400.3, 302.6, 506.4, 509.1h, 603.3, 603.10a, 701.21a, 800.4a
- Gap class: S (the controller was a stored field read in about 360 places), `docs/design/control-change.md`, approved with the Korvold slices; done as the proposal recommends, a pure refactor first and the effect on top
- Code: `rules/control.ts` (`controllerOf`, `controlledSince`, `controlEndOf`), `rules/control-effects.ts` (`runGainControl`, `dropEffects`, `endLostControlEffects`), `rules/effects-on.ts` (the effects of a state grouped by object, shared with `derive-layers.ts`), `rules/combat-removal.ts` (CR 506.4), `rules/continuous.ts` (the three `expire...Effects` return events), `rules/flow.ts`, `rules/sba.ts` (a "for as long as you control this" effect ends with the state-based actions), `rules/mana-abilities.ts` and `rules/conditions.ts` (`controlledSince` for CR 302.6 and Echo), `rules/zones.ts` (`recordLki` freezes the controller), `state/types.ts` (`ContinuousChange` `control`, `EffectEnd` `whileControlling`, `GameObject.controlChangedAt`), `state/events.ts` (`controlChanged`), `ast/types.ts` (`Effect` `gainControl`, `ControlDuration`), `ast/validate-parts.ts`, `ast/features.ts`, `view.ts` (`ObjectView.controlEnds`)
- AST node: `{ kind: 'gainControl', objects: { kind: 'target', id: 't1' }, duration: 'untilEndOfTurn' }` (also `untilEndOfYourNextTurn`, `forAsLongAsYouControlThis` for an ability of a permanent, `permanent`); the objects are a `target` or the `triggerSubject` permanent
- Decision and action: none. The controller of the effect gains control of the permanent as the effect resolves, when it is still on the battlefield (a permanent that is gone is skipped); the owner never changes, so a stolen creature that dies goes to its owner's graveyard, a token ceases to exist, a commander asks its owner about the command zone. Of several effects on one permanent the latest decides and one that ends leaves the others. The new controller has the abilities (activated, mana, triggered, static) and the old one loses them; what is already on the stack stays with the player who controlled it. A creature taken this turn cannot attack or use {T} abilities (CR 302.6) unless it has haste, one kept through the thief's next turn can, one that goes back to its owner in the middle of their own turn is sick again; a permanent that is not a creature is not sick. A permanent whose controller changes leaves combat. An effect "until the end of your next turn" is the next turn of its controller after this moment; "for as long as you control this" does nothing when that is already not so as it begins (CR 611.2b), and ends for good as the permanent leaves or changes controller.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-control-change.test.ts`, `ast-control-validate.test.ts`, `control-reads.test.ts` (no rule reads the stored field), `korvold-control-games.test.ts` (the AI soak). Mutation-checked: no combat removal fails 2 tests, a `controlledSince` that ignores the effect 1, no `controlChangedAt` 1, no end of "for as long as" 3, no `tappedForMana` event 8.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 43, 2026-11-02
- Web: the log line "X gain(s) control of Y", the flag "taken" on the board tile of such a permanent, the preview's fact "Taken from its owner (...) until ..." (`game/korvold-text.ts` `controlFact`), the words "Gain control of target creature until end of turn" (`ui/decisions/ability-label.ts`); the permanent is on the side of its controller (the board places by `ObjectView.controller`)
- What the parser must produce: `effect.gain-control` (`effect:gainControl`): "gain control of target creature [until end of turn | until the end of your next turn | for as long as you control ~]", "... of that artifact ...", "... of it ...".
- Notes: the refactor that made the controller derived is described in D-189; a new read of a permanent's controller must go through `controllerOf` (the scan test fails otherwise). With two seats the game ends when a player loses, so CR 800.4a is not built. Not built: giving control to another player than the controller of the effect (an exchange), a static ability that gives control (an Aura), Suspend's haste ending when control is lost.

### "Whenever an opponent taps an artifact for mana, gain control of that artifact until the end of your next turn." (Treasure Nabber)

- CR: 106.12, 603.2, 603.3, 605.1b, 608.2b, 611.2b, 400.7, 613.1b
- Gap class: T and P: a trigger (the event of tapping for mana) and the effect above; the analysis called the card S because of the effect
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Trigger` `tapsForMana`), `state/events.ts` (`tappedForMana`), `rules/mana-abilities.ts` (`payManaAbilityCosts` emits it right after `tapped`), `rules/triggers.ts` (`occurrences`, `matches`: the player and the permanent as it was when tapped, from last-known information when it is gone), `ast/validate.ts` (an effect that adds mana is refused: that is the triggered mana ability `tappedForMana`)
- AST node: `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger: { kind: 'tapsForMana', who: 'opponent', what: { types: ['artifact'] } }, effect: { kind: 'gainControl', objects: { kind: 'triggerSubject' }, duration: 'untilEndOfYourNextTurn' } }`
- Decision and action: none. Each time an opponent activates a mana ability with {T} of an artifact the ability triggers, whatever the mana is for, and goes on the stack the next time a player would get priority (after the spell the mana was being paid for). Resolving it, its controller gains control of that artifact until the end of their next turn (the following turn of theirs even when the tap was in their own turn); the artifact untaps for them in their untap step and they may tap it for mana; at the cleanup step of that turn it goes back. A Treasure is sacrificed as part of tapping it, so it is gone when the ability resolves and nothing happens to it (CR 608.2b); the ability still triggers. The effect does not depend on the Nabber: it stays when the Nabber leaves (CR 611.2b: the duration is the turn, not the source). The controller of the Nabber tapping an artifact of their own, or the opponent tapping a land, does not trigger it; two Nabbers trigger twice, each ability's controller ordering them.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-control-change.test.ts` (the last group), `parser-p42.test.ts`, `convert-grammar-engine-p42.test.ts`, `korvold-control-games.test.ts`
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 43, 2026-11-02
- Web: "Whenever an opponent taps an artifact for mana", "Gain control of it until the end of your next turn" (`game/trigger-text.ts`, `ui/decisions/ability-label.ts`)
- What the parser must produce: `trigger.taps-for-mana` (`trigger:tapsForMana`) with `effect.gain-control`, "that artifact" bound to the permanent tapped.
- Notes: the trigger is for a player who is "an opponent" or "a player", and "you" is the triggered mana ability's (`trigger.tapped-for-mana`, which only adds mana).

Treasure Nabber reads completely and passes `validateCard` (run from the owner's request file wording through `parser-p42.test.ts`). It is not in the golden corpus (it is an acceptance name for the owner's `pnpm corpus:extract`), and no card of the corpus changed status: training 162 of 192, hold-out 52 of 61, as at parser version 46. Its power and toughness are not in the owner's request file; the SYNTHETIC fixture's 3/2 is invented, and the real card gets them from Scryfall's data when the owner imports it.

## Voting: Emissary Green (engine slice and parser stage P44; CR 701.38, D-194, D-195)

- CR: 701.38a (each player, starting with a specified player and proceeding in turn order, chooses one of the listed choices), 701.38b (the choices may be words with no rules meaning that are each connected to a different effect), 701.38c ("voting" is only an actual vote), 701.38d (several votes at once: not built), 608.2d (the choice is made as the effect is applied), 603.5 (a triggered ability's choices are made as it resolves), 110.2 (the player who creates a token is its controller), 122.1 (counters). Read with `pnpm rule 701.38`.
- Gap class: S in the playbook's list, built as P (the proposal `docs/design/voting.md` said so itself): the machinery existed.
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Effect` `vote`, `Amount` `votesFor`), `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate-parts.ts` (the options, `Scope.voteOptions`), `rules/vote.ts` (`runVote`, `tallyOf`), `rules/vote-answer.ts` (`answerResolutionVote`), `rules/effects.ts` (the dispatch), `rules/stack.ts` (`StepOutcome.votes` into `ResolutionContext.votes`), `rules/amounts.ts` (`votesFor`), `rules/select.ts` (`SelectContext.votes`), `api.ts` (`answerChooseOption`), `state/types.ts` (`Instruction` `vote`), `state/decisions.ts` (`ChooseOptionDecision` purpose `vote`, `VoteQuestion`, `VoteCast`), `state/events.ts` (`optionChosen` purpose `vote`), `cards/fixtures/synthetic-vote.ts`; parser: `rules/effect/vote.ts`, `rules/amount/votes.ts`, `rules/effect/counters.ts`, `rules/effect/player.ts`, `lower/korvold.ts` (`VOTE`), `lower/amounts.ts`; AI: `casual-vote.ts`, `heuristic-vote.ts`; web: `ui/decisions/option-names.ts`, `NamedOptionDialog.tsx`, `game/describe.ts`, `game/amount-text.ts`, `ui/decisions/ability-label.ts`.
- AST node: `{ kind: 'vote', options: ['profit', 'security'], startingWith: 'you' }`, then later effects of the same sequence read `{ kind: 'votesFor', option: 'profit' }`: Emissary Green is `{ kind: 'sequence', effects: [vote, createToken { Treasure, count: multiple { factor: 2, of: votesFor profit } }, addCounters { each creature you control, '+1/+1', count: votesFor security }] }` under `trigger: attacks this`.
- Decision and action: `chooseOption` with `purpose: 'vote'` for each voter in turn, asked of that voter; `options` are the card's words in its order, `vote: { controller, cast }` the votes so far (public), `source` the spell or ability that resolves. The answer is `{ kind: 'chooseOption', option }`, refused unless the option is offered and the seat is the voter's; the event is `optionChosen` with purpose `vote`. No cancel.
- When it asks: as the effect resolves, once for each player still in the game, starting with the controller of the spell or ability and in turn order (`seatsInOrder`), one at a time; a vote is never asked for a player who has lost. After the last vote the rest of the sequence runs at once with the tally (an option nobody chose counts 0; a tie is nothing special: each option's effect scales with its own votes).
- Tests: `scenario-xx-vote.test.ts` (the first voter and the order, the votes the second voter sees, refusals, the log, ties and counts, a spell controlled by the second seat, three seats and a player who has lost, Emissary Green's profit, security and split votes, no trigger without an attack, replay), `ast-vote-validate.test.ts`, `packages/cards/test/convert-grammar-engine-p44.test.ts` (the parsed card played), `parser-p44-needs.test.ts`; AI: `vote-agents.test.ts`, `vote-games.test.ts` (the `vote` style); web: `vote-text.test.ts`, `vote-render.test.tsx`.
- Mutation checks: see D-194 (the engine's vote and validation tests fail for each of twelve changes but one equivalent mutant).
- Example cards: Emissary Green (real wording from the owner's request file of 2026-10-29, through the grammar; `SYNTHETIC Emissary Green` carries the tests, its 3/4 is invented because the file gives none); `SYNTHETIC Ballot Rite` (three options, an effect against the opponent).
- Cards unlocked: Emissary Green.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 46, `PARSER_VERSION` 49, 2026-11-02
- Web: the named-option dialog titled with the source, "Vote for profit or security. Opponent voted for profit.", one button for each option ("Profit", "Security"), no way out; the log "You vote for profit (Emissary Green)." and "Opponent votes for security (Emissary Green)."; no browser test (no new dialog kind).
- What the parser must produce: `effect.vote` ("starting with you, each player votes for A or B", also "A, B or C") as the clause `vote` with a `choiceKind` argument of kind `option`; the amount atom "the number of A votes" (read after "equal to", also "twice ...", "half ..., rounded down", "... plus ...") as `votesFor`; "You create a number of Treasure tokens equal to ..." through `token.number-of`; "Put a number of +1/+1 counters on each creature you control equal to ..." as `effect.add-counters-number-of`. The order (a vote before the count of its votes, an option the vote offers) is the engine's validation, not the grammar's.
- Notes: (1) AI: the casual and greedy agents: the controller votes for the first option, the others for the option with the fewest votes so far (the later one on a tie); the heuristic agent plays each option out on its sample and scores the board; random picks among the options. (2) One vote for each player (CR 701.38d is not built); the vote starts with the controller; the options are words. (3) A second `vote` in one resolution replaces the tally of the first. (4) The official ruling could not be read (Scryfall is blocked).

### Status of the card at parser version 49

Emissary Green reads completely and passes `validateCard` (run from the owner's request file wording through `convert-grammar-engine-p44.test.ts`, its parsed abilities equal the engine fixture's). It is not in the golden corpus (it is an acceptance name for the owner's `pnpm corpus:extract`), and no card of the corpus changed status or AST: the snapshot moved only its version, training 162 of 192, hold-out 52 of 61 (a gap of 0.8 points). Its power and toughness are not in the owner's request file; the SYNTHETIC fixture's 3/4 is invented, and the real card gets them from Scryfall's data when the owner imports it.

## The Ring tempts you: There and Back Again (engine slice and parser stage P45; CR 701.54, D-196, D-197)

- CR: 701.54a (each time the Ring tempts you, choose a creature you control: it becomes your Ring-bearer until another creature does or another player gains control of it), 701.54b (a designation, not a copiable value), 701.54c (the emblem named The Ring and its four levels), 701.54d ("whenever the Ring tempts you" even when everything was impossible), 701.54e ("is your Ring-bearer": on the battlefield under your control with the designation), 509.1b (evasion is read as blockers are declared), 509.3d ("becomes blocked by a creature" triggers once for each creature that blocks), 603.7 and 603.7c (a delayed triggered ability and the object it names), 611.2b ("for as long as you control this"), 400.7 (a new object has no designation), 704.5j (the legend rule sees the supertype)
- Gap class: S (`docs/design/the-ring.md`): a player-level emblem, a designation of a permanent, and three triggered abilities that belong to the player.
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Effect` `ringTempts` and `delayedSacrifice`, `sacrifice.byController`, `restrict` with the duration `forAsLongAsYouControlThis`, `Trigger` `ringTempted`, `Condition` `controlsRingBearer`), `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate-parts.ts`, `ast/validate-continuous.ts`, `ast/validate.ts`, `rules/ring-bearer.ts`, `rules/ring.ts` (`runRingTempts`, `endLostRingBearers`, `findRingTriggers`, the three abilities and their texts), `rules/inherent.ts`, `rules/effects.ts` (the dispatch, `byController`, `delayedSacrifice`), `rules/continuous.ts` and `rules/control-effects.ts` (`endOf`), `rules/derive.ts` (the supertype), `rules/combat-legality.ts` (`evades`), `rules/sba.ts`, `rules/triggers.ts` (`ringTempted`, `findRingTriggers`), `rules/trigger-stack.ts`, `rules/trigger-subject.ts`, `rules/stack.ts` (`answerResolutionChoice`), `rules/conditions.ts`, `state/types.ts` (`SeatState.ring`, `GameObject.ringBearer`, `Instruction` `ringTempts`, `InherentAbility`), `state/decisions.ts` (purpose `ringBearer`), `state/events.ts` (`ringTempted`, `ringBearerLost`), `view.ts`, `invariants.ts`, `testing/*` (`seat.ring(n)`, `{ ringBearer: true }`, `expect.ringLevel`, `expect.ringBearer`), `cards/fixtures/synthetic-ring.ts`.
- AST nodes: `{ kind: 'ringTempts' }`; `{ kind: 'triggered', trigger: { kind: 'ringTempted' }, ... }`; `{ kind: 'controlsRingBearer' }` as `interveningIf` or in a `conditional`; `{ kind: 'restrict', objects, restriction: 'cantBlock', duration: 'forAsLongAsYouControlThis' }`; `{ kind: 'sacrifice', objects: { kind: 'this' }, byController: true }`. There and Back Again chapter I is a `triggered` chapter ability with the target slot `{ id: 't1', min: 0, max: 1, what: creature }` and a `sequence [restrict t1 cantBlock forAsLongAsYouControlThis, ringTempts]`; chapter III creates the custom token Smaug (supertypes legendary, flying, haste and a `dies` trigger that creates fourteen Treasure tokens).
- Decision and action: `choosePermanent` with `purpose: 'ringBearer'`, `min` = `max` = 1, `legal` the creatures the player controls (as `derive` types them), asked of the player the Ring tempts, only with two or more creatures; the answer is `{ kind: 'choosePermanents', objects: [one] }`. No cancel; a creature that is not in `legal` is refused. Events: `ringTempted { seat, times, bearer, first }` once the choice is made (also with no creature, `bearer` null), `ringBearerLost { seat, object }` when another creature becomes the bearer or another player gains control of it.
- When it asks: as the effect resolves (the instruction waits at the head of the queue and nothing has changed before the answer). A player with one creature has it chosen at once and is not asked; with none only the count moves.
- The levels: `SeatState.ring.tempted` is the count; the abilities are on from 1 (legendary and not blocked by greater power), 2 (loot: when the Ring-bearer is declared as an attacker), 3 (for each creature declared as a blocker of the Ring-bearer, a delayed triggered ability sacrifices it, by its controller, as the end of combat step begins), 4 (when the Ring-bearer deals combat damage to a player, each opponent loses 3 life). The levels are cumulative. Each ability goes on the stack as "The Ring" with no source and the rule's text, and can be responded to.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-ring.test.ts` (the question, its answers and refusals, one creature, none, a noncreature permanent, the watcher, the second tempt moving or keeping the designation, the opponent's Ring, each level, the view, the loss by death and by control, the condition, replay and hash, the Saga's chapters I to III including Smaug and its fourteen Treasure tokens, the target that cannot block and the end of that when the Saga leaves), `ast-ring-validate.test.ts`, `packages/cards/test/parser-p45.test.ts`, `parser-p45-needs.test.ts`, `convert-grammar-engine-p45.test.ts`; AI: `ring-agents.test.ts`, `ring-games.test.ts` (the `ring` deck style); web: `ring-text.test.ts`, `ring-render.test.tsx`.
- Mutation checks: see D-196 (six changes, each caught by the scenario tests).
- Example cards: There and Back Again (real wording from the owner's request file of 2026-10-29, through the grammar; `SYNTHETIC There and Back Again` carries the tests, nothing in it is invented); `SYNTHETIC Ring Rite`, `SYNTHETIC Ring Watcher` and `SYNTHETIC Bearer Oath` (invented: the Ring on its own, its trigger and its condition).
- Cards unlocked: There and Back Again.
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 47, `PARSER_VERSION` 50, 2026-11-02
- Web: the permanent panel titled "Choose your Ring-bearer" with the prompt "The Ring tempts you: choose your Ring-bearer, a creature you control, for There and Back Again." and the badge "Ring-bearer" on the chosen creature (no new dialog kind, so no browser test); a "Ring-bearer" flag on the tile and a "Ring N" chip on the player's strip, whose tooltip lists the four lines and which are on; the log "The Ring tempts you (the first time). Grizzly Bears becomes your Ring-bearer." and "Grizzly Bears is no longer your Ring-bearer."; the abilities on the stack are "The Ring", worded from the kind of trigger relative to the viewer (`game/monarch-text.ts`).
- What the parser must produce: `effect.ring-tempts` ("the Ring tempts you") as the clause `ringTempts`; `trigger.ring-tempts` as the event `ringTempted`; `condition.ring-bearer` ("you control your Ring-bearer") as `controlsRingBearer`; `effect.restrict-while-controlling` ("up to one target creature can't block for as long as you control ~") as the clause `restrict` with the duration `whileYouControlSelf`; `token.named-legendary` ("Smaug, a legendary 6/6 red Dragon creature token with flying, haste, and \"When Smaug dies, ...\"") as a token with `named` and `legendary`, the name inside its quotes read as `~`, and the keyword list "A, B, and \"...\"" for any token.
- Notes: (1) AI: the casual and greedy agents choose the creature with the most power (the first the engine lists on a tie), the heuristic agent scores power, toughness, keywords and the opposing creatures with greater power that cannot block it, random picks. (2) The proposal's recommendation (swap the card out) was not taken: the owner approved the slice. (3) The designation holds the player (`ringBearer: SeatId`), so it does not come back when control does. (4) The official ruling could not be read (Scryfall is blocked); the CR text decides.

### Status of the card at parser version 50

There and Back Again reads completely and passes `validateCard` (run from the owner's request file wording through `convert-grammar-engine-p45.test.ts`, its parsed abilities equal the engine fixture's). It is not in the golden corpus (it is an acceptance name for the owner's `pnpm corpus:extract`), and no card of the corpus changed status or AST: the snapshot moved only its version, training 162 of 192, hold-out 52 of 61, acceptance 1 of 1 (a gap of 0.8 points). A Saga has no power or toughness, so nothing in the SYNTHETIC fixture is invented; the card's layout (`saga`) and the chapter symbols come from the owner's file and Scryfall's data when the owner imports it.

## Planeswalkers: loyalty abilities, planeswalker tokens, emblems, Empower Jace (engine D-200, D-201; parser stage P47, D-202; agents and web D-203; `ENGINE_VERSION` 48, `PARSER_VERSION` 52)

- CR: 306.5a to 306.5d and 122.1e (printed loyalty, the intrinsic replacement effect "enters with loyalty counters", loyalty as the number of counters), 606.1 to 606.6 and 107.15 (loyalty abilities: an activated ability with a loyalty symbol in its cost, once a turn for each permanent at sorcery timing, "[+N]" puts counters, "[-N]" removes, "[0]" neither, "[-X]", a negative cost needs that many counters), 704.5i (0 loyalty), 113.7a (an ability still resolves after its walker is gone), 306.6, 506.3, 506.4, 506.4c, 506.4d, 508.5, 509.1a, 510.1b (attacking and blocking planeswalkers, removal from combat), 120.3c and 120.3 (damage to a planeswalker removes loyalty), 115.1 ("target player or planeswalker"), 205.3j and 205.3d (planeswalker types), 114.1 to 114.5 (emblems), 701.71a (Empower Jace), 119.5 and 119.9 (a life total that becomes a number), 903.3, 903.8, 903.9, 903.10a and 113.6n (a planeswalker commander). Two old rules are gone from this CR and are not implemented: 306.4 (the planeswalker uniqueness rule) and 306.7 (redirecting damage from a player to a planeswalker).
- Gap class: S (`docs/design/planeswalkers.md`): a new ability family with its own timing rule, a new token kind, a new object kind (the emblem), new decisions and UI.
- Code: `ast/types.ts` (`Cost` `loyalty`, `TargetSpec` `playerOrObject`, `Effect` `createEmblem`, `empowerJace`, `setLifeTotal`, `Keyword` `canBeCommander`), `ast/features.ts`, `ast/validate.ts` (`checkLoyaltyAbility`), `ast/validate-parts.ts` (`loyaltyOffPlaneswalkerIssues`, the token loyalty, the new effects and target), `rules/loyalty.ts`, `rules/activate-legality.ts`, `rules/activate-costs.ts`, `rules/activate.ts`, `rules/x-value.ts` (`XCost.loyalty`), `rules/tokens.ts` and `rules/zones.ts` (loyalty and defense counters of tokens and copies), `rules/emblem.ts`, `rules/empower.ts`, `rules/effects.ts`, `rules/derive-statics.ts` and `rules/triggers.ts` (an emblem's abilities), `rules/sba.ts`, `rules/defending.ts`, `rules/combat.ts`, `rules/combat-removal.ts`, `rules/control-effects.ts`, `rules/select.ts` (`legalTargets`), `data/planeswalker-types.ts` (generated: `pnpm planeswalker-types:generate`), `cards/tokens.ts` (`JACE_TOKEN`), `view.ts` (`printedLoyalty`, `loyaltyUsed`, `emblem`), `state/types.ts` (`GameObject.emblem`, `AttackerState.defender` and `targetGone`, `TurnLog.activated[].loyalty`). Parser: `parser/rules/cost/index.ts` (`cost.loyalty`), `segment.ts` (the loyalty prefix), `rules/keyword/commander.ts`, `rules/effect/planeswalker.ts`, `lower/planeswalker.ts`, `lower/activated.ts`, `sentence.ts` (`LOYALTY_SENTENCE`). Agents: `packages/ai/src/casual-loyalty.ts`, `casual-combat.ts`. Web: `game/cost-text.ts`, `ui/decisions/priority.ts`, `ui/decisions/ability-label.ts`, `ui/board/resolve.ts`, `ui/board/Tile.tsx`, `ui/board/PlayerStrip.tsx`, `game/describe.ts`.
- AST nodes: a loyalty ability is `{ kind: 'activated', cost: [{ kind: 'loyalty', change: 1 }], effect, targets? }` with `change` a whole number or `'x'` for "[-X]"; no `timing`, no `oncePerTurn`. The target `{ kind: 'playerOrObject', player: 'any' | 'opponent', filter: { types: ['planeswalker'] } }`. `{ kind: 'createEmblem', abilities: [static or triggered ...], text }`, `{ kind: 'empowerJace', amount }`, `{ kind: 'setLifeTotal', player, amount }`, `{ kind: 'keyword', keyword: { kind: 'canBeCommander' } }`. A planeswalker token is a `TokenDefinition` with `types: ['planeswalker']` and `loyalty`.
- Decision and action: a loyalty ability is the ordinary `activate` option and action (`{ kind: 'activate', object, abilityIndex }`), listed only at sorcery timing, while no loyalty ability of that permanent was activated this turn, and while the cost can be paid; `[-X]` asks the usual `chooseX` (`perX.loyalty` is 1, `max` the loyalty). Empower Jace asks `choosePermanent` with `purpose: 'empowerJace'` only with two or more Jace tokens. No new decision kind.
- When it asks / what happens: costs are paid with the other costs that need no choice (the loyalty counters first); the ability is on the stack and resolves with the last-known information of its walker; a cancelled activation rewinds the counters (the snapshot). The note in `turnLog.activated` is written as the activation completes, so a cancelled one does not count.
- Tests: `scenario-xx-loyalty` (cost, timing, once per permanent, 606.6, `[0]`, `[-X]`, an ultimate that outlives its walker, the rewind, statics and triggers of a walker, a loyalty ability that adds mana), `scenario-xx-planeswalker-model` (loyalty of a cast card, a token, a token copy; planeswalker types; the view), `scenario-xx-planeswalker-damage` (the target "player or planeswalker", lifelink, infect, a creature that is a planeswalker, shields, the order question and redirection with a planeswalker), `scenario-xx-planeswalker-combat` (a stolen and a retyped planeswalker, the animated planeswalker), `scenario-xx-planeswalker-commander`, `scenario-xx-emblems`, `scenario-xx-empower-jace`, `scenario-xx-set-life`, `ast-loyalty-validate`; the parser's `parser-p47`, `convert-grammar-engine-p47` (the real planeswalkers played in the engine); `planeswalker-games` (soak); `e2e/planeswalkers.e2e.ts`; `apps/web/test/planeswalkers-model.test.ts`.
- Mutation checks: see D-200 and D-201 (the once-per-permanent mark, the sorcery timing, the 606.6 bound, the `[0]` cost, a token's loyalty, `targetGone`, the emblem exemption of CR 704.5d, an emblem's statics and triggers, the Jace token creation: each caught).
- Example cards: the SYNTHETIC planeswalkers of `cards/fixtures/synthetic-planeswalkers.ts` (plus, zero and minus with a target; `[-X]`; an ultimate that takes all its loyalty; statics and a trigger on the walker; one that becomes a creature; one whose ability adds mana; a commander), the token makers, the emblem rites, the life setter, the spells that take a walker or its types away; the real cards: Garruk Wildspeaker, Jace Beleren, Elspeth, Sun's Champion, Gideon, Ally of Zendikar, Vraska, Relic Seeker, Protege's Awakening, Keeper of the Quiet Hour, Lavalanche (owner's golden corpus, real text).
- Cards unlocked: the six above, with Lavalanche's real target. Every other real planeswalker of the corpus stops at a line that is not a planeswalker matter (D-202 lists them).
- Added in: `ENGINE_VERSION` 48, `PARSER_VERSION` 52, 2026-11-04.
- Web: the chooser lists the loyalty abilities of a clicked planeswalker with their cost ("+1: You gain 2 life", "−2: ...", "0: ...", "−X: ..."), always as a menu; the tile shows the loyalty in the corner (the card in a hand shows the printed one) and a "used" flag once a loyalty ability was activated this turn; the emblem is in the command zone strip, its text as the title; the log says "You get an emblem."
- What the parser must produce: `cost.loyalty` ("+1", "−2", "0", "−X", brackets optional) as the cost `loyalty`, only on a planeswalker face and alone; the body of a loyalty ability is read with the choices of a triggered ability's; `keyword.can-be-commander`; `effect.get-emblem` (`You get an emblem with "..."`) as `createEmblem` with the abilities of the quoted text, `this emblem` read as `~`; `effect.empower-jace`; `effect.life-total-becomes`; `effect.lead-becomes-creature` ("..., ~ becomes a 5/5 Human Soldier Ally creature with indestructible that's still a planeswalker") as `setTypes` (additive), `setPt`, `grantKeyword`; "to him/her/~" as the recipient of `effect.prevent-all`; `noun.player-or-planeswalker` as `playerOrObject`.
- Notes: (1) AI: the casual, greedy and heuristic agents use one loyalty ability a turn by the small rule of `casual-loyalty.ts` (ultimate, then a minus with a hostile target keeping 2, then the highest plus, then zero), never a `[-X]`; they send safe attackers at an opposing planeswalker they can kill; the random agent plays any legal option. (2) Not built, listed in the ROADMAP: planeswalker back faces of double-faced cards (the three flip walkers), Compleated, trample over planeswalkers (CR 702.19c), Myriad and enter-attacking choosing a planeswalker (CR 508.4), loyalty abilities granted to other permanents, an emblem's ability that changes the timing of loyalty abilities (Teferi, Temporal Archmage), "loses the game" as an effect, an untap that chooses ("untap up to two lands"). (3) The rulings of the cards could not be read (Scryfall is not reachable from the sandbox): the points the CR text leaves to rulings (the X announced for a `[-X]` with fewer counters than X, a loyalty ability copied or granted, a loyalty ability that reads the loyalty of a walker that has left) were decided by the CR text only and are tested as such.

### Status of the cards at parser version 52

Garruk Wildspeaker, Jace Beleren, Elspeth, Sun's Champion, Gideon, Ally of Zendikar, Vraska, Relic Seeker, Protege's Awakening and Keeper of the Quiet Hour read completely and pass `validateCard`, and `convert-grammar-engine-p47.test.ts` plays each of their abilities in the engine from the real text. The golden corpus: acceptance 80 of 138 supported (73 before), training 162 of 192 and hold-out 52 of 61 unchanged. The other real planeswalkers of the corpus are unsupported, each at the line named in D-202.

## The Vraska deck (whole-deck program, branch `m66-vraska-deck`; D-204 to the last D- of the program; `docs/proposals/vraska-deck-analysis.md` has the per-card build status)

One section for the whole program, a block for each group. The real texts are in the golden corpus; the cards are played from them in `packages/cards/test/convert-grammar-engine-p48.test.ts` and `convert-grammar-engine-p49.test.ts` (and the ones after).

### Group 2 (D-206; `ENGINE_VERSION` 50, `PARSER_VERSION` 54)

- CR: 702.182a (Job select), 611.3a and 613.8 (a static ability's condition that no layer changes), 509.1c (requirements on blockers), 603.2c and 603.10a (a batch of deaths; look-back), 701.9a (discard), 701.26a and 502.4 (untap; what triggers in the untap step), 400.7 (a card that leaves a graveyard), 601.2h and 603.4 (mana spent; the intervening "if" read again), 601.2a (cast from a zone), 115.1 and 601.2c (what a spell targets), 701.3 (attach).
- AST: keyword `jobSelect`; conditions `yourTurn`, `cardLeftYourGraveyard`, `manaSpentAtLeast { count }`, `castFromZone { zone }`; triggers `becomesUntapped { what }`, `discarded { who }`, `diesBatch { what }`; restriction `mustBeBlocked`; `ObjectFilter.targetsObject`; the event `discarded` has `to`; `TurnLog.leftGraveyard`; `StackData.castFrom`; `DeclareBlockersDecision.attackers[].mustBeBlocked`; `attach` is a `may` effect `didPrevious` can read.
- Rules: `rules/combat-legality.ts` (`unmetBlockRequirements`), `rules/triggers.ts` (the three triggers, the batch dedupe), `rules/flow.ts` (the untap step notes its triggers), `rules/conditions.ts` and `rules/static-conditions.ts`, `rules/select.ts` (`targetsObject`), `rules/derive-keywords.ts` (Job select), `rules/trigger-subject.ts`.
- Agents: `withRequiredBlocks` in `packages/ai/src/legal.ts`, applied by `withinBlockBudget`, which every agent's declaration goes through.
- Web: wording of the three triggers, `yourTurn`, `mustBeBlocked` and the Job select reminder (`trigger-text.ts`, `condition-text.ts`, `static-text.ts`, `artifacts-text.ts`, `ui/board/keywords.ts`).
- Cards unlocked: Dragoon's Lance, White Mage's Staff, Magitek Scythe, Gau, Feral Youth, G'raha Tia, Sahagin, Inkshape Demonstrator, Key to the City, Retrieve the Esper.
- Limits: the "must be blocked" search tries every assignment (a handful of attackers and blockers); a creature that must block (CR 509.1c's other requirement) is not built. A granted ability given to an attached permanent is read for a triggered ability only.

### Group 3 (D-207; `ENGINE_VERSION` 51, `PARSER_VERSION` 55)

- CR: 701.9 and 118.8 (discard as a cost), 201.2 (names), 601.2c and 601.2f (targets, then the total cost), 303.4f and 303.4g (an Aura that enters without being cast), 113.3c, 603.3 and 701.6 (countering a triggered ability), 611.2b (a duration bound to a permanent), 122.1 and 122.2 (removing counters), 607.2a and 406.3 (linked exile).

- Limits: see D-221 to D-225.

## The Hofri Ghostforge deck, group 1 (whole-deck program, branch `m72-hofri`; D-229; `ENGINE_VERSION` 67, `PARSER_VERSION` 69)

- CR: 400.7, 603.2c (batch triggers of cards leaving a graveyard or going to exile), 601.2i (the nth spell of a turn), 903.8 (commander casts), 603.4 (an intervening "if" about the controller of the permanent), 502.3 (the untap step), 613.1d and 205.1a (a permanent that is not a creature), 702.16 (protection from a color), 605.1a and 106.4 (mana another player adds), 118.3 and 701.17 ("mill a card" as a cost), 113.6b (an ability that works from the hand), 506.4 and 509.1g (attacking or blocking).
- AST: triggers `leftGraveyard { who: 'you' }`, `exiledFromLibraryOrGraveyard { who: 'you' }` and `castSpell.nth`; amounts `commanderCasts { of?: 'triggerSubject' }` (also a `StaticPtAmount`); `PlayerStat` `lands`; conditions `subjectControllerHasMore { stat }` and `cardsInGraveyard { compare }`; statics `untapInOthersUntapStep { what }` and `loseTypes { subject, types }`; the keyword `protection { from: 'color', colors }` (it was in the AST and not run); effects `chooseOne.chooser` and `addMana.player`; `ManaAbility.spentRider` `spells.commander` and `grants: { scryCommanderCasts: true }`; the costs `mill`, `exileThis` (from the battlefield too) and `discardThis` (with `functionsIn: 'hand'`) of an ordinary activated ability; `ObjectFilter.attackingOrBlocking`; `grantKeyword.duration` `forAsLongAsYouControlThis`.
- Rules: `rules/triggers.ts` (the occurrences of the two graveyard triggers, `nth`, the player of an enters event), `rules/protection.ts` (`hasProtectionFrom`, used by `canBeTargetedBy`, `dealDamageAsking`, `canAttach`, `canBlockAttacker`), `rules/untap-others.ts`, `rules/derive-group-types.ts` and `rules/derive-layers.ts` (`loseTypes`), `rules/conditions.ts` and `rules/condition-reads.ts`, `rules/activate-costs.ts` (mill, exile this), `rules/choose-one.ts` (the chooser), `rules/cast.ts` (the commander rider, `nth`).
- Parser (stage P68): `trigger.cards-leave-graveyard`, `trigger.cards-exiled-library-graveyard`, `trigger.cast-nth`, `trigger.enters-one-or-more-creatures`, `trigger.you-create-tokens`, `condition.cards-in-graveyard` ("fewer than N"; "N or more" stays `condition.amount-count`), `condition.that-player-more-lands`, `cost.discard-this`, `cost.exile-this`, `effect.player-adds-mana`, `effect.mana-rider-scry-commander`, `effect.gain-keyword-while-controlling`, `amount.for-each-commander-cast`, `static.untap-in-others-step`, the unit "~ isn't a creature" of `static.conditional-prefix`, and `keyword.line` reads "protection from [color]" (one or more colors).
- Web: wording in `trigger-text.ts`, `condition-text.ts`, `amount-text.ts`, `dragons-text.ts` (the rider), `filter-text.ts`, `play-rule-text.ts`. Tests: `packages/cards/test/convert-grammar-engine-p68.test.ts`, `hofri-deck.test.ts`.
- Limits: damage prevented by protection is applied before the other prevention effects; protection from a quality that is not a color is not read; "target player adds one mana of any color" is a stack ability with a choice of five fixed colors.

## Turbulent Steppe: "enters tapped unless your opponents control N or more lands" (branch `m73-turbulent-steppe`; D-236; `ENGINE_VERSION` 73, `PARSER_VERSION` 76)

- CR: 614.12, 614.1c, 109.4, 102.2, 110.2, 305.6.
- AST: no new node. The condition is `amountIs { amount: countOf { filter: { types: ['land'], controller: 'opponent' } }, compare: { cmp: 'atLeast', value: N } }` inside `entersTapped.unless`; the count is of every opponent's lands together, read through `controllerOf`.
- Parser (stage P76): `condition.opponents-control-at-least` (`rules/condition/hofri.ts`), a plural phrase with no controller and no "other". Not `condition.opponent-controls-at-least` ("an opponent controls", one opponent, D-135).
- Tests: `packages/engine/test/scenario-xx-enters-tapped-unless.test.ts` (the engine with a hand-written card), `packages/cards/test/convert-grammar-engine-p73.test.ts` (Turbulent Steppe from the text the owner supplied, not a Scryfall extraction).
- Limits: the card is not in the golden corpus until the owner's `pnpm corpus:extract`; with more than two players the lands of all the opponents are counted together.
